His face was turned in the opposite direction when we came up with him. He was floating peacefully on his back, and it was plain that he had not observed our approach. For when, treading water easily in his rear, I wished him good morning in my most conciliatory tone, he stood not upon the order of his sinking, but went under like so much pig-iron.

===Chinese===
事后复盘，我看得出，在争取德里克教授这场战役里，我的指挥有一两处昏招。首先是时间地点选得糟糕。当时我却不这么想。我心想，两个人在旱地上相遇，一个把另一个傲然视若无睹地走过去，原是轻而易举的事；可要是后者——别忘了，他泳技平平——在水里、在脚够不着底的深处被人搭讪，这份功夫可就难了。依我看，教授在水里，比在岸上好说话。

第二个错——这一条几乎立刻就得到了证实——是把厄克里奇带了去。其实谈不上我带他，倒不如说是甩他不掉。第二天早晨七点三刻，他在屋外碎石道上同我碰头，身上一件晦暗的雨衣，敞开一晃，露出里面一条紫色泳裤。我承认，我的心往下一沉。不幸的是，我劝他别陪同前往的一切努力，都被他归因于一种情有可原的紧张——用他的话说，怯场了。

“振作点，小老弟！”他打气似的吼道，“这我早料到了。冥冥中似有什么告诉我，事到临头你准会泄气。有你老马这样的汉子在身边，你运气真是好极了。哼，你要是孤身一人，半句整话也说不出口，只会干瞪着人家，嗷嗷干嚎。可有我跟着你呢，小老弟，我跟着你呢。你的话头一断，只管指望我给它续上。”

于是事情便成了这样：到了科布防波堤，远远望见教授那颗灰白的脑袋在水面起伏，我们纵身入水，朝他疾游过去。

我们赶上他时，他的脸正朝向另一边。他仰面朝天，安详地漂浮着，显然没察觉我们逼近。因为，当我在他身后从容踩着水，用我最息事宁人的声调向他道早安时，他顾不上讲究沉没的礼数，径直像块生铁锭似的沉了下去。

===Original===
I waited courteously until he rose to the surface again, when I repeated my remark.

He expelled the last remnant of water from his mouth with a wrathful splutter, and cleared his eyes with the back of his hand. I confess to a slight feeling of apprehension as I met his gaze. Nor was my uneasiness diminished by the spectacle of Ukridge splashing tactfully in the background like a large seal. Ukridge so far had made no remarks. He had dived in very flat, and I imagine that his breath had not yet returned to him. He had the air of one who intends to get used to his surroundings before trusting himself to speech.

“The water is delightfully warm,” I said.

“Oh, it’s you!” said the professor; and I could not cheat myself into the belief that he spoke cordially. Ukridge snorted loudly in the offing. The professor turned sharply, as if anxious to observe this marine phenomenon; and the annoyed gurgle which he gave showed that he was not approving of Ukridge either. I did not approve of Ukridge myself. I wished he had not come. Ukridge, in the water, lacks dignity. I felt that he prejudiced my case.

“You are swimming splendidly this morning,” I went on perseveringly, feeling that an ounce of flattery is worth a pound of rhetoric. “If,” I added, “you will allow me to say so.”

===Chinese===
我彬彬有礼地等他再浮上水面，然后把话重说了一遍。

他怒气冲冲地喷溅着，吐出口中最后一点残水，用手背抹了抹眼睛。迎上他的目光时，我承认心里微微发怵。厄克里奇在后方像头大海豹似的识趣地扑腾，这一景象也没能减轻我的不安。厄克里奇至今一言未发。他入水时姿势拍得极平，我想他那口气还没缓回来。他那副神气，是打算先适应了环境，再放心开口的人。

“这水温宜人极了。”我说。

“哦，是你！”教授说；他这口气热络不热络，我骗不了自己。厄克里奇在近海处大声喷了个响鼻。教授猛地转身，仿佛急于一睹这海洋奇观；他发出的那声恼怒的咕噜表明，他对厄克里奇也无好感。我自己对厄克里奇也无好感。我巴不得他没来。厄克里奇一入水，便有失体统。我觉得他坏了我的事。

“您今早游得真是漂亮。”我锲而不舍地续道，深谙一钱奉承抵得上一磅雄辩的道理。“倘若，”我又补一句，“您容我直言的话。”

===Original===
“I will not!” he snapped. “I﻿—” here a small wave, noticing that his mouth was open, stepped in. “I wish,” he resumed warmly, “as I said in me letter, to have nothing to do with you. I consider that ye’ve behaved in a manner that can only be described as abominable, and I will thank you to leave me alone.”

“But allow me﻿—”

“I will not allow ye, sir. I will allow ye nothing. Is it not enough to make me the laughingstock, the butt, sir, of this town, without pursuing me in this way when I wish to enjoy a quiet swim?”

“Now, laddie, laddie,” said Ukridge, placing a large hand on his shoulder, “these are harsh words! Be reasonable! Think before you speak. You little know﻿ ﻿…”

“Go to the devil!” said the professor. “I wish to have nothing to do with either of you. I should be glad if you would cease this persecution. Persecution, sir!”

===Chinese===
“不容！”他厉声道。“俺——”话音未落，一个小浪见他的嘴张着，便进去了。“俺希望，”他热烈地续道，“正像俺信里说的，同您一刀两断。俺认为您的行径，只能用恶劣透顶来形容，请您行行好，离俺远点。”

“可是请容我——”

“俺不会容您，先生。俺什么都容不得您。俺已经成了全镇的笑柄、笑料，先生，这还不够吗？俺只想安安静静游个泳，您还要这般追赶？”

“行了，老弟，老弟，”厄克里奇把一只大手搭上他的肩膀，“这话可就重了！讲点道理！想好了再开口。您哪里知道……”

“见鬼去吧！”教授说，“俺跟你们俩哪一个都两不相干。你们若肯停止这场迫害，俺感激不尽。迫害，先生！”

===Original===
His remarks, which I have placed on paper as if they were continuous and uninterrupted, were punctuated in reality by a series of gasps and puffings, as he received and ejected the successors of the wave he had swallowed at the beginning of our little chat. The art of conducting conversation while in the water is not given to every swimmer. This he seemed to realise, for, as if to close the interview, he proceeded to make his way as quickly as he could to the shore. Unfortunately, his first dash brought him squarely up against Ukridge, who, not having expected the collision, clutched wildly at him and took him below the surface again. They came up a moment later on the worst terms.

“Are you trying to drown me, sir?” barked the professor.

“My dear old horse,” said Ukridge complainingly, “it’s a little hard. You might look where you’re going.”

“You grappled with me!”

“You took me by surprise, laddie. Rid yourself of the impression that you’re playing water-polo.”

===Chinese===
他这些话，我在纸上写得仿佛一气呵成、绝无中断，实际上却让一阵阵喘息与喷气打着标点——他刚把开场那口浪吞下去，又接连收进吐出它的后继者们。在水里同时进行谈话的艺术，并非人人皆能。他似乎也明白这一点，因为像是要结束会晤似的，他开始竭尽全力往岸边游。不幸，他头一个冲刺便正正撞上厄克里奇；后者没提防这场相撞，慌乱中一把死抱住他，又把他按到了水面之下。片刻后两人浮上来时，关系已坏到了极点。

“您是想淹死俺吗，先生？”教授喝道。

“我亲爱的老马，”厄克里奇委屈地说，“这可有点冤。您也该看看自己往哪儿游。”

“是您抱住了俺！”

“您杀了我个措手不及，老弟。请打消您在打水球的错觉。”

===Original===
“But, professor,” I said, joining the group and treading water, “one moment.”

I was growing annoyed with the man. I could have ducked him, but for the reflection that my prospects of obtaining his consent to my engagement would scarcely have been enhanced thereby.

“But, professor,” I said, “one moment.”

“Go away, sir! I have nothing to say to you.”

“But he has lots to say to you,” said Ukridge. “Now’s the time, old horse,” he added encouragingly to me. “Spill the news!”

===Chinese===
“可是，教授，”我加入这堆人，踩着水说，“请稍等片刻。”

我对这个人渐渐光火。我本可以把他按到水里涮一涮，只是转念一想：这样做，恐怕无助于增进我求得他准婚的前景。

“可是，教授，”我说，“稍等片刻。”

“走开，先生！俺跟您无话可说。”

“可他跟您有的是话说，”厄克里奇道，“正是时候，老马，”他又给我打气，“把好消息抖出来！”

===Original===
Without preamble I gave out the text of my address.

“I love your daughter, Phyllis, Mr. Derrick. She loves me. In fact, we are engaged.”

“Devilish well put, laddie,” said Ukridge approvingly.

The professor went under as if he had been seized with cramp. It was a little trying having to argue with a man, of whom one could not predict with certainty that at any given moment he would not be under water. It tended to spoil the flow of one’s eloquence. The best of arguments is useless if the listener suddenly disappears in the middle of it.

“Stick to it, old horse,” said Ukridge. “I think you’re going to bring it off.”

===Chinese===
我开门见山，端出了我演讲的正文。

“我爱您的女儿菲莉斯，德里克先生。她也爱我。实际上，我们已经订了婚。”

“措辞妙极了，小老弟。”厄克里奇赞许道。

教授沉了下去，仿佛突然抽了筋。同人讲理，而这个人你又没把握担保他哪一刻不待在水下，未免有点折磨人。这容易折损雄辩的流畅。再精彩的论证，听众讲到一半突然没影了，也是白搭。

“咬住不放，老马，”厄克里奇说，“我看你要马到成功了。”

===Original===
I stuck to it.

“Mr. Derrick,” I said, as his head emerged, “you are naturally surprised.”

“You would be,” said Ukridge. “We don’t blame you,” he added handsomely.

“You﻿—you﻿—you﻿—” So far from cooling the professor, liberal doses of water seemed to make him more heated. “You impudent scoundrel!”

My reply was more gentlemanly, more courteous, on a higher plane altogether.

===Chinese===
我咬住了不放。

“德里克先生，”等他的头冒出来，我说，“您自然是大吃一惊。”

“换谁都得惊，”厄克里奇说，“我们不怪您。”他宽宏大量地补了一句。

“你——你——你——”大剂量的水非但没给教授降温，反而似乎叫他愈烧愈旺。“你这无法无天的恶棍！”

我的回话则更有君子之风，更加谦恭有礼，格调整体高出一筹。

===Original===
I said, winningly: “Cannot we let bygones be bygones?”

From his remarks I gathered that we could not. I continued. I was under the unfortunate necessity of having to condense my speech. I was not able to let myself go as I could have wished, for time was an important consideration. Ere long, swallowing water at his present rate, the professor must inevitably become waterlogged.

“I have loved your daughter,” I said rapidly, “ever since I first saw her﻿ ﻿…”

“And he’s a capital chap,” interjected Ukridge. “One of the best. Known him for years. You’ll like him.”

“I learned last night that she loved me. But she will not marry me without your consent. Stretch your arms out straight from the shoulders and fill your lungs well and you can’t sink. So I have come this morning to ask for your consent.”

===Chinese===
我动听地说：“过去的，咱们就不能让它过去吗？”

从他的话里我听出：不能。我继续讲。我不幸不得不压缩讲稿。没法像指望的那样纵情挥洒，因为时间是重要考量。照他眼下这个灌水速率，要不了多久，教授就不可避免要泡胀沉底了。

“我自初见令嫒，”我语速飞快地说，“便一往情深……”

“而且他是条顶好的汉子，”厄克里奇插话道，“拔尖的那种。我认识他多年了。您会喜欢他的。”

“我昨夜得知她也爱我。但没有您的应允，她不肯嫁我。把双臂从肩头平直伸出，吸满两肺，您就沉不下去。所以我今早前来，求您应允。”

===Original===
“Give it!” advised Ukridge. “Couldn’t do better. A very sound fellow. Pots of money, too. At least he will have when he marries.”

“I know we have not been on the best of terms lately. For Heaven’s sake don’t try to talk, or you’ll sink. The fault,” I said, generously, “was mine﻿ ﻿…”

“Well put,” said Ukridge.

“But when you have heard my explanation, I am sure you will forgive me. There, I told you so.”

He reappeared some few feet to the left. I swam up, and resumed.

===Chinese===
“答应了吧！”厄克里奇进言道，“再好不过了。一个非常靠谱的汉子。钱也多得是。至少他一结婚就有了。”

“我知道近来我们处得不睦。千万老天在上，别开口说话，不然要沉的。错处，”我宽宏地说，“在我……”

“说得好。”厄克里奇说。

“可等您听完我的解释，我确信您会原谅我。瞧，我说什么来着。”

他在左边几英尺外重新露头。我游过去，接着讲。

===Original===
“When you left us so abruptly after our little dinner-party﻿—”

“Come again some night,” said Ukridge cordially. “Any time you’re passing.”

“… you put me in a very awkward position. I was desperately in love with your daughter, and as long as you were in the frame of mind in which you left I could not hope to find an opportunity of revealing my feelings to her.”

“Revealing feelings is good,” said Ukridge approvingly. “Neat.”

“You see what a fix I was in, don’t you? Keep your arms well out. I thought for hours and hours, to try and find some means of bringing about a reconciliation. You wouldn’t believe how hard I thought.”

===Chinese===
“那日小宴之后，您拂袖而去——”

“改天夜里请再来，”厄克里奇热忱地说，“路过就好，随时光临。”

“……您可把我置于十分难堪的境地了。我爱令嫒爱得神魂颠倒，而只要您还抱着拂袖而去时的心绪，我就休想寻到机会向她吐露心迹。”

“吐露心迹，用得好，”厄克里奇赞许道，“干净利落。”

“您明白我处境多尴尬，对不对？两臂尽管摊开。我左思右想了许多个钟头，想找出一条重修旧好的路子。我费的那番脑子，您都不会信。”

===Original===
“Got as thin as a corkscrew,” said Ukridge.

“At last, seeing you fishing one morning when I was on the Cob, it struck me all of a sudden﻿ ﻿…”

“You know how it is,” said Ukridge.

“… all of a sudden that the very best way would be to arrange a little boating accident. I was confident that I could rescue you all right.”

Here I paused, and he seized the opportunity to curse me﻿—briefly, with a wary eye on an incoming wavelet.

===Chinese===
“人都想瘦成了一根开瓶钻。”厄克里奇说。

“末了，一天早晨我在科布防波堤上，望见您在垂钓，忽然灵光一闪……”

“那种滋味您是懂的。”厄克里奇说。

“……灵光一闪：最好的法子，莫过于安排一场小小的翻船事故。我满有把握，准能把您救起来。”

讲到这里我停了停，他抓住机会咒骂了我一顿——简短有力，还提防地瞟着一记涌来的小浪。

===Original===
“If it hadn’t been for the inscrutable workings of Providence, which has a mania for upsetting everything, all would have been well. In fact, all was well till you found out.”

“Always the way,” said Ukridge sadly. “Always the way.”

“You young blackguard!”

He managed to slip past me, and made for the shore.

“Look at the thing from the standpoint of a philosopher, old horse,” urged Ukridge, splashing after him. “The fact that the rescue was arranged oughtn’t to matter. I mean to say, you didn’t know it at the time, so, relatively, it was not, and you were genuinely saved from a watery grave and all that sort of thing.”

===Chinese===
“若不是上天的安排深不可测——它有个癖好，专爱把一切都掀翻——本来万事大吉。实际上，在您察觉之前，一切本已大吉。”

“向来如此，”厄克里奇怃然道，“向来如此。”

“你这小恶棍！”

他从我身边挣脱滑过，直奔岸去。

“从哲学家的角度看看这事，老马，”厄克里奇一路泼着水追劝，“救援是预先安排的，这一节无关宏旨。我是说，您当时并不知道，所以，相对而言，它就不算数；您可是货真价实地被人从水坟里救了出来，如此等等。”

===Original===
I had not imagined Ukridge capable of such an excursion into metaphysics. I saw the truth of his line of argument so clearly that it seemed to me impossible for anyone else to get confused over it. I had certainly pulled the professor out of the water, and the fact that I had first caused him to be pushed in had nothing to do with the case. Either a man is a gallant rescuer or he is not a gallant rescuer. There is no middle course. I had saved his life﻿—for he would certainly have drowned if left to himself﻿—and I was entitled to his gratitude. That was all there was to be said about it.

These things both Ukridge and I tried to make plain as we swam along. But whether it was that the salt water he had swallowed had dulled the professor’s normally keen intelligence or that our power of stating a case was too weak, the fact remains that he reached the beach an unconvinced man.

“Then may I consider,” I said, “that your objections are removed? I have your consent?”

He stamped angrily, and his bare foot came down on a small, sharp pebble. With a brief exclamation he seized his foot in one hand and hopped up the beach. While hopping, he delivered his ultimatum. Probably the only instance on record of a father adopting this attitude in dismissing a suitor.

“You may not!” he cried. “You may consider no such thing. My objections were never more absolute. You detain me in the water, sir, till I am blue, sir, blue with cold, in order to listen to the most preposterous and impudent nonsense I ever heard.”

===Chinese===
我没想到厄克里奇还能做这般形而上学的远足。他那条论证的真理，我看得如此分明，以致我觉得任何人都不可能在此上面犯迷糊。我确确实实把教授从水里拉了出来；至于我先安排人把他推下去，这一事实与本案无关。一个人要么是英勇的救命恩人，要么不是，没有中间路线。我救了他的命——因为把他撂在那里的话，他准得淹死——我理应受他一份感激。这桩事能讲的，全在于此。

这些道理，厄克里奇和我一边游，一边竭力阐明白。可是，不知是他吞下的海水钝化了教授平日敏锐的智力，还是我们陈述案情的功力太弱，事实是：他踏上沙滩时，依然是个未被说服的人。

“那么可否认为，”我说，“您的反对已经撤销？您应允了？”

他怒冲冲一跺脚，光脚板正踩上一颗小而尖的卵石。他短短惊呼一声，一手攥住脚，单腿蹦上了海滩。蹦跳之间，他发布了最后通牒。做父亲的用这般姿势打发求婚者，有案可稽的，恐怕仅此一例。

“您不可以！”他嚷道，“您什么都不可以认为。俺的反对从未这般斩钉截铁。您把俺扣在水里，先生，扣得俺都发青了，先生，冻得发青，就为听俺此生所闻最荒唐、最放肆的浑话。”

===Original===
This was unjust. If he had listened attentively from the first and avoided interruptions and had not behaved like a submarine we should have got through the business in half the time.

I said so.

“Don’t talk to me, sir,” he replied, hobbling off to his dressing-tent. “I will not listen to you. I will have nothing to do with you. I consider you impudent, sir.”

“I assure you it was unintentional.”

“Isch!” he said﻿—being the first occasion and the last on which I have ever heard that remarkable monosyllable proceed from the mouth of man. And he vanished into his tent.

===Chinese===
这话不公道。他若从头专心听讲，不来打岔，也不像艘潜水艇那样忽沉忽浮，这桩事只需一半时间便可办完。

我如此说了。

“别跟俺说话，先生，”他答道，一瘸一拐走向更衣帐篷，“俺不听您的。俺跟您两不相干。俺认为您放肆无礼，先生。”

“我向您担保，那绝非有意。”

“咄！”他说——这一声奇绝的单音节从人类口中吐出，是我此生所闻的头一遭，也是末一遭。说罢他便隐入帐篷，踪影全无。

===Original===
“Laddie,” said Ukridge solemnly, “do you know what I think?”

“Well?”

“You haven’t clicked, old horse!” said Ukridge.

===Chinese===
“小老弟，”厄克里奇郑重地说，“你可知道我在想什么？”

“想什么？”

“你没戏了，老马！”厄克里奇说。

## V Buckling To / 五 撸袖开工

===Original===
Sunshine, streaming into my bedroom through the open window, woke me next day as distant clocks were striking eight. It was a lovely morning, cool and fresh. The grass of the lawn, wet with dew, sparkled in the sun. A thrush, who knew all about early birds and their perquisites, was filling in the time before the arrival of the worm with a song or two, as he sat in the bushes. In the ivy a colony of sparrows were opening the day with brisk scuffling. On the gravel in front of the house lay the mongrel, Bob, blinking lazily.

The gleam of the sea through the trees turned my thoughts to bathing. I dressed quickly and went out. Bob rose to meet me, waving an absurdly long tail. The hatchet was definitely buried now. That little matter of the jug of water was forgotten.

A walk of five minutes down the hill brought me, accompanied by Bob, to the sleepy little town. I passed through the narrow street, and turned on to the beach, walking in the direction of the combination of pier and breakwater which loomed up through the faint mist.

The tide was high, and, leaving my clothes to the care of Bob, who treated them as a handy bed, I dived into twelve feet of clear, cold water. As I swam, I compared it with the morning tub of London, and felt that I had done well to come with Ukridge to this pleasant spot. Not that I could rely on unbroken calm during the whole of my visit. I knew nothing of chicken-farming, but I was certain that Ukridge knew less. There would be some strenuous moments before that farm became a profitable commercial speculation. At the thought of Ukridge toiling on a hot afternoon to manage an undisciplined mob of fowls, I laughed, and swallowed a generous mouthful of salt water; and, turning, swam back to Bob and my clothes.

On my return, I found Ukridge, in his shirt sleeves and minus a collar, assailing a large ham. Mrs. Ukridge, looking younger and more childlike than ever in brown holland, smiled at me over the teapot.

“Hullo, old horse,” bellowed Ukridge, “where have you been? Bathing? Hope it’s made you feel fit for work, because we’ve got to buckle to this morning.”

===Chinese===
第二天，阳光从敞开的窗口涌进我的卧室，将我唤醒，远处的钟正敲八点。真是个可爱的早晨，凉爽而清新。草坪上的青草沾满露珠，在太阳底下熠熠闪光。一只画眉——对“早起的鸟儿”及其特权素有研究——蹲在灌木丛里，趁虫子还没到岗，先唱上一两支歌消磨时间。常春藤里，一窝麻雀正以轻快的推搡开启新的一天。屋前碎石地上，卧着那条杂种狗博布，懒洋洋地眨着眼。

树影间海水的闪光，把我的心思引向了海浴。我匆匆穿好衣服出了门。博布起身迎我，摇着一条长得荒唐的尾巴。战斧如今确确实实埋掉了。那桩水壶的小小过节，也已抛诸脑后。

博布陪伴着我，下山走了五分钟，便到了这座睡意沉沉的小镇。我穿过窄街，转上沙滩，朝着薄雾中隐约浮现的那座栈桥兼防波堤走去。

正值涨潮。我把衣服托付给博布照管——它把它们当作一张现成的床——自己一头扎进十二英尺清澈冰凉的水里。一路游着，我拿它同伦敦的晨间冷水浴一比，觉得跟厄克里奇来这宜人的地方，实在来得其所。倒不是指望此行全程太平无事。养鸡我一窍不通，可我敢肯定厄克里奇懂得更少。不等那农场变成一桩赚钱的买卖，少不得有几番苦战的时刻。想到厄克里奇在大热天的下午辛苦劳碌、管教一群散漫鸡众的光景，我笑了，随即吞下一大口海水；便转过身，游回博布和我的衣服那里。

回到住处，我看见厄克里奇只穿衬衫、没戴领子，正向一只大火腿发起强攻。厄克里奇太太一身棕色亚麻布裙，显得比往常更年轻、更孩子气，正隔着茶壶朝我微笑。

“喂，老马，”厄克里奇大吼道，“你上哪儿去了？洗海澡？但愿它让你干起活来浑身是劲，因为今天上午咱们可得撸袖子大干了。”

===Original===
“The fowls have arrived, Mr. Garnet,” said Mrs. Ukridge, opening her eyes till she looked like an astonished kitten. “*Such* a lot of them. They’re making such a noise.”

To support her statement there floated in through the window a cackling which for volume and variety beat anything I had ever heard. Judging from the noise, it seemed as if England had been drained of fowls and the entire tribe of them dumped into the yard of Ukridge’s farm.

“There seems to have been no stint,” I said.

“Quite a goodish few, aren’t there?” said Ukridge complacently. “But that’s what we want. No good starting on a small scale. The more you have, the bigger the profits.”

“What sorts have you got mostly?” I asked, showing a professional interest.

“Oh, all sorts. My theory, laddie, is this. It doesn’t matter a bit what kind we get, because they’ll all lay; and if we sell settings of eggs, which we will, we’ll merely say it’s an unfortunate accident if they turn out mixed when hatched. Bless you, people don’t mind what breed a fowl is, so long as it’s got two legs and a beak. These dealer chaps were so infernally particular. ‘Any Dorkings?’ they said. ‘All right,’ I said, ‘bring on your Dorkings.’ ‘Or perhaps you will require a few Minorcas?’ ‘Very well,’ I said, ‘unleash the Minorcas.’ They were going on﻿—they’d have gone on for hours﻿—but I stopped ’em. ‘Look here, my dear old college chum,’ I said kindly but firmly to the manager johnny﻿—decent old buck, with the manners of a marquess﻿—‘look here,’ I said, ‘life is short, and we’re neither of us as young as we used to be. Don’t let us waste the golden hours playing guessing games. I want fowls. You sell fowls. So give me some of all sorts. Mix ’em up, laddie,’ I said, ‘mix ’em up.’ And he has, by Jove. You go into the yard and look at ’em. Beale has turned them out of their crates. There must be one of every breed ever invented.”

===Chinese===
“鸡到货了，加尼特先生。”厄克里奇太太说着，把眼睛睁得活像一只惊愕的小猫。“来了*这么*一大群。吵得可厉害啦。”

为给她的说法作证，一阵咯咯哒哒的鸡鸣从窗口飘了进来，论音量、论花样，都盖过我此生所闻的一切。听这动静，仿佛整个英格兰的鸡都被淘换一空，全族都被倾倒进了厄克里奇农场的院子。

“看来是不曾吝惜。”我说。

“数目委实不少，对不对？”厄克里奇扬扬自得地说。“可这正是咱们要的。小家子气地起家，没半点好处。鸡越多，利越大。”

“主要进的些什么品种？”我问，露出一副行家的兴趣。

“哦，各色各样。我的理论是这样的，小老弟：进什么品种全不相干，反正它们都要下蛋；咱们将来卖的是种蛋——这是一定的——孵出来若是五花八门，只消说一声‘不巧出了点岔子’便是。老天保佑，人们才不管一只鸡是什么品种呢，只要它长着两条腿一张嘴。那几个贩子老兄偏要打破砂锅：‘有多尔金鸡吗？’‘好啊，’我说，‘把您的多尔金鸡都牵来吧。’‘要不您兴许还要几只米诺卡？’‘很好，’我说，‘把米诺卡也放出来。’他们还待要讲下去——准能一讲几个钟头——叫我打住了。‘听我说，我亲爱的老同学，’我客客气气又斩钉截铁地对那位经理老兄说——是个体面的老好人，做派赛过侯爵——‘听我说，人生苦短，咱们俩都不复当年之少。别把金子般的时光糟蹋在猜谜上。我要鸡。你卖鸡。那么，每样都给我来一些。把它们掺和掺和，小老弟，’我说，‘掺和掺和。’老天在上，他果真掺和了。你到院子里瞧瞧去。比尔已经把它们从板条箱里放出来了。自古发明的每一种鸡，保准这儿都有一只。”

===Original===
“Where are you going to put them?”

“That spot we chose by the paddock. That’s the place. Plenty of mud for them to scratch about in, and they can go into the field when they feel like it, and pick up worms, or whatever they feed on. We must rig them up some sort of shanty, I suppose, this morning. We’ll go and tell ’em to send up some wire-netting and stuff from the town.”

“Then we shall want hen-coops. We shall have to make those.”

“Of course. So we shall. Millie, didn’t I tell you that old Garnet was the man to think of things. I forgot the coops. We can’t buy some, I suppose? On tick, of course.”

“Cheaper to make them. Suppose we get a lot of boxes. Sugar boxes are as good as any. It won’t take long to knock up a few coops.”

Ukridge thumped the table with enthusiasm, upsetting his cup.

===Chinese===
“你打算把它们安置在哪儿？”

“就咱们在围场旁边选的那块地。就是那儿。泥巴管够，由它们刨个痛快；它们乐意时可以进田里，啄啄虫子什么的——反正它们吃的那路东西。我想今天上午总得给它们草草搭个棚子。咱们这就去镇上关照一声，让他们送些铁丝网之类的上来。”

“那咱们还需要鸡笼。这些只怕得自己动手做。”

“可不是。正是。米莉，我不是跟你说过吗，老加尼就是那个想得周全的人。鸡笼的事，我就忘了。咱们就不能买现成的吗？赊账便是。”

“自己做便宜。咱们去弄一批箱子来。方糖箱就再好不过。敲敲打打弄几个笼子，费不了多少工夫。”

厄克里奇热烈地一拳捶在桌上，把茶杯都震翻了。

===Original===
“Garny, old horse, you’re a marvel. You think of everything. We’ll buckle to right away, and get the whole place fixed up the same as mother makes it. What an infernal noise those birds are making. I suppose they don’t feel at home in the yard. Wait till they see the A1 compact residential mansions we’re going to put up for them. Finished breakfast? Then let’s go out. Come along, Millie.”

The redheaded Beale, discovered leaning in an attitude of thought on the yard gate and observing the feathered mob below with much interest, was roused from his reflections and despatched to the town for the wire and sugar boxes. Ukridge, taking his place at the gate, gazed at the fowls with the affectionate air of a proprietor.

“Well, they have certainly taken you at your word,” I said, “as far as variety is concerned.”

The man with the manners of a marquess seemed to have been at great pains to send a really representative selection of fowls. There were blue ones, black ones, white, grey, yellow, brown, big, little, Dorkings, Minorcas, Cochin Chinas, Bantams, Wyandottes. It was an imposing spectacle.

The Hired Man returned towards the end of the morning, preceded by a cart containing the necessary wire and boxes; and Ukridge, whose enthusiasm brooked no delay, started immediately the task of fashioning the coops, while I, assisted by Beale, draped the wire-netting about the chosen spot next to the paddock. There were little unpleasantnesses﻿—once a roar of anguish told that Ukridge’s hammer had found the wrong billet, and on another occasion my flannel trousers suffered on the wire﻿—but the work proceeded steadily. By the middle of the afternoon, things were in a sufficiently advanced state to suggest to Ukridge the advisability of a halt for refreshments.

“That’s the way to do it,” he said, beaming through misty pince-nez over a long glass. “That is the stuff to administer to ’em! At this rate we shall have the place in corking condition before bedtime. Quiet efficiency﻿—that’s the wheeze! What do you think of those for coops, Beale?”

===Chinese===
“加尼老马，你真是个奇才。什么事都想得到。咱们这就撸袖子开工，把整个地方拾掇得跟娘亲手笔一模一样。这群鸟吵得可真够邪乎。我猜它们在院里住不惯。等它们见了咱们要给它们盖的甲等紧凑宅邸，再瞧吧。吃完了吗？那就走吧。来，米莉。”

红头发的比尔，被人发现正倚在院门上作沉思状，饶有兴味地观察着下方那群带羽毛的暴民。他被从沉思中唤醒，派往镇上去买铁丝网和方糖箱。厄克里奇接替他站到门口，以业主的慈爱目光凝视群鸡。

“嗯，就品种多样而论，他们确确实实没辜负你的吩咐。”我说。

那位做派赛过侯爵的老兄，看来是下足了功夫，送来了一批真正有代表性的精选。有蓝的、黑的、白的、灰的、黄的、棕的，有大的、小的，有多尔金、米诺卡、交趾鸡、矮脚鸡、怀恩多特。好一派壮观的景象。

将近晌午，雇来的老家人回来了，前头由一辆板车开道，车上装着必备的铁丝网和箱子；厄克里奇的热情容不得半点耽搁，当即动手打造鸡笼；我则在比尔协助下，把铁丝网披挂到围场旁那块选定的地盘上。小小的不快也有几桩——一回，一声痛苦的怒吼表明厄克里奇的锤子找错了垫木，另一回，我的法兰绒裤子也在铁丝网上挂了彩——但工程稳步推进。到下半晌，诸事已进展到相当地步，足以提醒厄克里奇：暂停犒赏一番，方为上策。

“就得这么干，”他隔着一副蒙雾的夹鼻眼镜，擎着高脚大杯容光焕发地说，“这就是该喂给他们的东西！照这个速度，睡觉之前咱们就能把这地方拾掇得呱呱叫。闷声干活出效率——这才是诀窍！那些笼子你觉得如何，比尔？”

===Original===
The Hired Man examined them woodenly.

“I’ve seen worse, sir.”

He continued his examination.

“But not many,” he added. Beale’s passion for the truth had made him unpopular in three regiments.

“They aren’t so bad,” I said, “but I’m glad I’m not a fowl.”

===Chinese===
雇来的老家人面无表情地检视起笼子来。

“更糟的俺也见过，先生。”

他继续检视。

“不过不多。”他补了一句。比尔这份对真理的热忱，曾叫他在三个团里都不得人心。

“它们不算太糟，”我说，“可幸亏我不是一只鸡。”

===Original===
“So you ought to be,” said Ukridge, “considering the way you’ve put up that wire. You’ll have them strangling themselves.”

In spite of earnest labour the housing arrangements of the fowls were still in an incomplete state at the end of the day. The details of the evening’s work are preserved in a letter which I wrote that night to my friend Lickford.

> “… Have you ever played a game called Pigs in Clover? We have just finished a merry bout of it, with hens instead of marbles, which has lasted for an hour and a half. We are all dead tired, except the Hired Man, who seems to be made of india-rubber. He has just gone for a stroll on the beach. Wants some exercise, I suppose. Personally, I feel as if I should never move again. You have no conception of the difficulty of rounding up fowls and getting them safely to bed. Having no proper place to put them, we were obliged to stow some of them in the cube sugar-boxes and the rest in the basement. It has only just occurred to me that they ought to have had perches to roost on. It didn’t strike me before. I shan’t mention it to Ukridge, or that indomitable man will start making some, and drag me into it, too. After all, a hen can rough it for one night, and if I did a stroke more work I should collapse.

> “My idea was to do the thing on the slow but sure principle. That is to say, take each bird singly and carry it to bed. It would have taken some time, but there would have been no confusion. But you can imagine that that sort of thing would not appeal to Stanley Featherstonehaugh! He likes his manoeuvres to be on a large, dashing, Napoleonic scale. He said, ‘Open the yard gate and let the blighters come out into the open; then sail in and drive them in mass formation through the back door into the basement.’ It was a great idea, but there was one fatal flaw in it. It didn’t allow for the hens scattering. We opened the gate, and out they all came like an audience coming out of a theatre. Then we closed in on them to bring off the big drive. For about thirty seconds it looked as if we might do it. Then Bob, the Hired Man’s dog, an animal who likes to be in whatever’s going on, rushed out of the house into the middle of them, barking. There was a perfect stampede, and Heaven only knows where some of those fowls are now. There was one in particular, a large yellow bird, which, I should imagine, is nearing London by this time. The last I saw of it, it was navigating at the rate of knots in that direction, with Bob after it, barking his hardest. The fowl was showing a rare turn of speed and gaining rapidly. Presently Bob came back, panting, having evidently given the thing up. We, in the meantime, were chasing the rest of the birds all over the garden. The affair had now resolved itself into the course of action I had suggested originally, except that instead of collecting them quietly and at our leisure, we had to run miles for each one we captured. After a time we introduced some sort of system into it. Mrs. Ukridge stood at the door. We chased the hens and brought them in. Then, as we put each through into the basement, she shut the door on it. We also arranged Ukridge’s sugar-box coops in a row, and when we caught a fowl we put it in the coop and stuck a board in front of it. By these strenuous means we gathered in about two-thirds of the lot. The rest are all over England. A few may be still in Dorsetshire, but I should not like to bet on it.

> “So you see things are being managed on the up-to-date chicken farm on good, sound Ukridge principles. It is only the beginning. I look with confidence for further interesting events. I believe if Ukridge kept white mice he would manage to get feverish excitement out of it. He is at present lying on the sofa, smoking one of his infernal brand of cigars, drinking whisky and soda, and complaining with some bitterness because the whisky isn’t as good as some he once tasted in Belfast. From the basement I can hear faintly the murmur of innumerable fowls.”

===Chinese===
“你也真该庆幸，”厄克里奇说，“瞧你拉那铁丝网的架势。你这是存心让它们集体自缢。”

尽管干得卖力，到天黑收工时，鸡群的住房安排仍处于未竟状态。晚间劳作的种种细节，都保存在我当夜写给朋友利克福德的一封信里。

> “……你玩过一种叫《猪入圈》的游戏吗？我们刚痛痛快快玩了一轮，只是拿母鸡代替了弹珠，前后耗了一个半钟头。除了雇来的老家人，我们都累瘫了——那位老哥仿佛是橡皮做的。他这会儿刚去海滩散步。大概是想锻炼锻炼。至于我，只觉得这辈子再也挪不动窝了。把鸡拢起来、平安送上床，这里面的难处，你毫无概念。没有正经地方安置它们，我们只好把一部分塞进方糖箱，余下的全塞进地窖。直到刚刚我才想到，它们本该有几根栖木才对。先前竟没想到。这话我可不打算对厄克里奇讲，否则那位百折不挠的汉子保准立刻动手做起来，还要把我也拖进去。说到底，一只母鸡挨一晚上粗糙日子不碍事，而我再干半分活儿就要散架了。

> “我的主意，本是按慢而稳的原则来办。也就是说，一只一只地拎，把每只鸟单独送上床。费是费些工夫，但绝无乱子。可你想，这路做派哪能入得了斯坦利·费瑟斯通豪的法眼！他喜欢他的军事行动规模宏大、雷厉风行、一派拿破仑气象。他说：‘打开院门，把这群捣蛋鬼放进开阔地；然后咱们扬帆杀入，把它们成建制地从后门赶进地窖。’主意妙极，只可惜有个致命破绽：它没算到母鸡会四散奔逃。我们开了门，鸡群倾巢而出，活像散场的观众涌出剧院。随即我们收拢包围，要完成这场大驱赶。约莫三十秒工夫，眼看着就要成了。这时博布——雇来的老家人那条狗，一只但凡有热闹必到场的动物——从屋里冲出来，直插鸡群中央，狂吠不止。登时大溃逃，那群鸡有几只如今流落何方，只有天晓得。其中有一只尤其可观，一只黄色大鸡，我料它这会儿已快到伦敦了。我最后一眼见它，它正以全速朝那个方向导航前进，博布在后头拼命吠追。那鸡显出一副罕见的脚力，越跑越远。不多时博布耷拉着舌头回来了，显然已放弃这项事业。与此同时，我们正满花园追捕余下的鸡。事态至此，已归结为我最初提议的那套方案，只是并非从容不迫静静收拢，而是每逮一只得跑上一英里。过了一阵，我们理出些章法。厄克里奇太太把守房门。我们追鸡、递鸡。每送一只进地窖，她便随手关门。我们又把厄克里奇的方糖箱笼子排成一列，逮着一只就塞进一只笼子，再在前面插块木板。靠这些苦办法，我们收拢了大约三分之二。其余的遍布英格兰各地。少数几只或许还在多塞特郡境内，但我可不敢下注。

> “于是你瞧，这座最新式养鸡场里的一切，都照着地道、过硬的厄克里奇原则在运转。这还只是开锣。我满有信心，静候更精彩的事件。我相信厄克里奇哪怕养小白鼠，也能闹出个热火朝天。他此刻正躺在沙发上，抽着他那个邪门牌子的雪茄，喝着威士忌苏打，还愤愤抱怨这威士忌不如他当年在贝尔法斯特喝过的某种。地窖那边，隐隐传来无数只鸡的低吟。”

## Dedication / 献词

===Original===
To W. Townend

Dear Bill﻿—

I have never been much of a lad for the

> To —﻿—﻿— 

 But For Whose Sympathy and Encouragement 

 This Book 

===Chinese===
致 W. 汤恩德

亲爱的比尔——

我从来就不是那种热衷于写

> 致———

 若非此君同情与鼓励 

 此书 

===Original===
 Would Never Have Been Written

type of dedication. It sounds so weak-minded. But in the case of *Love Among the Chickens* it is unavoidable. It was not so much that you sympathised and encouraged﻿—where you really came out strong was that you gave me the stuff. I like people who sympathise with me. I am grateful to those who encourage me. But the man to whom I raise the Wodehouse hat﻿—owing to the increased cost of living, the same old brown one I had last year. It is being complained of on all sides, but the public must bear it like men till the straw hat season comes round﻿—I say, the man to whom I raise this venerable relic is the man who gives me the material.

Sixteen years ago, my William, when we were young and spritely lads; when you were a tricky centre-forward and I a fast bowler; when your head was covered with hair and my list of “Hobbies” in *Who’s Who* included Boxing; I received from you one morning about thirty closely-written foolscap pages, giving me the details of your friend —﻿—﻿—’s adventures on his Devonshire chicken farm. Round these I wove as funny a plot as I could, but the book stands or falls by the stuff you gave me about “Ukridge”﻿—the things that actually happened.

You will notice that I have practically rewritten the book. There was some pretty bad work in it, and it had “dated.” As an instance of the way in which the march of modern civilisation has left the 1906 edition behind, I may mention that on page twenty-one I was able to make Ukridge speak of selling eggs at six for fivepence!

===Chinese===
 断无写成之日

这类献词的人。那腔调听上去未免太没脑子。可是在《养鸡场里的爱情》这件事上，却回避不得。倒不是说你怎么同情了我、鼓励了我——你真正大显身手之处，在于你把素材给了我。我喜欢同情我的人。我感激鼓励我的人。但那位让我为他脱下伍德豪斯帽的人——由于生活费用上涨，仍是我去年那顶棕色旧帽。各界人士都对它啧有烦言，但公众必须像男子汉一般隐忍，直到草帽季节到来——我是说，让我为这顶古老圣物脱帽的人，便是把素材给我的人。

十六年前，我的威廉，那时我们还是年轻雀跃的小伙子；那时你是球风刁钻的中锋，我是出手迅捷的快投手；那时你满头头发，我在《名人录》里的“爱好”一栏还写着拳击；一天早晨，我收到你寄来的约莫三十页写得密密麻麻的大页手稿，向我细述你那位某某朋友在他德文郡养鸡场上的种种奇遇。我围绕这些材料，尽力编出了一个尽量逗笑的故事，但这本书的成败，全系于你给我的关于“厄克里奇”的素材——那些实实在在发生过的事。

你会发现，我其实把这本书差不多重写了一遍。原稿里有些地方写得相当糟糕，而且已经“过时”。要举一个现代文明的进程把 1906 年版远远甩在身后的例子，我只需说：在第二十一页上，我居然能让厄克里奇说什么鸡蛋六个只卖五便士！

===Original===
Yours ever,

P. G. Wodehouse.

London, 1920.

===Chinese===
你永远的，

P. G. 伍德豪斯。

伦敦，1920 年。

## IX Dies Irae / 九 震怒之日

===Original===
Why is it, I wonder, that stories of Retribution calling at the wrong address, strike us as funny instead of pathetic? I myself had been amused by them many a time. In a book which I had read only a few days before our cold-dinner party a shop-woman, annoyed with an omnibus conductor, had thrown a superannuated orange at him. It had found its billet not on him but on a perfectly inoffensive spectator. The missile, said the writer, “ ’it a young copper full in the hyeball.” I had enjoyed this when I read it, but now that Fate had arranged a precisely similar situation, with myself in the role of the young copper, the fun of the thing appealed to me not at all.

It was Ukridge who was to blame for the professor’s regrettable explosion and departure, and he ought by all laws of justice to have suffered for it. As it was, I was the only person materially affected. It did not matter to Ukridge. He did not care twopence one way or the other. If the professor were friendly, he was willing to talk to him by the hour on any subject, pleasant or unpleasant. If, on the other hand, he wished to have nothing more to do with us, it did not worry him. He was content to let him go. Ukridge was a self-sufficing person.

But to me it was a serious matter. More than serious. If I have done my work as historian with an adequate degree of skill, the reader should have gathered by this time the state of my feelings.

> “I did not love as others do:
 

 None ever did that I’ve heard tell of.
 

 My passion was a byword through
 

 The town she was, of course, the belle of.”

At least it was﻿—fortunately﻿—not quite that; but it was certainly genuine and most disturbing, and it grew with the days. Somebody with a taste for juggling with figures might write a very readable page or so of statistics in connection with the growth of love. In some cases it is, I believe, slow. In my own I can only say that Jack’s beanstalk was a backward plant in comparison. It is true that we had not seen a great deal of one another, and that, when we had met, our interview had been brief and our conversation conventional; but it is the intervals between the meeting that do the real damage. Absence﻿—I do not claim the thought as my own﻿—makes the heart grow fonder. And now, thanks to Ukridge’s amazing idiocy, a barrier had been thrust between us. Lord knows, the business of fishing for a girl’s heart is sufficiently difficult and delicate without the addition of needless obstacles. To cut out the naval miscreant under equal conditions would have been a task ample enough for my modest needs. It was terrible to have to reestablish myself in the good graces of the professor before I could so much as begin to dream of Phyllis. Ukridge gave me no balm.

===Chinese===
我常纳闷：报应找错了门的故事，为何总叫我们发笑，而不觉其可悲？我自己就被这类故事逗乐过许多回。我们那顿冷餐宴前几天刚读过的一本书里，有个女店员被公共马车售票员惹恼，朝他掷了一只过了季的陈橙子。橙子没有命中他，倒落在一位全然无辜的看客身上。据那位作者描写，这枚暗器「端端正正砸中小警察的眼珠儿」。读到此处时我曾大乐，可如今命运安排了一出如出一辙的戏码，由我亲自出演那个小警察，其中的妙处我便一点儿也领略不到了。

教授那番令人扼腕的发作与拂袖而去，责任全在厄克里奇，照一切公道的法理，受报应的本该是他。而实际情况是，唯一蒙受实质损失的却是我。于厄克里奇则无关痛痒。好也罢歹也罢，他连两个便士的计较都没有。教授若肯亲善，无论题目愉快与否，他都乐与人家畅谈几个钟头；教授若从此不愿与我们相干，他也毫不在意，乐得由他去。厄克里奇是个自足自洽的人。

可于我，这是件严重的事。不止严重。倘若本人身为史官，技艺还算过得去，读者此刻理应已经揣度出我的心境了。

> 「我爱人爱得与众人不同：
 

 从没听说谁这样爱过。
 

 我这片痴情人人当口头禅，
 

 传遍她——自然是全镇花魁——住的城。」

至少它还算——万幸——没到那个田地；但它千真万确是真的，而且搅得人心神难安，还与日俱长。哪位有玩弄数字之癖的先生，尽可以就爱情的增长写上一两页颇为可读的统计资料。据我所信，有些病例里这增长是缓慢的。至于我这一例，我只能说，杰克的那根豆茎与之相比都要算发育迟缓的植物了。诚然，我们彼此见面不多；即令见了，晤谈短促，言语客套；可真正酿成大患的，正是两次见面之间的空当。分别——这话非我首创——使情人心愈思愈切。而如今，多亏厄克里奇那惊人的白痴行径，一道高墙横亘在我与她之间。天知道，垂钓一颗姑娘的芳心，本已够艰难够精细，无需再添无谓的障碍。便在条件对等之下，要剪除那个海军恶棍，于我谦逊的胃口也已是一桩够浩大的工程；如今竟须先在教授跟前重建圣眷，方配开始梦到菲莉斯——这真是雪上加霜。厄克里奇却无半点膏药给我。

===Original===
“Well, after all,” he said, when I pointed out to him quietly but plainly my opinion of his tactlessness, “what does it matter? Old Derrick isn’t the only person in the world. If he doesn’t want to know us, laddie, we just jolly well pull ourselves together and stagger along without him. It’s quite possible to be happy without knowing old Derrick. Millions of people are going about the world at this moment, singing like larks out of pure lightheartedness, who don’t even know of his existence. And, as a matter of fact, old horse, we haven’t time to waste making friends and being the social pets. Too much to do on the farm. Strict business is the watchword, my boy. We must be the keen, tense men of affairs, or, before we know where we are, we shall find ourselves right in the gumbo.

“I’ve noticed, Garny, old horse, that you haven’t been the whale for work lately that you might be. You must buckle to, laddie. There must be no slackness. We are at a critical stage. On our work now depends the success of the speculation. Look at those damned cocks. They’re always fighting. Heave a stone at them, laddie, while you’re up. What’s the matter with you? You seem pipped. Can’t get the novel off your chest, or what? You take my tip and give your brain a rest. Nothing like manual labour for clearing the brain. All the doctors say so. Those coops ought to be painted today or tomorrow. Mind you, I think old Derrick would be all right if one persevered﻿—”

“﻿—and didn’t call him a fat little buffer and contradict everything he said and spoil all his stories by breaking in with chestnuts of your own in the middle,” I interrupted with bitterness.

“My dear old son, he didn’t mind being called a fat little buffer. You keep harping on that. It’s no discredit to a man to be a fat little buffer. Some of the noblest men I have met have been fat little buffers. What was the matter with old Derrick was a touch of liver. I said to myself, when I saw him eating cheese, ‘that fellow’s going to have a nasty shooting pain sooner or later.’ I say, laddie, just heave another rock or two at those cocks, will you. They’ll slay each other.”

I had hoped, fearing the while that there was not much chance of such a thing happening, that the professor might get over his feeling of injury during the night and be as friendly as ever next day. But he was evidently a man who had no objection whatever to letting the sun go down upon his wrath, for when I met him on the following morning on the beach, he cut me in the most uncompromising manner.

Phyllis was with him at the time, and also another girl, who was, I supposed, from the strong likeness between them, her sister. She had the same mass of soft brown hair. But to me she appeared almost commonplace in comparison.

===Chinese===
等我婉转而明白地向他指出，我对他那番不识进退有何看法，他答道：「唉，究竟呢，有什么要紧？老德里克又不是世上仅有的人物。他既不愿结识咱们，小老弟，咱们就大大方方振作精神，没有他也照样踉跄前进。不认识老德里克而照样快活，完全是可能的。此时此刻，正有几百万人满世界溜达，纯粹出于心头轻快而学百灵鸟歌唱，他们连有他这号人都不知道。况且，实话说，老伙计，咱们也没有工夫虚掷在交朋结友、当社交红人上头。农场要干的事太多。一丝不苟办实事才是口号，小子。咱们必须做雷厉风行、紧绷绷的事务家，不然，还没弄清身在何处，就发现自己齐脖子陷进黏糊糊的泥塘里了。」

「我还注意到，加尼老伙计，你近来干起活来，可不如你可能有的那股鲸鱼劲儿。你得奋发，小老弟。不许有一丝松弛。咱们正处在紧要关头。这场投机事业的成败，如今全看咱们的工夫。瞧那群该死的公鸡，老在打架。你反正起来了，顺手撂块石头轰它们一轰，小老弟。你是怎么啦？瞧着蔫头蔫脑的。小说憋不出来，还是怎么？听我一句，让你的脑子歇一歇。要涤清脑子，什么也比不上体力劳动。医生们都这么说。那批鸡笼今明两天就该上漆了。不过说实在的，我看老德里克那边，只要有恒心，总有办法——」

「——有恒心，还不兴管人家叫矮胖小老头儿，不兴句句抬杠，不兴人家故事讲到一半，拿你自己那批陈年笑料横插进去，把场子搅了。」我怨愤地打断道。

「我亲爱的老儿子，他才不在乎人家叫他矮胖小老头儿呢。你偏揪住这个不放。做矮胖小老头儿又不丢人。我平生识得的一些最贵重的人物，就是矮胖小老头儿。老德里克的毛病，是肝脏略微欠爽。我瞧见他吃奶酪时就心里有数：『这家伙早晚要挨一阵刀扎似的锐痛。』我说小老弟，劳驾再朝那群公鸡撂一两块石头。它们要自相残杀了。」

我原本指望——一面又惶惶地觉得此事成算无几——教授或能一夜之间消了委屈，次日言归于好。但他显然是个全不忌讳「含怒到日落」的人：次日清晨我在海滩上迎面遇上他，他以最不留余地的方式，给了我一个视而不见。

其时菲莉斯正陪在他身边，还有一位姑娘——从两人酷似的眉眼推断，我想是她妹妹。她也有着那一头浓密的棕色软发。可是在我眼里，两两相较，她简直近乎寻常了。

===Original===
It is never pleasant to be cut dead, even when you have done something to deserve it. It is like treading on nothing where one imagined a stair to be. In the present instance the pang was mitigated to a certain extent﻿—not largely﻿—by the fact that Phyllis looked at me. She did not move her head, and I could not have declared positively that she moved her eyes; but nevertheless she certainly looked at me. It was something. She seemed to say that duty compelled her to follow her father’s lead, and that the act must not be taken as evidence of any personal animus.

That, at least, was how I read off the message.

Two days later I met Mr. Chase in the village.

“Hullo, so you’re back,” I said.

“You’ve discovered my secret,” he admitted; “will you have a cigar or a coconut?”

There was a pause.

===Chinese===
被人当死人一般无视，终究不是愉快的事，哪怕你确曾做过招此报应的亏心事。那好比一脚踏了个空——原以为那级台阶还在。在眼下这一例中，创痛总算被一个事实稍稍——仅是稍稍——冲淡：菲莉斯看了我一眼。她头未尝动，她眼珠转没转，我也不能斩钉截铁；然而她确凿是看了我一眼的。这就不枉了。她仿佛在说：本分所系，她不得不随父亲行事，此举万不可引为她个人怀恨的证据。

起码，那讯息我是这般解读的。

两天后，我在村中遇见了蔡斯先生。

「喂，你回来了。」我说。

「你窥破了我的秘密，」他坦承，「来支雪茄，还是来个椰子？」

一阵沉默。

===Original===
“Trouble, I hear, while I was away,” he said.

I nodded.

“The man I live with, Ukridge, did what you warned me against. Touched on the Irish question.”

“Home Rule?”

“He mentioned it among other things.”

“And the professor went off?”

===Chinese===
「我不在的时候，听说出了乱子。」他说。

我点头。

「同我住在一处的那位厄克里奇，干下了你告诫我莫干的事。碰了爱尔兰问题。」

「地方自治？」

「他在好些话题里捎带提了它。」

「教授便炸了？」

===Original===
“Like a bomb.”

“He would. So now you have parted brass rags. It’s a pity.”

I agreed. I am glad to say that I suppressed the desire to ask him to use his influence, if any, with Mr. Derrick to effect a reconciliation. I felt that I must play the game. To request one’s rival to give one assistance in the struggle, to the end that he may be the more readily cut out, can hardly be considered cricket.

“I ought not to be speaking to you, you know,” said Mr. Chase. “You’re under arrest.”

“He’s still﻿—?” I stopped for a word.

“Very much so. I’ll do what I can.”

===Chinese===
「像炸弹一般。」

「他必如此。这么说，你们割袍断义了。可惜。」

我深然其说。我很欣慰地宣布，我抑制住了请他运用其影响力——倘若他有影响力的话——向德里克先生疏通修好的欲望。我自觉必须行事磊落。请自己的情敌在这场角逐里援手，以冀他更利落地被剪除，这无论如何算不得板球家风度。

「按理我不该同你讲话，你是明白人，」蔡斯先生说，「你如今是个在押之身。」

「他还在——？」我顿住，搜寻一个字眼。

「还在得很。我当效力所能及。」

===Original===
“It’s very good of you.”

“But the time is not yet ripe. He may be said at present to be simmering down.”

“I see. Thanks. Goodbye.”

“So long.”

And Mr. Chase walked on with long strides to the Cob.

The days passed slowly. I saw nothing more of Phyllis or her sister. The professor I met once or twice on the links. I had taken earnestly to golf in this time of stress. Golf is the game of disappointed lovers. On the other hand, it does not follow that because a man is a failure as a lover he will be any good at all on the links. My game was distinctly poor at first. But a round or two put me back into my proper form, which is fair.

===Chinese===
「足感盛情。」

「只是时机未熟。目前只可说，他正以文火慢慢降温。」

「明白了。多谢。再会。」

「回见。」

蔡斯先生遂迈开大步，向科布防波堤而去。

日子过得很慢。菲莉斯和她妹妹，我再未见过。教授倒在高尔夫球场上偶遇过一两回。在这段忧患之秋，我认真地打起了高尔夫。高尔夫是失意情人的运动。可是话又说回来，情场上的失败者未必在球场上就有半分可取。起初我的球艺糟糕透顶，但一两轮下来，我便恢复了固有的水准——也就是平平。

===Original===
The professor’s demeanour at these accidental meetings on the links was a faithful reproduction of his attitude on the beach. Only by a studied imitation of the Absolute Stranger did he show that he had observed my presence.

Once or twice, after dinner, when Ukridge was smoking one of his special cigars while Mrs. Ukridge nursed Edwin (now moving in society once more, and in his right mind), I lit my pipe and walked out across the fields through the cool summer night till I came to the hedge that shut off the Derricks’ grounds. Not the hedge through which I had made my first entrance, but another, lower, and nearer the house. Standing there under the shade of a tree I could see the lighted windows of the drawing-room. Generally there was music inside, and, the windows being opened on account of the warmth of the night, I was able to make myself a little more miserable by hearing Phyllis sing. It deepened the feeling of banishment.

I shall never forget those furtive visits. The intense stillness of the night, broken by an occasional rustling in the grass or the hedge; the smell of the flowers in the garden beyond; the distant drone of the sea.

> “God makes sech nights, all white and still,
 

 Fur’z you to look and listen.”

Another day had generally begun before I moved from my hiding place, and started for home, surprised to find my limbs stiff and my clothes bathed with dew.

===Chinese===
球场上这几次不期而遇，教授的举止忠实复刻了他在海滩上的姿态。唯有凭借一番刻意排演的「绝对陌生人」做派，他才透出些许已察觉我在场的意思。

有一两回，晚饭之后，趁厄克里奇抽着他那种特制雪茄、厄克里奇太太看护埃德温（此子如今重出交际场，神智也复了原）的当口，我点上烟斗，穿过凉爽的夏夜，越过田野，一路走到圈着德里克家地界的树篱跟前。不是我头一回翻越的那道，而是另一道，更矮些，也离宅子更近。立在树荫下，望得见客厅里灯火通明的窗户。屋里通常有音乐，又因夜暖开着窗，我便得以及听菲莉斯歌唱，把自己弄得再凄惶几分。流放之感愈见其深了。

那些鬼祟的夜访，我终生不忘。夜的死寂，偶被草间或篱间一阵窸窣打破；篱那边园子里的花香；远处海的单调低吟。

> 「上帝造出这样的夜，白净又安宁，
 

 就为着叫你瞧瞧，听听。」

往往要等另一天业已开始，我才从潜伏处起身回家，惊觉四肢僵直，衣衫尽被露水打湿。

## X I Enlist the Services of a Minion / 十 我雇用了一名打手

===Original===
It would be interesting to know to what extent the work of authors is influenced by their private affairs. If life is flowing smoothly, are the novels they write in that period of content coloured with optimism? And if things are running crosswise, do they work off the resultant gloom on their faithful public? If, for instance, Mr. W. W. Jacobs had toothache, would he write like Hugh Walpole? If Maxim Gorky were invited to lunch by Trotsky, to meet Lenin, would he sit down and dash off a trifle in the vein of Stephen Leacock? Probably the eminent have the power of detaching their writing self from their living, work-a-day self; but, for my own part, the frame of mind in which I now found myself had a disastrous effect on my novel that was to be. I had designed it as a light comedy effort. Here and there a page or two to steady the reader and show him what I could do in the way of pathos if I cared to try; but in the main a thing of sunshine and laughter. But now great slabs of gloom began to work themselves into the scheme of it. A magnificent despondency became its keynote. It would not do. I felt that I must make a resolute effort to shake off my depression. More than ever the need of conciliating the professor was borne in upon me. Day and night I spurred my brain to think of some suitable means of engineering a reconciliation.

In the meantime I worked hard among the fowls, drove furiously on the links, and swam about the harbour when the affairs of the farm did not require my attention.

Things were not going well on our model chicken farm. Little accidents marred the harmony of life in the fowl-run. On one occasion a hen﻿—not Aunt Elizabeth, I am sorry to say﻿—fell into a pot of tar, and came out an unspeakable object. Ukridge put his spare pair of tennis shoes in the incubator, to dry them, and permanently spoiled the future of half-a-dozen eggs which happened to have got there first. Chickens kept straying into the wrong coops, where they got badly pecked by the residents. Edwin slew a couple of Wyandottes, and was only saved from execution by the tears of Mrs. Ukridge.

In spite of these occurrences, however, his buoyant optimism never deserted Ukridge.

“After all,” he said, “what’s one bird more or less? Yes, I know I made a fuss when that beast of a cat lunched off those two, but that was simply the principle of the thing. I’m not going to pay large sums for chickens purely in order that a cat which I’ve never liked can lunch well. Still, we’ve plenty left, and the eggs are coming in better now, though we’ve still a deal of leeway to make up yet in that line. I got a letter from Whiteley’s this morning asking when my first consignment was going to arrive. You know, these people make a mistake in hurrying a man. It annoys him. It irritates him. When we really get going, Garny, my boy, I shall drop Whiteley’s. I shall cut them out of my list and send my eggs to their trade rivals. They shall have a sharp lesson. It’s a little hard. Here am I, worked to death looking after things down here, and these men have the impertinence to bother me about their wretched business. Come in and have a drink, laddie, and let’s talk it over.”

It was on the morning after this that I heard him calling me in a voice in which I detected agitation. I was strolling about the paddock, as was my habit after breakfast, thinking about Phyllis and trying to get my novel into shape. I had just framed a more than usually murky scene for use in the earlier part of the book, when Ukridge shouted to me from the fowl-run.

===Chinese===
作家的私事在多大程度上左右其作品，探究起来想必有趣。倘若生活顺遂，他们在那段顺心岁月里写出的小说，是否也染着乐观的色彩？倘若诸事舛错，他们是否就把郁结的晦气发泄到忠实的读者头上？譬如说，W. W. 雅各布斯先生（W. W. Jacobs）若是牙疼，会不会写出休·沃波尔（Hugh Walpole）的调子？马克西姆·高尔基若蒙托洛茨基邀去午餐、得见列宁，会不会坐下来一挥而就，写一篇斯蒂芬·李科克（Stephen Leacock）式的轻松小玩意儿？名流们多半有本事把写作的自我同过日子的自我分开；至于我，眼下的心境对我那部未来小说产生了灾难性的影响。我原打算写成一部轻喜剧：此处彼处安插一两页，好让读者定定神，也让他瞧瞧我若肯卖弄悲情能有怎样的本事；但大体上是一件阳光与欢笑之作。可如今，大块大块的阴郁开始楔进它的格局，一种堂皇的颓唐成了它的基调。这可不行。我自觉必须奋发一搏，甩掉消沉。我比以往更迫切地痛感到，非向教授修好不可。我日以继夜地鞭策脑筋，思量安排一场和解的合宜方略。

在此期间，我在鸡群中勤恳劳作，在高尔夫球场上奋力挥杆，农场事务无暇顾及我时，便去海港里游上几圈。

我们的模范养鸡场诸事不顺。小小的意外损害着鸡场生活的和气。有一回，一只母鸡——很遗憾，不是伊丽莎白姨妈——跌进了柏油桶，出来时成了一个不堪入目的物件。厄克里奇把他那双备用网球鞋搁进孵化器里去烘干，就此葬送了碰巧先住进去的半打鸡蛋的前程。小鸡们不断误入别家的鸡笼，被笼中住户啄得头破血流。埃德温咬死了两只怀恩多特鸡，全凭厄克里奇太太的眼泪才免于一死。

尽管事故迭出，那股乐天的劲头却始终不离厄克里奇左右。

「说到底，」他说，「多一只鸟少一只鸟算得了什么？是，我知道那头畜生猫拿那两位当午餐的时候，我是闹了一场，可那纯粹是原则问题。我总不能纯粹为了让一只我从来就不喜欢的猫吃好午餐，就大把大把地花钱买鸡吧。好在咱们还剩得多，眼下蛋也来得渐顺了，虽说这一行咱们要补的窟窿还大着呢。今早我收到怀特利商场（Whiteley’s）的信，问我头一批货几时能到。你知道吗，这些人催人可是失策。招人烦。惹人恼。等咱们真正上了轨道，加尼我的小子，我就把怀特利甩了。我把他们从名单上勾掉，把蛋发给他们的商业对头。要给他们一个狠狠的教训。这就有点儿叫人心寒了。我在这儿看摊子，活活累死，这帮人居然好意思拿他们那点破买卖来搅扰我。进来喝一杯，小老弟，咱们合计合计。」

正是这番话之后的第二天早晨，我听见他喊我，嗓音里听得出激动。我正在围场里溜达——这是我早饭后的习惯——想着菲莉斯，同时试着把小说理出个头绪。我刚为这本书的前部构思好一幕格外阴郁的场面，厄克里奇就从鸡场那头朝我喊起来。

===Original===
“Garny, come here. I want you to see the most astounding thing.”

“What’s the matter?” I asked.

“Blest if I know. Look at those chickens. They’ve been doing that for the last half-hour.”

I inspected the chickens. There was certainly something the matter with them. They were yawning﻿—broadly, as if we bored them. They stood about singly and in groups, opening and shutting their beaks. It was an uncanny spectacle.

“What’s the matter with them?”

“Can a chicken get a fit of the blues?” I asked. “Because if so, that’s what they’ve got. I never saw a more bored-looking lot of birds.”

===Chinese===
「加尼，过来。我要你开开眼，瞧瞧最骇人听闻的事。」

「怎么了？」我问。

「鬼才知道。瞧那些鸡。这半个钟头它们一直这样。」

我检视群鸡。它们确实有毛病。它们在打哈欠——张得老大，仿佛我们把它们烦着了似的。它们或独自或成群地站着，一开一合地动嘴。这是一幅瘆人的景象。

「它们这是怎么了？」

「鸡也会犯忧郁症吗？」我问。「要是会，它们得的就是这个。我从没见过神色更厌倦的一群鸟。」

===Original===
“Oh, do look at that poor little brown one by the coop,” said Mrs. Ukridge sympathetically; “I’m sure it’s not well. See, it’s lying down. What *can* be the matter with it?”

“I tell you what we’ll do,” said Ukridge. “We’ll ask Beale. He once lived with an aunt who kept fowls. He’ll know all about it. Beale!”

No answer.

“Beale!!”

A sturdy form in shirtsleeves appeared through the bushes, carrying a boot. We seemed to have interrupted him in the act of cleaning it.

“Beale, you know all about fowls. What’s the matter with these chickens?”

===Chinese===
「喔，快看笼边那只可怜的小棕鸡，」厄克里奇太太满怀同情地说，「我敢保它不舒服。瞧，它躺下了。它*到底*能是怎么啦？」

「我告诉你们个办法，」厄克里奇说。「咱们问比尔。他从前跟一位养鸡的姨妈住过。他门儿清。比尔！」

无人应答。

「比尔！！」

一个穿衬衫的壮实身影从灌木丛里钻出来，手里提着一只靴子。我们似乎打断了人家擦靴子的工程。

「比尔，你对鸡无所不知。这些鸡是怎么了？」

===Original===
The Hired Retainer examined the blasé birds with a wooden expression on his face.

“Well?” said Ukridge.

“The ’ole thing ’ere,” said the Hired Retainer, “is these ’ere fowls have been and got the roop.”

I had never heard of the disease before, but it sounded bad.

“Is that what makes them yawn like that?” said Mrs. Ukridge.

“Yes, ma’am.”

===Chinese===
这位雇仆板着一张木头脸，检视那群意兴阑珊的鸟。

「怎么样？」厄克里奇说。

「整个儿这档子事，」雇仆道，「就是这些鸡呀，愣是害上了鸡喉瘟。」

这病我先前闻所未闻，可听着就不妙。

「是这病才让它们那样打哈欠的吗？」厄克里奇太太问。

「是咧，太太。」

===Original===
“Poor things!”

“Yes, ma’am.”

“And have they all got it?”

“Yes, ma’am.”

“What ought we to do?” asked Ukridge.

“Well, my aunt, sir, when ’er fowls ’ad the roop, she gave them snuff.

===Chinese===
「可怜的东西！」

「是咧，太太。」

「它们全得了吗？」

「是咧，太太。」

「咱们该怎么办？」厄克里奇问。

「呃，老爷，俺姨妈家的鸡得鸡喉瘟那阵，她给它们闻鼻烟。

===Original===
“Give them snuff, she did,” he repeated, with relish, “every morning.”

“Snuff!” said Mrs. Ukridge.

“Yes, ma’am. She give ’em snuff till their eyes bubbled.”

Mrs. Ukridge uttered a faint squeak at this vivid piece of word-painting.

“And did it cure them?” asked Ukridge.

“No, sir,” responded the expert soothingly.

===Chinese===
「给它们闻鼻烟，她真干了，」他咂着嘴回味道，「天天早上。」

「鼻烟！」厄克里奇太太说。

「是咧，太太。她一个劲儿给它们闻，闻到眼珠子冒泡。」

听了这幅生动的文字写生，厄克里奇太太发出一声微弱的尖叫。

「那治好了它们吗？」厄克里奇问。

「没治好，老爷，」这位专家宽慰人心地答道。

===Original===
“Oh, go away, Beale, and clean your beastly boots,” said Ukridge. “You’re no use. Wait a minute. Who would know about this infernal roop thing? One of those farmer chaps would, I suppose. Beale, go off to the nearest farmer, and give him my compliments, and ask him what he does when his fowls get the roop.”

“Yes, sir.”

“No, I’ll go, Ukridge,” I said. “I want some exercise.”

I whistled to Bob, who was investigating a mole-heap in the paddock, and set off in the direction of the village of Up Lyme to consult Farmer Leigh on the matter. He had sold us some fowls shortly after our arrival, so might be expected to feel a kindly interest in their ailing families.

The path to Up Lyme lies across deep-grassed meadows. At intervals it passes over a stream by means of a footbridge. The stream curls through the meadows like a snake.

And at the first of these bridges I met Phyllis.

===Chinese===
「喔，走开吧比尔，擦你那双混账靴子去，」厄克里奇说，「你半点儿不顶用。且慢。这要命的鸡喉瘟，谁懂？我想哪位庄稼汉总该懂。比尔，你跑一趟最近的庄稼汉那儿，替我问候他，问他家的鸡得了鸡喉瘟他怎么办。」

「是咧，老爷。」

「不，还是我去吧，厄克里奇，」我说。「我正想运动运动。」

我吹哨唤来正在围场里考察鼹鼠丘的博布，朝上莱姆村（Up Lyme）方向进发，去就教于农夫利（Farmer Leigh）。我们到达后不久，他卖给我们一批鸡，想来他对这批病中的家属会怀着几分温厚的关切。

通往上莱姆的小径横穿深草的草甸，一路几度越过一条小溪，皆凭独木小桥。溪水像一条蛇，在草甸间蜿蜒而行。

而就在头一座桥上，我遇见了菲莉斯。

===Original===
I came upon her quite suddenly. The other end of the bridge was hidden from my view. I could hear somebody coming through the grass, but not till I was on the bridge did I see who it was. We reached the bridge simultaneously. She was alone. She carried a sketching-block. All nice girls sketch a little.

There was room for one alone on the footbridge, and I drew back to let her pass.

It being the privilege of woman to make the first sign of recognition, I said nothing. I merely lifted my hat in a non-committing fashion.

“Are you going to cut me, I wonder?” I said to myself. She answered the unspoken question as I hoped it would be answered.

“Mr. Garnet,” she said, stopping at the end of the bridge. A pause.

“I couldn’t tell you so before, but I am so sorry this has happened.”

===Chinese===
我与她撞了个措手不及。桥的另一端本在我视线之外。我听得见有人蹚草走来，但直到踏上桥面才看清来者是谁。我们同时上桥。她独自一人，携着一本写生簿。凡是好姑娘，都会画上几笔。

独木桥只容一人通行，我退后一步让她过。

既然先开口打招呼是女性的特权，我便一言不发，只以一副不置可否的姿态抬了抬帽子。

「我倒纳闷，您是不是打算不理我？」我暗自说。她对我这无声的提问，作了如我所愿的回答。

「加尼特先生，」她在桥那头站定，说道。一顿。

「这话我先前没法对您说，可事情闹成这样，我实在过意不去。」

===Original===
“Oh, thanks awfully,” I said, realising as I said it the miserable inadequacy of the English language. At a crisis when I would have given a month’s income to have said something neat, epigrammatic, suggestive, yet withal courteous and respectful, I could only find a hackneyed, unenthusiastic phrase which I should have used in accepting an invitation from a bore to lunch with him at his club.

“Of course you understand my friends﻿—must be my father’s friends.”

“Yes,” I said gloomily, “I suppose so.”

“So you must not think me rude if I﻿—I﻿—”

“Cut me,” said I, with masculine coarseness.

“Don’t seem to see you,” said she, with feminine delicacy, “when I am with my father. You will understand?”

===Chinese===
「喔，多谢多谢，」我说——话一出口便意识到英语何等贫乏得可怜。在这种关头，我情愿捐出一个月的收入换一句妥帖、隽永、意味深长而又不失恭敬的漂亮话，却只捞出一句用滥了的、不冷不热的客套——那句式，本该用来应付一个乏味人邀你去他俱乐部共进午餐。

「您当然明白，我的朋友——必须是我父亲的朋友。」

「是，」我郁郁地说，「我想也是。」

「所以，倘若我——我——，您千万别以为我无礼。」

「不理我。」我以男性的粗豪说道。

「没瞧见您，」她以女性的婉妙说道，「在我陪父亲的时候。您能体谅的罢？」

===Original===
“I shall understand.”

“You see,”﻿—she smiled﻿—“you are under arrest, as Tom says.”

Tom!

“I see,” I said.

“Goodbye.”

“Goodbye.”

===Chinese===
「我会体谅。」

「您瞧，」——她嫣然一笑——「正如汤姆所说，您是在押之身呢。」

汤姆！

「我明白了。」我说。

「再见。」

「再见。」

===Original===
I watched her out of sight, and went on to interview Mr. Leigh.

We had a long and intensely uninteresting conversation about the maladies to which chickens are subject. He was verbose and reminiscent. He took me over his farm, pointing out as we went Dorkings with pasts, and Cochin Chinas which he had cured of diseases generally fatal on, as far as I could gather, Christian Science principles.

I left at last with instructions to paint the throats of the stricken birds with turpentine﻿—a task imagination boggled at, and one which I proposed to leave exclusively to Ukridge and the Hired Retainer﻿—and also a slight headache. A visit to the Cob would, I thought, do me good. I had missed my bathe that morning, and was in need of a breath of sea-air.

It was high-tide, and there was deep water on three sides of the Cob.

In a small boat in the offing Professor Derrick appeared, fishing. I had seen him engaged in this pursuit once or twice before. His only companion was a gigantic boatman, by name Harry Hawk, possibly a descendant of the gentleman of that name who went to Widdicombe Fair with Bill Brewer and old Uncle Tom Cobley and all on a certain memorable occasion, and assisted at the fatal accident to Tom Pearse’s grey mare.

I sat on the seat at the end of the Cob and watched the professor. It was an instructive sight, an object-lesson to those who hold that optimism has died out of the race. I had never seen him catch a fish. He never looked to me as if he were at all likely to catch a fish. Yet he persevered.

===Chinese===
我目送她走远不见，才继续赶路，去拜会利先生。

我们就鸡类易患的诸病症进行了一场冗长而乏味至极的谈话。他絮叨，又好怀旧。他领我参观他的农场，一路指点着各有来历的多尔金鸡（Dorkings）和交趾鸡（Cochin Chinas）——据我所能听懂的，他是循着基督教科学派的原理，治好了它们一般必死无疑的病症。

我终于脱身时，带着两样东西：一是医嘱，用松节油涂刷病鸡的喉咙——这项差事光想想就犯怵，我打算完全留给厄克里奇和雇仆去办——二是一点轻微的头痛。我想，去科布防波堤走一遭兴许有好处。那天早晨我错过了游泳，正需要一口海上的空气。

时值满潮，科布三面都是深水。

远处海面上，一条小船里德里克教授正在垂钓。这营生我先前见他操办过一两回。他唯一的同伴是个块头极大的船夫，名叫哈里·霍克，说不定正是当年那位同名的绅士的后裔——那位绅士曾在某个难忘的日子，随同比尔·布鲁尔和老汤姆·科布利大叔暨全体人马同赴威迪科姆集市（Widdicombe Fair），并在汤姆·皮尔斯那匹灰马的致命事故中出过一份力。

我在科布尽头的座位上坐下，观望教授。这是一幅大有教益的景象，足以给那些认为乐观精神已在这个种族里绝迹的人现身说法。我从没见他钓上过一条鱼。他看上去也从来不像是钓得上鱼的样子。然而他坚韧不拔。

===Original===
There are few things more restful than to watch someone else busy under a warm sun. As I sat there, my pipe drawing nicely as the result of certain explorations conducted that morning with a straw, my mind ranged idly over large subjects and small. I thought of love and chicken-farming. I mused on the immortality of the soul and the deplorable speed at which two ounces of tobacco disappeared. In the end I always returned to the professor. Sitting, as I did, with my back to the beach, I could see nothing but his boat. It had the ocean to itself.

I began to ponder over the professor. I wondered dreamily if he were very hot. I tried to picture his boyhood. I speculated on his future, and the pleasure he extracted from life.

It was only when I heard him call out to Hawk to be careful, when a movement on the part of that oarsman set the boat rocking, that I began to weave romances round him in which I myself figured.

But, once started, I progressed rapidly. I imagined a sudden upset. Professor struggling in water. Myself (heroically): “Courage! I’m coming!” A few rapid strokes. Saved! Sequel, a subdued professor, dripping salt water and tears of gratitude, urging me to become his son-in-law. That sort of thing happened in fiction. It was a shame that it should not happen in real life. In my hot youth I once had seven stories in seven weekly penny papers in the same month, all dealing with a situation of the kind. Only the details differed. In “Not Really a Coward” Vincent Devereux had rescued the earl’s daughter from a fire, whereas in “Hilda’s Hero” it was the peppery old father whom Tom Slingsby saved. Singularly enough, from drowning. In other words, I, a very mediocre scribbler, had effected seven times in a single month what the Powers of the Universe could not manage once, even on the smallest scale.

---

It was precisely three minutes to twelve﻿—I had just consulted my watch﻿—that the great idea surged into my brain. At four minutes to twelve I had been grumbling impotently at Providence. By two minutes to twelve I had determined upon a manly and independent course of action.

===Chinese===
世间少有比望着别人在暖阳下忙碌更养神的事了。我坐在那里，烟斗经那天早晨用一根麦秆疏通探究之后抽得正顺，心思闲闲地在大题目与小题目间游荡。我想到了爱情与养鸡业。我默想到灵魂的不朽，以及两盎司烟丝消失之速度何等可叹。到头来我总回到教授身上。我这样背朝海滩坐着，视野里只有他那条船。整片大洋归它独享。

我开始对着教授出神。我恍恍惚惚地想，他热不热。我试着想象他的少年时代。我揣测他的前途，以及他从生活中榨出的乐趣。

直到听见他喊霍克当心——那位桨手一个动作，船身便摇晃起来——我才开始围绕他编织有我自己登场的传奇。

可是一旦开了头，我便进展神速。我想象一场猝不及防的倾覆。教授在水里扑腾。我（英武地）：「沉着！我来了！」几个利落的划水。得救了！后话：一位服帖的教授，滴着盐水和感激的泪，力劝我做他的东床快婿。这一类事情小说里常有，现实里偏偏没有，岂不冤哉。在我火热的青年时代，我曾有一个月在七家便士周刊上各发一篇小说，七篇写的全是这一路局面，只有细节不同。在《并非真懦夫》里，文森特·德弗罗从火中救了伯爵的女儿；而在《希尔达的英雄》里，被汤姆·斯灵斯比救下的则是那位火爆脾气的老父亲。说来也奇，正是从水里救的。换句话说，我这个三流写手，一个月里成就了七次的事，宇宙的大能们连一次也办不成，连最小规模的也办不成。

---

那个伟大的主意涌进我脑海时，正是差三分十二点——我刚看过表。差四分十二点时，我还在有气无力地怨天。到差两分十二点，我已定下一条有丈夫气、独立自主的行动方针。

===Original===
Briefly, it was this. Providence had failed to give satisfaction. I would, therefore, cease my connection with it, and start a rival business on my own account. After all, if you want a thing done well, you must do it yourself.

In other words, since a dramatic accident and rescue would not happen of its own accord, I would arrange one for myself. Hawk looked to me the sort of man who would do anything in a friendly way for a few shillings.

I had now to fight it out with Conscience. I quote the brief report which subsequently appeared in the *Recording Angel*:﻿—

> *Three-Round Contest*: **Conscience** (Celestial B.C.) *v.* J. **Garnet** (unattached).
> *Round One*.﻿—Conscience came to the scratch smiling and confident. Led off lightly with a statement that it would be bad for a man of the professor’s age to get wet. Garnet countered heavily, alluding to the warmth of the weather and the fact that the professor habitually enjoyed a bathe every day. Much sparring, Conscience not quite so confident, and apparently afraid to come to close quarters with his man. Time called, with little damage done.
> *Round Two*.﻿—Conscience, much freshened by the half minute’s rest, feinted with the charge of deceitfulness, and nearly got home heavily with “What would Phyllis say if she knew?” Garnet, however, sidestepped cleverly with “But she won’t know,” and followed up the advantage with a damaging, “Besides, it’s all for the best.” The round ended with a brisk rally on general principles, Garnet crowding in a lot of work. Conscience down twice, and only saved by the call of time.
> *Round Three (and last)*.﻿—Conscience came up very weak, and with Garnet as strong as ever it was plain that the round would be a brief one. This proved to be the case. Early in the second minute Garnet cross-countered with “All’s Fair in Love and War.” Conscience down and out. The winner left the ring without a mark.

===Chinese===
简而言之，是这么回事。天意未能令人满意。因此，我拟与它终止合作，自立门户，当它的对头号。毕竟，事情要想办好，非亲自动手不可。

换言之，既然富于戏剧性的意外与营救不肯自动发生，我便亲手安排一场。依我看，霍克正是那种看在几先令份上肯帮朋友办任何事的好汉。

这时我须得同良心决一胜负。其后刊于《记录天使》的简短战报，兹引用如下：——

> *三回合大赛*：**良心**（天界拳击会）*对* J. **加尼特**（无门无派）。
> *第一回合*。——良心含笑上场，自信满满。先轻刺一记：教授这把年纪的人，湿了身子恐怕不妙。加尼特沉重还击，援引天气之暖，以及教授惯于每日游泳的事实。一阵游斗，良心不复那般自信，且显然怕与对手短兵相接。鸣锣收场，两无损伤。
> *第二回合*。——良心经半分钟休憩，精神一爽，以「欺瞒」之罪虚晃一枪，随即「菲莉斯若知道了会怎么说」一记重拳险些得手。然而加尼特以「可她不会知道」灵巧侧步闪过，并趁势补上杀伤性的一句：「再说，这全是为了大家好。」本回合以一场关乎大原则的对攻作结，加尼特连连抢攻。良心两度倒地，全凭鸣锣方得解救。
> *第三回合（兼末回合）*。——良心出场时已虚弱不堪，而加尼特气力丝毫不减，这一回合之短，一眼便知。事实也应验了。第二分钟刚开始，加尼特便以「情场战场，兵不厌诈」一记交叉反击。良心倒地，出局。胜者不带半处伤痕，扬长离台。

===Original===
I rose, feeling much refreshed.

That afternoon I interviewed Mr. Hawk in the bar-parlour of the Net and Mackerel.

“Hawk,” I said to him darkly, over a mystic and conspirator-like pot of ale, “I want you, next time you take Professor Derrick out fishing”﻿—here I glanced round, to make sure that we were not overheard﻿—“to upset him.”

His astonished face rose slowly from the pot of ale like a full moon.

“What ’ud I do that for?” he gasped.

“Five shillings, I hope,” said I, “but I am prepared to go to ten.”

===Chinese===
我起身时，只觉神清气爽。

当天下午，我在「网与鲭鱼」客栈（the Net and Mackerel）的酒吧间里会见了霍克先生。

「霍克，」我隔着神秘兮兮、密谋口气的一罐麦酒，阴沉沉地对他说，「我要你，下次带德里克教授出海垂钓的时候」——说到此处我环顾四周，确认没人偷听——「把他掀进海里去。」

他那张惊愕的脸从酒罐后面缓缓升起，宛如一轮满月。

「俺干吗要那么干？」他倒抽一口气。

「但愿五个先令，」我说，「不过我预备加到十个。」

===Original===
He gurgled.

I encored his pot of ale.

He kept on gurgling.

I argued with the man.

I spoke splendidly. I was eloquent, but at the same time concise. My choice of words was superb. I crystallised my ideas into pithy sentences which a child could have understood.

And at the end of half-an-hour he had grasped the salient points of the scheme. Also he imagined that I wished the professor upset by way of a practical joke. He gave me to understand that this was the type of humour which was to be expected from a gentleman from London. I am afraid he must at one period in his career have lived at one of those watering-places at which trippers congregate. He did not seem to think highly of the Londoner.

===Chinese===
他咕噜了一声。

我为他那罐麦酒来了个返场。

他兀自咕噜。

我同这人展开论辩。

我说得气派。我雄辩，却又简洁。措辞精美绝伦。我把主意凝炼成孩童也听得懂的警策之句。

半个钟头之后，他总算把握了方案的要点。同时他认定，我是想借教授落水行一个实际的玩笑。他让我明白，这正是伦敦来的绅士们意想之中的那路幽默。我怕是他生涯中的某一时期，准在那种一日游游客麇集的海滨住过。他似乎不大瞧得起伦敦人。

===Original===
I let it rest at that. I could not give my true reason, and this served as well as any.

---

At the last moment he recollected that he, too, would get wet when the accident took place, and raised the price to a sovereign.

A mercenary man. It is painful to see how rapidly the old simple spirit is dying out of our rural districts. Twenty years ago a fisherman would have been charmed to do a little job like that for a screw of tobacco.

===Chinese===
我便由它去了。真实缘由不能奉告，这个说法同任何别的说法一样合用。

---

临到末一刻，他想起出事时他自己也要湿身，便把价钱涨到了一镑。

一个认钱不认人的家伙。眼见古朴之风在我们的乡间消逝得这般迅疾，令人痛心。二十年前，渔夫为了一小撮烟丝，办这么件小差事还巴不得呢。

## II Mr. and Mrs. S. F. Ukridge / 二 S. F. 厄克里奇先生与太太

===Original===
I have often thought that *Who’s Who*, though a bulky and well-meaning volume, omits too many of England’s greatest men. It is not comprehensive enough. I am in it, nestling among the G’s:﻿—

> “**Garnet**, Jeremy, o.s. of late Henry Garnet, vicar of Much Middlefold, Salop; author. Publications: *The Outsider*, *The Manœuvres of Arthur*. Hobbies: Cricket, football, swimming, golf. Clubs: Arts.”

But if you search among the U’s for **Ukridge**, Stanley Featherstonehaugh, details of whose tempestuous career would make really interesting reading, you find no mention of him. It seems unfair, though I imagine Ukridge bears it with fortitude. That much-enduring man has had a lifetime’s training in bearing things with fortitude.

He seemed in his customary jovial spirits now, as he dashed into the room, clinging on to the pince-nez which even ginger-beer wire rarely kept stable for two minutes together.

“My dear old man,” he shouted, springing at me and seizing my hand in a grip like the bite of a horse. “How *are* you, old buck? This is good. By Jove, this is fine, what?”

===Chinese===
我时常想，《名人录》（*Who’s Who*）虽是部厚重而好心的大书，却漏收了太多英格兰的伟人。它不够全。我自己倒是在里面，偎在 G 字头中间：——

> 「**加尼特**，杰里米，故亨利·加尼特之独子，其人生前为萨罗普郡小米德尔福尔德教区牧师；作家。著作：《局外人》《阿瑟的谋略》。爱好：板球、足球、游泳、高尔夫。俱乐部：艺术俱乐部。」

可是你到 U 字头里去找**厄克里奇，斯坦利·费瑟斯通豪**——他那风雷激荡的生涯若写出来，必定好看得很——却一字也寻不见。这似有不公，不过我想厄克里奇自有坚忍承之。这位久经磨难的汉子，一辈子受的就是坚忍承之的训练。

此刻他照常兴冲冲的，一阵风闯进屋来，一手揪着那副夹鼻眼镜——连姜汁酒丝做的镜架，也很少能叫它安稳上两分钟。

「亲爱的老伙计，」他嚷道，一蹦而起扑向我，一把握住我的手，那手劲赛过马咬。「你*可*好，老兄弟？太棒了。老天，这真是妙，是不是？」

===Original===
He dashed to the door and looked out.

“Come on, Millie! Pick up the waukeesis. Here’s old Garnet, looking just the same as ever. Devilish handsome fellow! You’ll be glad you came when you see him. Beats the Zoo hollow!”

There appeared round the corner of Ukridge a young woman. She paused in the doorway and smiled pleasantly.

“Garny, old horse,” said Ukridge with some pride, “this is *her*! The pride of the home. Companion of joys and sorrows and all the rest of it. In fact,” in a burst of confidence, “my wife.”

I bowed awkwardly. The idea of Ukridge married was something too overpowering to be readily assimilated.

“Buck up, old horse,” said Ukridge encouragingly. He had a painful habit of addressing all and sundry by that title. In his schoolmaster days﻿—at one period of his vivid career he and I had been colleagues on the staff of a private school﻿—he had made use of it interviewing the parents of new pupils, and the latter had gone away, as a rule, with a feeling that this must be either the easy manner of Genius or due to alcohol, and hoping for the best. He also used it to perfect strangers in the streets, and on one occasion had been heard to address a bishop by that title, rendering that dignitary, as Mr. Baboo Jaberjee would put it, *sotto voce* with gratification. “Surprised to find me married, what? Garny, old boy,”﻿—sinking his voice to a whisper almost inaudible on the other side of the street﻿—“take my tip. Go and jump off the dock yourself. You’ll feel another man. Give up this bachelor business. It’s a mug’s game. I look on you bachelors as excrescences on the social system. I regard you, old man, purely and simply as a wart. Go and get married, laddie, go and get married. By gad, I’ve forgotten to pay the cabby. Lend me a couple of bob, Garny old chap.”

===Chinese===
他冲到门口张望。

「来呀，米莉！抬起你的小脚脚。老加尼特就在这儿，模样儿一点儿没变。鬼精灵的帅小伙子！见了管教你觉得这趟不虚此行。把动物园都盖过去了！」

厄克里奇的身后转出一位少妇。她在门口站定，宜人地一笑。

「加尼老伙计，」厄克里奇不无得意地说，「这位就是*她*！家里的骄傲。苦乐与共的伴侣，以及其他种种。总而言之，」他倾心吐胆，「是我太太。」

我笨拙地鞠了一躬。厄克里奇居然结了婚——这个概念太过骇人，一时不易消化。

「振作点，老伙计，」厄克里奇鼓励道。他有个令人难堪的习惯，见谁都这么称呼。当年他做教员时——他那多彩生涯中有一段，你我同在一所私立学校的教席上共事——他接见新生家长时也这么叫，家长照例带着一种感觉离去：这要么是天才的随和，要么是喝了酒，只好往好处想。他对街上的生人也这么叫，有一回竟有人听见他用这头衔去称呼一位主教，据巴布·贾伯吉先生（Mr. Baboo Jaberjee）的措辞，那位尊贵者*低声*表示了欣慰。「想不到我结了婚吧，什么？加尼老小子，」——他把声音压到街对面几乎听不见的耳语——「听我一句。你也去跳一回码头吧。保你换了个人。把这光棍营生戒了吧。那是傻瓜的行当。我把你们这帮光棍看作社会机体上的赘疣。老伙计，我把你干脆看成一颗疣子。去结婚吧小老弟，去结婚吧。嚯，我忘了付车夫钱了。借两个先令，加尼老哥。」

===Original===
He was out of the door and on his way downstairs before the echoes of his last remark had ceased to shake the window. I was left to entertain Mrs. Ukridge.

So far her share in the conversation had been confined to the pleasant smile which was apparently her chief form of expression. Nobody talked very much when Ukridge was present. She sat on the edge of the armchair, looking very small and quiet. I was conscious of feeling a benevolent pity for her. If I had been a girl, I would have preferred to marry a volcano. A little of Ukridge, as his former headmaster had once said in a moody, reflective voice, went a very long way.

“You and Stanley have known each other a long time, haven’t you?” said the object of my commiseration, breaking the silence.

“Yes. Oh, yes. Several years. We were masters at the same school.”

Mrs. Ukridge leaned forward with round, shining eyes.

“Really? Oh, how nice!” she said ecstatically.

===Chinese===
他的余音还在窗玻璃上震颤，人已出门下楼去了。招待厄克里奇太太的差事落在我头上。

到目前为止，她在谈话里的份量，仅限于那个看来是她主要表达方式的宜人微笑。厄克里奇在场时，谁也不很多话。她坐在扶手椅边上，显得小小的、静静的。我自觉对她怀着一种仁慈的怜惜。我若是个姑娘，宁可嫁一座火山。正如厄克里奇从前的校长有一次以忧郁而沉思的声调所说，厄克里奇这东西，小小一点儿就顶很长一段路。

「您和斯坦利认识很久了吧？」我怜惜的对象开口打破沉默。

「是的。哦，是的。好几年了。我们同在一所学校任教。」

厄克里奇太太身子前倾，眼睛圆圆发亮。

「真的？喔，多好呀！」她心醉神迷地说。

===Original===
Not yet, to judge from her expression and the tone of her voice, had she found any disadvantages attached to the arduous position of being Mrs. Stanley Ukridge.

“He’s a wonderfully versatile man,” I said.

“I believe he could do anything.”

“He’d have a jolly good try!”

“Have you ever kept fowls?” asked Mrs. Ukridge, with apparent irrelevance.

I had not. She looked disappointed.

===Chinese===
看她的神情和口气，她还没发现当斯坦利·厄克里奇太太这个艰辛职位有什么不利之处。

「他是个惊人的多面手。」我说。

「我相信他什么都干得了。」

「他准会放手大试一场！」

「您养过鸡吗？」厄克里奇太太问，问得没头没脑。

我没养过。她面露失望。

===Original===
“I was hoping you might have had some experience. Stanley, of course, can turn his hand to anything; but I think experience is rather a good thing, don’t you?”

“Yes. But﻿ ﻿…”

“I have bought a shilling book called *Fowls and All About Them*, and this week’s copy of *C.A.C.*”

“*C.A.C.*?”

“*Chiefly About Chickens*. It’s a paper, you know. But it’s all rather hard to understand. You see, we﻿ ﻿… but here is Stanley. He will explain the whole thing.”

“Well, Garny, old horse,” said Ukridge, reentering the room after another energetic passage of the stairs. “Years since I saw you. Still buzzing along?”

===Chinese===
「我本指望您有点儿经验。斯坦利当然干什么都是一把好手；不过我想，经验总归是件好东西，您说呢？」

「是。不过……」

「我买了一先令一本的书，叫《鸡及其一切》（*Fowls and All About Them*），还有这礼拜的一期《C.A.C.》。」

「《C.A.C.》？」

「《主要关于鸡》（*Chiefly About Chickens*）。是份报纸，您知道。可这些都挺难懂。您瞧，我们……哦，斯坦利来了。他会把整件事讲明白的。」

「嗳，加尼老伙计，」厄克里奇说，又一阵轰轰烈烈的上下楼之后回到屋里。「好几年没见你了。还在嗡嗡地转？」

===Original===
“Still, so to speak, buzzing,” I assented.

“I was reading your last book the other day.”

“Yes?” I said, gratified. “How did you like it?”

“Well, as a matter of fact, laddie, I didn’t get beyond the third page, because the scurvy knave at the bookstall said he wasn’t running a free library, and in one way and another there was a certain amount of unpleasantness. Still, it seemed bright and interesting up to page three. But let’s settle down and talk business. I’ve got a scheme for you, Garny old man. Yessir, the idea of a thousand years. Now listen to me for a moment. Let me get a word in edgeways.”

He sat down on the table, and dragged up a chair as a leg-rest. Then he took off his pince-nez, wiped them, readjusted the ginger-beer wire behind his ears, and, having hit a brown patch on the knee of his grey flannel trousers several times, in the apparent hope of removing it, resumed:

“About fowls.”

===Chinese===
「可以说，还在转。」我应道。

「前几天我正在读你上一本书。」

「是吗？」我说，心中窃喜。「你觉得如何？」

「嗳，实不相瞒，小老弟，我没读到第三页以后，因为书摊上那个恶棍说，他这儿可不是免费图书馆，一来二去就闹得有点儿不愉快。不过，到第三页为止，似乎又明快又有趣。咱们坐下谈正事吧。我给你备了个方案，加尼老伙计。是，千年一遇的主意。且听我道来。容我把话插进个缝儿。」

他坐上桌子，拖过一把椅子来搁腿。然后摘下夹鼻眼镜，擦了擦，把姜汁酒丝在耳后重新安顿好，又对着灰色法兰绒裤膝上的一块褐斑拍了好几下——显然指望拍掉它——这才继续说：

「关于鸡。」

===Original===
The subject was beginning to interest me. It showed a curious tendency to creep into the conversation of the Ukridge family.

“I want you to give me your undivided attention for a moment. I was saying to my wife, as we came here, ‘Garnet’s the man! Clever devil, Garnet. Full of ideas.’ Didn’t I, Millie?”

“Yes, dear.”

“Laddie,” said Ukridge impressively, “we are going to keep fowls.”

He shifted himself farther on to the table and upset the ink-pot.

“Never mind,” he said, “it’ll soak in. It’s good for the texture. Or am I thinking of tobacco-ash on the carpet? Well, never mind. Listen to me! When I said that we were going to keep fowls, I didn’t mean in a small, piffling sort of way﻿—two cocks and a couple of hens and a golf-ball for a nest-egg. We are going to do it on a large scale. We are going to run a chicken farm!”

===Chinese===
这题目开始引起我的兴趣了。它表现出一种钻进厄克里奇家谈话的古怪倾向。

「我要你专心听我说一会儿。来的路上我跟内人说：『加尼特正是咱们的人！聪明的家伙，加尼特。一脑子主意。』是不是，米莉？」

「是的，亲爱的。」

「小老弟，」厄克里奇庄重地说，「我们要养鸡。」

他往桌子上又挪了挪，把墨水瓶掀翻了。

「不要紧，」他说，「它会渗进去的。对质地有好处。要不我说的是地毯上的烟灰？算了，不要紧。听我说！我说我们要养鸡，不是小打小闹地养——两只公鸡两三只母鸡，再拿个高尔夫球当引蛋。咱们要大规模地干。咱们要办一座养鸡场！」

===Original===
“A chicken farm,” echoed Mrs. Ukridge with an affectionate and admiring glance at her husband.

“Ah,” I said, feeling my responsibilities as chorus. “A chicken farm.”

“I’ve thought it all over, laddie, and it’s as clear as mud. No expenses, large profits, quick returns. Chickens, eggs, and the money streaming in faster than you can bank it. Winter and summer underclothing, my bonny boy, lined with crackling Bradbury’s. It’s the idea of a lifetime. Now listen to me for a moment. You get your hen﻿—”

“One hen?”

“Call it one for the sake of argument. It makes my calculations clearer. Very well, then. Harriet the hen﻿—you get her. Do you follow me so far?”

“Yes. You get a hen.”

===Chinese===
「养鸡场。」厄克里奇太太应声重复，深情而崇拜地看了丈夫一眼。

「啊。」我说，尽着合唱队的本分。「一座养鸡场。」

「我全都盘算好了，小老弟，清清楚楚，像烂泥一样清楚。没有开销，利润丰厚，回笼迅速。鸡啊，蛋啊，钱像流水般涌进来，你存都存不及。冬夏的内衣，我漂亮的孩子，全用脆响的布拉德伯里钞票做衬里。这是一辈子的主意。且听我说片刻。你先弄来一只母鸡——」

「一只母鸡？」

「为论证起见就算一只。这样我的账目清楚。很好。那么，母鸡哈丽雅特——你把她弄到手了。到这一步你跟上了吗？」

「跟上了。你弄到一只母鸡。」

===Original===
“I told you Garnet was a dashed bright fellow,” said Ukridge approvingly to his attentive wife. “Notice the way he keeps right after one’s ideas? Like a bloodhound. Well, where was I?”

“You’d just got a hen.”

“Exactly. The hen. Priscilla the pullet. Well, it lays an egg every day of the week. You sell the eggs, six for half a crown. Keep of hen costs nothing. Profit﻿—at least a couple of bob on every dozen eggs. What do you think of that?”

“I think I’d like to overhaul the figures in case of error.”

“Error!” shouted Ukridge, pounding the table till it groaned. “Error? Not a bit of it. Can’t you follow a simple calculation like that? Oh, I forgot to say that you get﻿—and here is the nub of the thing﻿—you get your first hen on tick. Anybody will be glad to let you have the hen on tick. Well, then, you let this hen﻿—this first, original hen, this on-tick hen﻿—you let it set and hatch chickens. Now follow me closely. Suppose you have a dozen hens. Very well, then. When each of the dozen has a dozen chickens, you send the old hens back to the chappies you borrowed them from, with thanks for kind loan; and there you are, starting business with a hundred and forty-four free chickens to your name. And after a bit, when the chickens grow up and begin to lay, all you have to do is to sit back in your chair and endorse the big cheques. Isn’t that so, Millie?”

===Chinese===
「我早说过加尼特是个鬼精明的家伙，」厄克里奇对洗耳恭听的太太赞许道。「注意到他紧咬住人家思路不放的样子吗？像条猎犬。好，我说到哪儿了？」

「你刚弄到一只母鸡。」

「正是。母鸡。小母鸡普丽西拉。好，它一礼拜天天都下蛋。你把蛋卖掉，半克朗六个。母鸡的饲养不要钱。利润——每打蛋至少两个先令。你以为如何？」

「我想把账目核一核，以防有错。」

「错！」厄克里奇吼道，捶得桌子直呻吟。「错？一点儿没有。这么简单的一笔账你都跟不上？哦，忘了说，你那只——要害就在这儿——你头一只母鸡是赊来的。谁都乐意赊你一只母鸡。好，然后，你让这只母鸡——这头一只、原始的、赊账来的母鸡——抱窝孵小鸡。仔细听着。假设你有十二只母鸡。很好。等这十二只每只孵出十二只小鸡，你把老母鸡还给借鸡给你的那些家伙，谢谢惠借；于是你就以一百四十四只白得的小鸡起家。过些时候，等小鸡长大开始下蛋，你只消往椅背上一靠，给大额支票背书就行了。是不是这样，米莉？」

===Original===
“Yes, dear.”

“We’ve fixed it all up. Do you know Combe Regis, in Dorsetshire? On the borders of Devon. Bathing. Sea-air. Splendid scenery. Just the place for a chicken farm. A friend of Millie’s﻿—girl she knew at school﻿—has lent us a topping old house, with large grounds. All we’ve got to do is to get in the fowls. I’ve ordered the first lot. We shall find them waiting for us when we arrive.”

“Well,” I said, “I’m sure I wish you luck. Mind you let me know how you get on.”

“Let you know!” roared Ukridge. “Why, my dear old horse, you’re coming with us.”

“Am I?” I said blankly.

“Certainly you are. We shall take no refusal. Will we, Millie?”

===Chinese===
「是的，亲爱的。」

「我们全安排妥了。你知道多塞特郡的库姆里吉斯吗？在德文郡边上。可以游泳。海上的空气。风景绝佳。正是办养鸡场的好地方。米莉的一位朋友——她上学时认识的一个姑娘——借给我们一幢顶呱呱的老房子，带大园子。咱们只消把鸡弄进去。头一批我已经订好了。咱们到的时候，它们准在那儿等着咱们。」

「好吧，」我说，「我一定祝你们顺利。可别忘了让我知道你们的进展。」

「让你知道！」厄克里奇咆哮道。「嗳呀，我亲爱的老伙计，你要跟咱们一起去。」

「是吗？」我茫然地说。

「你当然要去。咱们决不接受拒绝。会不会，米莉？」

===Original===
“No, dear.”

“Of course not. No refusal of any sort. Pack up tonight and meet us at Waterloo tomorrow.”

“It’s awfully good of you﻿ ﻿…”

“Not a bit of it﻿—not a bit of it. This is pure business. I was saying to Millie as we came along that you were the very man for us. A man with your flow of ideas will be invaluable on a chicken farm. Absolutely invaluable. You see,” proceeded Ukridge, “I’m one of those practical fellows. The hardheaded type. I go straight ahead, following my nose. What you want in a business of this sort is a touch of the dreamer to help out the practical mind. We look to you for suggestions, laddie. Flashes of inspiration and all that sort of thing. Of course, you take your share of the profits. That’s understood. Yes, yes, I must insist. Strict business between friends. Now, taking it that, at a conservative estimate, the net profits for the first fiscal year amount to﻿—five thousand﻿—no, better be on the safe side﻿—say, four thousand five hundred pounds﻿ ﻿… But we’ll arrange all that end of it when we get down there. Millie will look after that. She’s the secretary of the concern. She’s been writing letters to people asking for hens. So you see it’s a thoroughly organised business. How many hen-letters did you write last week, old girl?”

“Ten, dear.”

Ukridge turned triumphantly to me.

===Chinese===
「不会，亲爱的。」

「当然不会。什么拒绝都不接受。今晚收拾行李，明天滑铁卢车站见。」

「你们太客气了……」

「一点儿也不——一点儿也不。这纯粹是生意。来的路上我跟米莉就说，你正是咱们需要的人。一个像你这样主意泉涌的人，在养鸡场上是无价之宝。绝对无价。你要明白，」厄克里奇接着说，「我是那种实干家。硬脑袋型。我认准了鼻子朝前直走。这种生意里需要的，是一点儿梦想家气质，来帮衬实干家的头脑。咱们指望你出主意呢，小老弟。灵光一闪，诸如此类。当然，利润有你一份。这不必说的。是，是，我必须坚持。朋友之间，公事公办。这样吧，保守估计，第一个财政年度的纯利为——五千——不，稳妥起见——就算四千五百镑……不过这一摊到了那边再安排。米莉会照管。她是这买卖的秘书。她一直在给人写信讨鸡。可见这是桩组织严密的买卖。上礼拜你写了多少封鸡信，好姑娘？」

「十封，亲爱的。」

厄克里奇得意扬扬地转向我。

===Original===
“You hear? Ten. Ten letters asking for hens. That’s the way to succeed. Push and enterprise.”

“Six of them haven’t answered, Stanley, dear, and the rest refused.”

“Immaterial,” said Ukridge with a grand gesture. “That doesn’t matter. The point is that the letters were written. It shows we are solid and practical. Well now, can you get your things ready by tomorrow, Garny old horse?”
