It may have been liver or a chill, but it was certainly not the weather. The morning was perfect﻿—the most glorious of a glorious summer. There was a haze over the valley and out to sea which suggested a warm noon, when the sun should have begun the serious duties of the day. The birds were singing in the trees and breakfasting on the lawn, while Edwin, seated on one of the flowerbeds, watched them with the eye of a connoisseur. Occasionally, when a sparrow hopped in his direction, he would make a sudden spring, and the bird would fly away to the other side of the lawn. I had never seen Edwin catch a sparrow. I believe they looked on him as a bit of a crank, and humoured him by coming within springing distance, just to keep him amused. Dashing young cock-sparrows would show off before their particular hen-sparrows, and earn a cheap reputation for daredevilry by going within so many yards of Edwin’s lair, and then darting away. Bob was in his favourite place on the gravel. I took him with me down to the Cob to watch me bathe.

“What’s the matter with me today, Robert, old son?” I asked him, as I dried myself.

He blinked lazily, but contributed no suggestion.

===Chinese===

世上有一种人，是不信预感的。那种「怕要出什么不妙之事」的古怪感觉，他们总要归咎于肝火、着凉或者天气之类俗不可耐的缘由。而依我看来，此中名堂，远非走马观花的旁观者所能想见。

在俱乐部同教授碰面之后第三天，我一觉醒来，满心是闷闷的凶兆。不知怎么，我仿佛知道，这一天注定要跟我过不去。这兴许是肝火作祟，也许是受了凉，但肯定怪不着天气。清晨十全十美——是一个灿烂夏季里最灿烂的早晨。山谷上、海面上罩着一层薄雾，预告待太阳着手办理当天的正事之后，正午会很暖和。鸟儿在树间歌唱，在草坪上进早餐；埃德温蹲踞在一座花坛上，以行家的慧眼注视着它们。偶尔有麻雀朝他这边跳近，他便骤然一扑，那鸟便振翅飞往草坪另一头去。我从没见埃德温逮住过麻雀。我相信麻雀们把他看作一位无伤大雅的怪人，特意迁就他，跳进一扑可及的距离之内，好教他不致寂寞。血气方刚的少年公麻雀还要在各自身边的母麻雀面前显本事，冲到离埃德温的巢穴只剩几码的地方，挣一个亡命之徒的廉价名声，随即疾飞而遁。博布趴在砾石地上它中意的老位置。我带它一道下到科布防波堤，看我游泳。

「罗伯特，老伙计，我今天到底是怎么了？」我一面擦干身子，一面问它。

它懒洋洋地眨了眨眼，不贡献半点高见。

===Original===

“It’s no good looking bored,” I went on, “because I’m going to talk about myself, however much it bores you. Here am I, as fit as a prizefighter, living in the open air for I don’t know how long, eating good plain food﻿—bathing every morning﻿—sea-bathing, mind you﻿—and yet what’s the result? I feel beastly.”

Bob yawned, and gave a little whine.

“Yes,” I said, “I know I’m in love. But that can’t be it, because I was in love just as much a week ago, and I felt all right then. But isn’t she an angel, Bob? Eh? Isn’t she? And didn’t you feel bucked when she patted you? Of course you did. Anybody would. But how about Tom Chase? Don’t you think he’s a dangerous man? He calls her by her Christian name, you know, and behaves generally as if she belonged to him. And then he sees her every day, while I have to trust to meeting her at odd times, and then I generally feel such a fool I can’t think of anything to talk about except golf and the weather. He probably sings duets with her after dinner, and you know what comes of duets after dinner.”

Here Bob, who had been trying for some time to find a decent excuse for getting away, pretended to see something of importance at the other end of the Cob, and trotted off to investigate it, leaving me to finish dressing by myself.

===Chinese===

「摆出这副百无聊赖的嘴脸也没用，」我接着说，「因为我要谈的是我自己的事，管你腻烦不腻烦。瞧瞧我：壮得赛过职业拳师，在露天里住了不知多少时日，吃的是实惠的粗茶淡饭——天天早晨沐浴——注意，是海水浴——结果怎么样？浑身不自在。

博布打了个哈欠，低低呜了一声。

「是啊，」我说，「我知道我这是害了相思。可毛病不在这儿，因为一星期前我照样害相思，那会儿却安然无恙。可她是不是位天使，博布？嗯？是不是？她拍你的时候，你心里是不是美开了花？当然是。谁都得美。可是汤姆·蔡斯呢？你不觉得他是个危险人物么？你要知道，他开口闭口叫她的教名，一言一动都俨然她已归他所有。再说他天天见得着她，我却只能指望萍水相逢，而且一相逢便自觉傻头傻脑，除了高尔夫和天气，再想不出一句话可讲。晚饭后他说不定还同她唱二重唱呢，而晚饭后的二重唱会唱出什么结果，你是知道的。」

这时博布早已寻思了好一阵，想找个体面的借口脱身，便装作望见防波堤那一头有什么重大情况，颠儿颠儿地跑去查勘，剩下我独自把衣裳穿戴完毕。

===Original===

“Of course,” I said to myself, “It may be merely hunger. I may be all right after breakfast. But at present I seem to be working up for a really fine fit of the blues. I feel bad.”

I whistled to Bob, and started for home. On the beach I saw the professor some little distance away, and waved my towel in a friendly manner. He made no reply.

Of course, it was possible that he had not seen me; but for some reason his attitude struck me as ominous. As far as I could see, he was looking straight at me, and he was not a shortsighted man. I could think of no reason why he should cut me. We had met on the links on the previous morning, and he had been friendliness itself. He had called me “me dear boy,” supplied me with a gin and ginger beer at the clubhouse, and generally behaved as if he had been David and I Jonathan. Yet in certain moods we are inclined to make mountains out of molehills, and I went on my way, puzzled and uneasy, with a distinct impression that I had received the cut direct.

I felt hurt. What had I done that Providence should make things so unpleasant for me? It would be a little hard, as Ukridge would have said, if, after all my trouble, the professor had discovered some fresh grievance against me. Perhaps Ukridge had been irritating him again. I wished he would not identify me so completely with Ukridge. I could not be expected to control the man. Then I reflected that they could hardly have met in the few hours between my parting from the professor at the clubhouse and my meeting with him on the beach. Ukridge rarely left the farm. When he was not working among the fowls, he was lying on his back in the paddock, resting his massive mind.

===Chinese===

「当然，」我自忖，「也可能不过是饿了。吃过早饭说不定就没事了。可眼下我却像在酝酿一场顶刮刮的忧郁大发作。我难受。」

我吹了声口哨唤博布，动身回农庄。海滩上，我瞥见教授在相距不远的地方，便友好地朝他挥舞毛巾。他毫无表示。

当然，也可能是他没瞧见我；但不知怎的，他这副态度教我觉得凶多吉少。据我看来，他直勾勾望着我，而他并非近视。我想不出他有什么理由要冷落我。前一天早晨我们还在高尔夫球场上碰过头，他友善得无以复加，口口声声「我亲爱的孩子」，在俱乐部请我喝了一杯杜松子酒兑姜汁汽水，一言一动都俨然他是大卫、我是约拿单。然而人在某种心绪之下，惯会把鼹鼠丘看成高山，于是我一路走，一路纳闷，一路不安，只觉得自家的的确确挨了一记响亮的「当面不理」。

我觉得冤枉。我做了什么事，上天要这样同我过不去？倘若辛苦一场之后，教授又寻出新的碴儿来记恨我——照厄克里奇的说法，那就未免有点儿够呛了。多半又是厄克里奇招惹了他。我但愿他别把我同厄克里奇如此严丝合缝地捆作一处。总不能指望我去管束那个人吧。转念一想，从我在俱乐部同教授分手，到我在海滩上同他照面，统共不过几个钟头，他们俩多半无缘相会。厄克里奇轻易不离开农庄。不在鸡群中操劳的时候，他便仰面朝天躺在围场里，让那颗硕大的头脑歇息。

===Original===

I came to the conclusion that after all the professor had not seen me.

“I’m an idiot, Bob,” I said, as we turned in at the farm gate, “and I let my imagination run away with me.”

Bob wagged his tail in approval of the sentiment.

Breakfast was ready when I got in. There was a cold chicken on the sideboard, devilled chicken on the table, a trio of boiled eggs, and a dish of scrambled eggs. As regarded quantity Mrs. Beale never failed us.

===Chinese===

我于是得出结论：教授终究是没有瞧见我。

「我是个傻瓜，博布，」我们拐进农庄大门时我说，「净由着想象力脱缰撒野。」

博布摇了摇尾巴，对这一感想表示嘉许。

我进屋时早饭已经摆好。食柜上一只冷鸡，桌上一碟辣味烤鸡，白煮蛋三枚成局，另有炒蛋一盘。论分量，比尔太太从不曾亏待我们。

===Original===

Ukridge was sorting the letters.

“Morning, Garny,” he said. “One for you, Millie.”

“It’s from Aunt Elizabeth,” said Mrs. Ukridge, looking at the envelope.

I had only heard casual mention of this relative hitherto, but I had built up a mental picture of her partly from remarks which Ukridge had let fall, but principally from the fact that he had named the most malignant hen in our fowl-run after her. A severe lady, I imagined with a cold eye.

===Chinese===

厄克里奇正在分拣信件。

「早啊，加尼，」他说。「你一封，米莉。」

「伊丽莎白姨妈来的，」厄克里奇太太望着信封说。

这位亲眷，我先前只偶尔听人提起，心里却早给她画好了像：一半凭厄克里奇漏出的只言片语，更主要的，是凭一桩事实——我们鸡场里最凶悍的那只母鸡，便是照着她的名字命的名。我想象中那是一位严苛的太太，还生着一只冷冰冰的眼睛。

===Original===

“Wish she’d enclose a cheque,” said Ukridge. “She could spare it. You’ve no idea, Garny, old man, how disgustingly and indecently rich that woman is. She lives in Kensington on an income which would do her well in Park Lane. But as a touching proposition she has proved almost negligible. She steadfastly refuses to part.”

“I think she would, dear, if she knew how much we needed it. But I don’t like to ask her. She’s so curious, and says such horrid things.”

“She does,” agreed Ukridge, gloomily. He spoke as one who had had experience. “Two for you, Garny. All the rest for me. Ten of them, and all bills.”

He spread the envelopes out on the table, and drew one at a venture.

“Whiteley’s,” he said. “Getting jumpy. Are in receipt of my favour of the 7th inst., and are at a loss to understand. It’s rummy about these blighters, but they never seem able to understand a damn thing. It’s hard! You put things in words of one syllable for them, and they just goggle and wonder what it all means. They want something on account. Upon my Sam, I’m disappointed with Whiteley’s. I’d been thinking in rather a kindly spirit of them, and feeling that they were a more intelligent lot than Harrod’s. I’d had half a mind to give Harrod’s the miss-in-baulk and hand my whole trade over to these fellows. But not now, dash it! Whiteley’s have disappointed me. From the way they write, you’d think they thought I was doing it for fun. How can I let them have their infernal money when there isn’t any? Here’s one from Dorchester. Smith, the chap we got the gramophone from. Wants to know when I’m going to settle up for sixteen records.”

===Chinese===

「但愿她随信附张支票来，」厄克里奇说。「她拿得出来。加尼老兄，你简直想象不到，那女人阔得多么恶心、多么不成体统。她住在肯辛顿，那份进项搬到公园巷也尽够她过得滋润。可是论『感人』的掏钱前景，事实证明约等于零。她立场坚定，死也不肯松口。」

「亲爱的，她要是知道我们何等缺钱，我想她肯给的。可是我不愿意开口向她讨。她那人爱盘根问底，嘴又刻毒。」

「可不是，」厄克里奇郁郁地附和，口气俨然一位过来人。「你两封，加尼。其余全归我。十封，清一色账单。」

他把信封在桌上摊开，信手拈出一封。

「怀特利公司的。坐不住了。『兹接获本月七日大函，殊难明了。』这帮家伙就是这么邪门儿，他们好像连一件要命的事都闹不明白。真是没天理！你把话掰成一音一节喂到他们嘴边，他们只会干瞪眼珠子，寻思这一大套到底是啥意思。他们要一笔先付。我以我的萨姆起誓，怀特利公司真叫我寒心。我本来还对他们存着几分好意，觉得这拨人比哈罗德那伙明白点儿，都存了心思要给哈罗德来一记『故意脱杆』，把我全部买卖挪到这几位名下。可现在休提，见鬼！怀特利公司负了我。瞧他们写信这架势，你还当他们当我闹着玩儿呢。钱根儿全无，我怎么付他们那笔要命的钱？这封是多尔切斯特来的。史密斯，卖留声机给咱们的那个家伙。想知道我打算几时结清那十六张唱片。」

===Original===

“Sordid brute!”

I wanted to get on with my own correspondence, but Ukridge held me with a glittering eye.

“The chicken-men, the dealer people, you know, want me to pay for the first lot of hens. Considering that they all died of roop, and that I was going to send them back anyhow after I’d got them to hatch out a few chickens, I call that cool. I mean to say, business is business. That’s what these fellows don’t seem to understand. I can’t afford to pay enormous sums for birds which die off quicker than I can get them in.”

“I shall never speak to Aunt Elizabeth again,” said Mrs. Ukridge suddenly.

===Chinese===

「腌臜畜生！」

我本想接着料理自己的信件，无奈厄克里奇目光灼灼，把我牢牢摄在原地。

「贩鸡的商家，就是做鸡生意的那路人，你懂的，要我付头一批母鸡的钱。想想看，那批鸡无一幸免，全死于鸡疫，何况我原本打算等它们替我孵出几窝小鸡之后统统退货，这份要求未免脸皮太厚。我是说，生意归生意。这帮家伙偏偏不懂这个道理。要我拿出大笔大笔的钞票，去买那些死得比进货还快的鸡，我可供养不起。」

「我再也不理伊丽莎白姨妈了，」厄克里奇太太蓦地说。

===Original===

She had dropped the letter she had been reading, and was staring indignantly in front of her. There were two little red spots on her cheeks.

“What’s the matter, old chap?” inquired Ukridge affectionately, glancing up from his pile of bills and forgetting his own troubles in an instant. “Buck up! Aunt Elizabeth been getting on your nerves again? What’s she been saying this time?”

Mrs. Ukridge left the room with a sob. Ukridge sprang at the letter.

“If that demon doesn’t stop writing her infernal letters and upsetting Millie, I shall strangle her with my bare hands, regardless of her age and sex.” He turned over the pages of the letter till he came to the passage which had caused the trouble. “Well, upon my Sam! Listen to this, Garny, old horse. ‘You tell me nothing regarding the success of this chicken farm of yours, and I confess that I find your silence ominous. You know my opinion of your husband. He is perfectly helpless in any matter requiring the exercise of a little common sense and business capability.’ ” He stared at me, amazed. “I like that! ’Pon my soul, that is really rich! I could have believed almost anything of that blighted female, but I did think she had a reasonable amount of intelligence. Why, you know that it’s just in matters requiring common sense and business capability that I come out really strong.”

===Chinese===

她把手里的信跌落在地，愤然直视前方，腮帮上浮起两小团红晕。

「怎么了，老伙计？」厄克里奇满怀慈爱地探问，从他那摞账单上抬起眼来，刹那间把自己的烦恼忘了个干净。「振作点儿！又是伊丽莎白姨妈搅了你的心？这回她说了些什么？」

厄克里奇太太噙着一声抽泣离屋而去。厄克里奇一个箭步扑向那封信。

「那老妖婆再敢给她写混账信、再敢招米莉伤心，我就凭这双赤手空拳掐死她，年纪性别一概不论。」他把信纸一页页翻过去，翻到闯祸的那一段。「好哇，我以我的萨姆起誓！听听这段，加尼老马。『你对令夫这座养鸡场兴旺与否只字不提，恕我直言，你的沉默令人生疑。你知道我对令夫的看法。凡遇需要些许常识与办事才干之事，他一概束手无策。』」他瞪住我，骇异万分。「听听这个！老天在上，这话可真够味儿！那个可恶的女人，我几乎什么都肯信她干得出来，唯独以为她还剩几分理智。咦，你不是不知道，恰恰在需要常识与办事才干的地方，才数我大放异彩。」

===Original===

“Of course, old man,” I replied dutifully. “The woman’s a fool.”

“That’s what she calls me two lines further on. No wonder Millie was upset. Why can’t these cats leave people alone?”

“Oh, woman, woman!” I threw in helpfully.

“Always interfering﻿—”

“Rotten!”

===Chinese===

「可不是，老兄，」我恭顺地应道。「这女人是个糊涂蛋。」

「两行之后她就这么骂我了。难怪米莉伤心。这帮长舌婆就不能容人清静清静么？」

「唉，女人啊，女人！」我在一旁凑趣帮腔。

「横竖要插一杠子——」

「讨厌！」

===Original===

“And backbiting﻿—”

“Awful!”

“I shan’t stand it.”

“I shouldn’t!”

===Chinese===

「背后嚼舌根——」

「可恨！」

「我忍无可忍了。」

「换我也忍无可忍！」

===Original===

“Look here! On the next page she calls me a gaby!”

“It’s time you took a strong line.”

“And in the very next sentence refers to me as a perfect guffin. What’s a guffin, Garny, old boy?”

I considered the point.

“Broadly speaking, I should say, one who guffs.”

===Chinese===

「你瞧这儿！下一页她管我叫傻瓜！」

「你早该拿出强硬手段了。」

「紧挨着的下一句，又把我称作一个十足的咕芬。咕芬是个什么，加尼老弟？」

我把这个问题掂量了一番。

「泛泛而论，依我看，大概是干咕芬这一行的人吧。」

===Original===

“I believe it’s actionable.”

“I shouldn’t wonder.”

Ukridge rushed to the door.

“Millie!”

===Chinese===

「我看这话够得上诽谤罪。」

「说不定真够。」

厄克里奇风也似的冲向门口。

「米莉！」

===Original===

He slammed the door, and I heard him dashing upstairs.

I turned to my letters. One was from Lickford, with a Cornish postmark. I glanced through it and laid it aside for a more exhaustive perusal.

The other was in a strange handwriting. I looked at the signature. “Patrick Derrick.” This was queer. What had the professor to say to me?

The next moment my heart seemed to spring to my throat.

===Chinese===

他砰地带上门，我听见他蹬蹬蹬奔上楼去。

我转而料理自己的信。一封是利克福德来的，盖着康沃尔的邮戳。我浏览一遍，搁在一旁，留待日后细读。

另一封笔迹陌生。我看落款。「帕特里克·德里克」。这就怪了。教授有什么话要对我说？

下一刻，我的心仿佛一下子蹿到了喉咙口。

===Original===

“Sir,” the letter began.

A pleasant cheery opening!

Then it got off the mark, so to speak, like lightning. There was no sparring for an opening, no dignified parade of set phrases, leading up to the main point. It was the letter of a man who was almost too furious to write. It gave me the impression that, if he had not written it, he would have been obliged to have taken some very violent form of exercise by way of relief to his soul.

“You will be good enough to look on our acquaintance as closed. I have no wish to associate with persons of your stamp. If we should happen to meet, you will be good enough to treat me as a total stranger, as I shall treat you. And, if I may be allowed to give you a word of advice, I should recommend you in future, when you wish to exercise your humour, to do so in some less practical manner than by bribing boatmen to upset your﻿—(*friends* crossed out thickly, and *acquaintances* substituted). If you require further enlightenment in this matter, the enclosed letter may be of service to you.”

===Chinese===

「先生：」信是这样起头的。

好一个亲切爽利的开场！

接着，不妨这么说，它如闪电般冲出了起跑器。没有虚晃试探的开局，没有一整套道貌岸然的套话层层铺陈、引向正题。这是一个怒得几乎握不住笔的人写来的信。它给我的印象是：他若不写这封信，就只好去做某种异常剧烈的运动，好教灵魂透一口气。

「我们的交往，就此一笔勾销，望你知悉。我无意与你这路人相与。倘若狭路相逢，请你全然当我是陌路，我也自当全然当你是陌路。再者，若蒙俯允，容我进一言：日后阁下若要施展幽默，奉劝挑一个实操性稍逊的路数，别再贿赂船夫去掀翻你的——（*朋友*一词被浓浓划去，改书*泛泛之交*）。此事如需进一步点拨，随信附上的那封信或可效劳。」

===Original===

With which he remained mine faithfully, Patrick Derrick.

The enclosed letter was from one Jane Muspratt. It was bright and interesting.

> “Dear Sir,﻿—My Harry, Mr. Hawk, sas to me how it was him upsetting the boat and you, not because he is not steady in a boat which he is no man more so in Combe Regis, but because one of the gentmen what keeps chikkens up the hill, the little one, Mr. Garnick his name is, says to him, Hawk, I’ll give you a sovrin to upset Mr. Derick in your boat, and my Harry being esily led was took in and did, but he’s sory now and wishes he hadn’t, and he sas he’ll niver do a prackticle joke again for anyone even for a banknote.﻿—Yours obedly.,

> “Jane Muspratt.”

===Chinese===

说了这许多，他到底依旧「忠实于我」——帕特里克·德里克。

附来的那封信，出自某位简·马斯普拉特之手。写得活泼，有趣。

> 「敬爱的先生：——俺家哈里，霍克先生，跟俺说，把船弄翻、把您老撂进水里的是他，倒不是因为他坐船不稳当——论稳当，库姆里吉斯没一个爷们儿比得上他——只是因为山上养鸡的几位老爷里头，个头小的那一位，名字叫加尼克先生，跟他说：霍克，我给你一枚金镑，叫你把德里克先生从船上翻下去。俺家哈里这人耳朵根子软，一哄就上了当，就翻了。可他这会儿懊悔啦，但愿没干过才好。他说往后谁也别想再哄他干恶作剧了，哪怕给张钞票也不干。——您恭顺的仆人，

> 「简·马斯普拉特。」

===Original===

Oh, woman, woman!

At the bottom of everything! History is full of tragedies caused by the lethal sex. Who lost Mark Antony the world? A woman. Who let Samson in so atrociously? Woman again. Why did Bill Bailey leave home? Once more, because of a woman. And here was I, Jerry Garnet, harmless, well-meaning writer of minor novels, going through the same old mill.

I cursed Jane Muspratt. What chance had I with Phyllis now? Could I hope to win over the professor again? I cursed Jane Muspratt for the second time.

My thoughts wandered to Mr. Harry Hawk. The villain! The scoundrel! What business had he to betray me?﻿ ﻿… Well, I could settle with him. The man who lays a hand upon a woman, save in the way of kindness, is justly disliked by Society; so the woman Muspratt, culpable as she was, was safe from me. But what of the man Hawk? There no such considerations swayed me. I would interview the man Hawk. I would give him the most hectic ten minutes of his career. I would say things to him the recollection of which would make him start up shrieking in his bed in the small hours of the night. I would arise, and be a man, and slay him; take him grossly, full of bread, with all his crimes broad-blown, as flush as May; at gaming, swearing, or about some act that had no relish of salvation in it.

===Chinese===

噢，女人啊，女人！

万事的风波底下都有她们！史册里满是被这致命性别酿成的悲剧。谁把整个世界从马克·安东尼手里断送了？一个女人。谁把参孙坑得那样惨？又是女人。比尔·贝利为什么离家出走？还是因为一个女人。而今轮到了我，杰里米·加尼特——一个与人无争、宅心仁厚的末流小说家——被送进同一盘老磨里碾。

我咒骂简·马斯普拉特。如今我同菲莉斯还有什么指望？我还能指望重新博得教授的好感么？我第二次咒骂简·马斯普拉特。

我的思绪飘到了哈里·霍克先生身上。恶棍！无赖！他有什么脸出卖我？……好吧，这笔账我同他算得清。凡对女人动手者——除非出于善意——理应见弃于上流社会；所以那位马斯普拉特家的女人，纵然罪在不赦，在我这里却是安全的。可霍克其人呢？对付他，我全无这等顾忌。我要去会一会这个霍克。我要赏他此生最滚烫的十分钟。我要对他发表一席话，教他后半夜从床上惊坐起来、尖声怪叫的，正是这席话的余音。我要奋起，做个男子汉，宰了他；趁他大腹便便、酒足饭饱、满身罪孽开得正艳、红得赛过五月之际将他拿下；趁他赌钱、赌咒，或正干着毫无得救滋味勾当的当口。

===Original===

The Demon!

My life﻿—ruined. My future﻿—grey and black. My heart﻿—shattered. And why? Because of the scoundrel, Hawk.

Phyllis would meet me in the village, on the Cob, on the links, and pass by as if I were the Invisible Man. And why? Because of the reptile, Hawk. The worm, Hawk. The dastard and varlet, Hawk.

I crammed my hat on, and hurried out of the house towards the village.

===Chinese===

恶魔！

我的一生——毁了。我的前途——灰黑。我的心——碎了。为什么？因为那个恶棍，霍克。

菲莉斯会在村里、在科布防波堤上、在高尔夫球场上遇见我，然后擦身而过，仿佛我是隐形人。为什么？因为那条爬虫，霍克。那条蛆虫，霍克。那个懦夫兼恶棍，霍克。

我把帽子往头上一扣，冲出屋去，直奔村子。

## IV The Arrival / 四 抵达

===Original===

From Axminster to Combe Regis the line runs through country as attractive as any that can be found in the island, and the train, as if in appreciation of this fact, does not hurry over the journey. It was late afternoon by the time we reached our destination.

The arrangements for the carrying of luggage at Combe Regis border on the primitive. Boxes are left on the platform, and later, when he thinks of it, a carrier looks in and conveys them into the valley and up the hill on the opposite side to the address written on the labels. The owner walks. Combe Regis is not a place for the halt and maimed.

Ukridge led us in the direction of the farm, which lay across the valley, looking through woods to the sea. The place was visible from the station, from which, indeed, standing as it did on the top of a hill, the view was extensive.

Halfway up the slope on the other side of the valley we left the road and made our way across a spongy field, Ukridge explaining that this was a shortcut. We climbed through a hedge, crossed a stream and another field, and after negotiating a difficult bank, topped with barbed wire, found ourselves in a garden.

===Chinese===

从阿克斯明斯特到库姆里吉斯，铁路一路穿行于全岛数一数二的美景之间，而那趟火车，仿佛也懂得欣赏这份景致，一路不慌不忙。抵达目的地时，已近黄昏。

库姆里吉斯的行李搬运设施，近乎原始。箱子撂在站台上，过些时候，等搬运工想起来了，才来照看一眼，把它们运进谷地、拉上对面山坡，送到行李标签上写的地址。物主本人则靠步行。库姆里吉斯可不是腿脚不便、身带伤残之辈待的地方。

厄克里奇领着我们朝农庄走去。农庄坐落在谷地对岸，穿过树林便可望见大海。从车站就能望见那地方；车站自己立在小山顶上，视野也确实开阔。

爬对面山坡爬到一半，我们离开大路，蹚过一片湿海绵似的草地——厄克里奇解释说这是条近道。我们钻过一道树篱，蹚过一条小溪、又一片田，翻过一道顶上拉着带刺铁丝网的陡坎，便进了一座园子。

===Original===

Ukridge mopped his forehead, and restored his pince-nez to their original position from which the passage of the barbed wire had dislodged them.

“This is the place,” he said. “We’ve come in by the back way. Saves time. Tired, Millie?”

“A little, dear. I should like some tea.”

“Same here,” I agreed.

“That’ll be all right,” said Ukridge. “A most competent man of the name of Beale and his wife are in charge at present. I wrote to them telling them that we were coming today. They will be ready for us. That’s the way to do things, Garny old horse. Quiet efficiency. Perfect organisation.”

===Chinese===

厄克里奇抹了抹额头，把夹鼻眼镜扶回原位——过铁丝网那一路，早把它碰歪了。

「就是这儿了，」他说。「咱们走的是后门。省时间。累了吧，米莉？」

「有点儿，亲爱的。我想喝杯茶。」

「我也是，」我附和。

「这好办，」厄克里奇说。「眼下有个姓比尔的家伙和他老婆管着这地方，人是极能干的。我写过信告诉他们咱们今天到。他们准会候着咱们。办事就得这么办，加尼老马。不动声色的效率。尽善尽美的组织。」

===Original===

We were at the front door by this time. Ukridge rang the bell. The noise echoed through the house, but there were no answering footsteps. He rang again. There is no mistaking the note of a bell in an empty house. It was plain that the competent man and his wife were out.

“Now what?” I said.

Mrs. Ukridge looked at her husband with calm confidence.

“This,” said Ukridge, leaning against the door and endeavouring to button his collar at the back, “reminds me of an afternoon in the Argentine. Two other cheery sportsmen and myself tried for three-quarters of an hour to get into an empty house where there looked as if there might be something to drink, and we’d just got the door open when the owner turned up from behind a tree with a shotgun. It was a little difficult to explain. As a matter of fact, we never did what you might call really thresh the matter out thoroughly in all its aspects, and you’d be surprised what a devil of a time it takes to pick buckshot out of a fellow. There was a dog, too.”

===Chinese===

这时我们已到正门前。厄克里奇按铃。铃声在屋子里回荡，却无人应门。他又按了一遍。空屋里门铃的动静是错不了的。显然，那位能干的家伙和他老婆都出去了。

「这下怎么办？」我说。

厄克里奇太太神色镇定，满有把握地望着丈夫。

「这情形，」厄克里奇倚着门，努力去够后领的扣子，「叫我想起阿根廷的一个下午。另外两位兴致勃勃的运动家和我本人，花了三刻钟想钻进一所空房子——那里头看着像是有酒可喝——门刚弄开，主人就从一棵树后面端着猎枪转出来了。那可有点儿难解释。事实上，这件事你所谓的方方面面，我们到底也没能彻底掰扯清楚；而且从一个伙计身上起出鹿弹要费多大劲，你准会吓一跳。还有一条狗来着。」

===Original===

He broke off, musing dreamily on the happy past, and at this moment history partially repeated itself. From the other side of the door came a dissatisfied whine, followed by a short bark.

“Hullo,” said Ukridge, “Beale has a dog.” He frowned, annoyed. “What right,” he added in an aggrieved tone, “has a beastly mongrel, belonging to a man I employ, to keep me out of my own house? It’s a little hard. Here am I, slaving day and night to support Beale, and when I try to get into my own house his infernal dog barks at me. Upon my Sam it’s hard!” He brooded for a moment on the injustice of things. “Here, let me get to the keyhole. I’ll reason with the brute.”

He put his mouth to the keyhole and roared “Goo’ dog!” through it. Instantly the door shook as some heavy object hurled itself against it. The barking rang through the house.

“Come round to the back,” said Ukridge, giving up the idea of conciliation, “we’ll get in through the kitchen window.”

The kitchen window proved to be insecurely latched. Ukridge threw it open and we climbed in. The dog, hearing the noise, raced back along the passage and flung himself at the door, scratching at the panels. Ukridge listened with growing indignation.

===Chinese===

他收住话头，悠悠然回味着那段好时光；也就在这时，历史局部重演了。门那头传来一声不满的呜咽，接着是短促的一吠。

「嚯，」厄克里奇说，「比尔养了条狗。」他皱起眉，大为光火。「凭什么，」他又委屈不平地添了一句，「我雇的人养的破杂种狗，凭什么拦着不让我进自家的房子？这就有点儿说不过去了。我在这儿日夜操劳养活比尔，等我进了自家的大门，他那条要命的狗倒冲我叫。我以我的萨姆起誓，真没天理！」他就世道的不公寻思了片刻。「来，让我凑到锁眼跟前。我去跟这畜生讲讲道理。」

他把嘴凑上锁眼，冲里头吼了一声「好狗狗！」门板登时一震，有个沉甸甸的东西朝它撞了上来。狗吠声响彻全屋。

「绕到后头去，」厄克里奇说，断然放弃了讲和的念头，「咱们从厨房窗户进去。」

厨房窗户的搭扣没扣牢。厄克里奇一把推开，我们爬了进去。那狗听见动静，顺着过道一路奔回，扑在门上，抓挠门板。厄克里奇听着，火气节节上升。

===Original===

“Millie, you know how to light a fire. Garnet and I will be collecting cups and things. When that scoundrel Beale arrives I shall tear him limb from limb. Deserting us like this! The man must be a thorough fraud. He told me he was an old soldier. If that’s the sort of discipline they used to keep in his regiment, thank God we’ve got a Navy! Damn, I’ve broken a plate. How’s the fire getting on, Millie? I’ll chop Beale into little bits. What’s that you’ve got there, Garny old horse? Tea? Good. Where’s the bread? There goes another plate. Where’s Mrs. Beale, too? By Jove, that woman wants killing as much as her blackguard of a husband. Whoever heard of a cook deliberately leaving her post on the day when her master and mistress were expected back? The abandoned woman. Look here, I’ll give that dog three minutes, and if it doesn’t stop scratching that door by then, I’ll take a rolling pin and go out and have a heart-to-heart talk with it. It’s a little hard. My own house, and the first thing I find when I arrive is somebody else’s beastly dog scratching holes in the doors and ruining the expensive paint. Stop it, you brute!”

The dog’s reply was to continue his operations with immense vigour.

Ukridge’s eyes gleamed behind their glasses.

“Give me a good large jug, laddie,” he said with ominous calm.

===Chinese===

「米莉，你会生火。加尼特和我去张罗茶杯什么的一阵。等那个恶棍比尔回来，我要把他撕成碎块。竟这么样扔下咱们不管！这家伙准是个彻头彻尾的骗子。他跟我说他当过兵。要是他们团队里平时就这种军纪，感谢上帝咱们还有海军！该死，我碰碎了一只盘子。火生得怎么样了，米莉？我要把比尔剁成肉丁。你手里拿的什么，加尼老马？茶叶？好。面包在哪儿？又去了一只盘子。比尔太太又上哪儿去了？老天，那女人跟她那流氓丈夫一样，也欠收拾。谁听说过有厨子专挑主人夫妇要回来的日子擅离职守？这不要脸的女人。听着，我给那条狗三分钟，到时它要是还挠那扇门，我就抄起擀面杖出去，同它开诚布公地谈一谈。这就有点儿说不过去了。自家的房子，进门头一件事，就是别人家的臭狗在门上抓洞，糟蹋昂贵的油漆。住手，你这畜生！」

那狗的回答，是愈发卖力地继续它的事业。

厄克里奇的眼镜片后头，目光炯炯。

「给我拿只趁手的大水壶来，小老弟，」他说，语调平静得不祥。

===Original===

He took the largest of the jugs from the dresser and strode with it into the scullery, whence came a sound of running water. He returned carrying the jug with both hands, his mien that of a general who sees his way to a masterstroke of strategy.

“Garny, old horse,” he said, “freeze onto the handle of the door, and, when I give the word, fling wide the gates. Then watch that animal get the surprise of a lifetime.”

I attached myself to the handle as directed. Ukridge gave the word. We had a momentary vision of an excited dog of the mongrel class framed in the open doorway, all eyes and teeth; then the passage was occupied by a spreading pool, and indignant barks from the distance told that the enemy was thinking the thing over in some safe retreat.

“Settled *his* hash,” said Ukridge complacently. “Nothing like resource, Garny my boy. Some men would have gone on letting a good door be ruined.”

===Chinese===

他从餐具柜上挑了最大的一只水壶，大步流星进了那间传出自来水声的洗涤室，回来时双手捧壶，那气度俨然一位成竹在胸、正要下一着妙手的将军。

「加尼老马，」他说，「攥住门把手，我一发话，你就城门大开。然后瞧那畜生领受它一辈子头一遭的惊喜。」

我遵命攥住门把手。厄克里奇一声令下。我们惊鸿一瞥，只见敞开的门框里嵌着一条激动的杂种狗，满眼满牙；随即过道里便铺开一泓不断扩张的水洼，远处愤愤的犬吠声报告说，敌军已在某个安全的退避所里从长计议了。

「摆平*他*了，」厄克里奇扬扬自得。「就是要有急智，加尼我的好兄弟。换了别人，准由着好好一扇门给毁了。」

===Original===

“And spoiled the dog for a ha’porth of water,” I said.

At this moment Mrs. Ukridge announced that the kettle was boiling. Over a cup of tea Ukridge became the man of business.

“I wonder when those fowls are going to arrive. They should have been here today. It’s a little hard. Here am I, all eagerness and anxiety, waiting to start an up-to-date chicken farm, and no fowls! I can’t run a chicken farm without fowls. If they don’t come tomorrow, I shall get after those people with a hatchet. There must be no slackness. They must bustle about. After tea I’ll show you the garden, and we’ll choose a place for a fowl-run. Tomorrow we must buckle to. Serious work will begin immediately after breakfast.”

“Suppose,” I said, “the fowls arrive before we’re ready for them?”

===Chinese===

「还只花了半便士的水，」我说。

就在这时，厄克里奇太太宣布水壶开了。一杯茶下肚，厄克里奇进入了生意人的状态。

「不知道那批鸡几时才到。本该今天到的。这就有点儿说不过去了。瞧我，满怀热望、满心焦虑，就等着开办一座最新式的养鸡场，偏偏没有鸡！没有鸡我办什么养鸡场？明天再不到，我就提着短斧去找那帮人算账。松松垮垮可不行。他们得振作起来。喝完茶我领你们看园子，咱们挑块地方做鸡圈。明天就得撸起袖子大干。正经活儿，早饭后立刻开始。」

「万一，」我说，「鸡在咱们准备好之前先到了呢？」

===Original===

“Why, then they must wait.”

“But you can’t keep fowls cooped up indefinitely in a crate.”

“Oh, that’ll be all right. There’s a basement to this house. We’ll let ’em run about there till we’re ready for them. There’s always a way of doing things if you look for it. Organisation, my boy. That’s the watchword. Quiet efficiency.”

“I hope you are going to let the hens hatch some of the eggs, dear,” said Mrs. Ukridge. “I should love to have some little chickens.”

“Of course. By all means. My idea,” said Ukridge, “was this. These people will send us fifty fowls of sorts. That means﻿—call it forty-five eggs a day. Let ’em﻿ ﻿… Well, I’m hanged! There’s that dog again. Where’s the jug?”

===Chinese===

「那它们就等着呗。」

「可你总不能让鸡在板条箱里无限期地关下去。」

「嗐，那好办。这房子有个地下室。先放它们在里头跑着，等咱们腾出手来。只要肯动脑筋，天下的办法总归是有的。组织，我的老弟。这就是口号。不动声色的效率。」

「亲爱的，我想你是打算让母鸡孵几只蛋的吧，」厄克里奇太太说。「我可爱看小鸡崽儿了。」

「当然。务必。我的想法，」厄克里奇说，「是这样的。这帮人会送来五十只杂七杂八的鸡。这就意味着——就算一天四十五只蛋吧。让它们……咦，见鬼！那狗又来了。水壶呢？」

===Original===

But this time an unforeseen interruption prevented the manoeuvre being the success it had been before. I had turned the handle and was about to pull the door open, while Ukridge, looking like some modern and dilapidated version of the *Discobolus*, stood beside me with his jug poised, when a voice spoke from the window.

“Stand still!” said the voice, “or I’ll corpse you!”

I dropped the handle. Ukridge dropped the jug. Mrs. Ukridge dropped her teacup. At the window, with a double-barrelled gun in his hands stood a short, square, redheaded man. The muzzle of his gun, which rested on the sill, was pointing in a straight line at the third button of my waistcoat.

Ukridge emitted a roar like that of a hungry lion.

===Chinese===

但这一回，天有不测，那套战术没能重奏凯歌。我刚拧开门把手，正要拉门；厄克里奇捧壶待发站在我旁边，活像一尊现代破落版的*掷铁饼者*；冷不防窗口有人开腔了。

「站住别动！」那声音说，「不然我叫你变死尸！」

我松了门把手。厄克里奇撒了水壶。厄克里奇太太跌了茶杯。窗口立着一个矮壮的红头发汉子，双手端着一杆双筒猎枪。枪口架在窗台上，笔直指着我马甲的第三颗纽扣。

厄克里奇发出一声饿狮般的怒吼。

===Original===

“Beale! You scoundrelly, unprincipled, demon! What the devil are you doing with that gun? Why were you out? What have you been doing? Why did you shout like that? Look what you’ve made me do.”

He pointed to the floor. The very old pair of tennis shoes which he wore were by this time generously soaked with the spilled water.

“Lor, Mr. Ukridge, sir, is that you?” said the redheaded man calmly. “I thought you was burglars.”

A short bark from the other side of the kitchen door, followed by a renewal of the scratching, drew Mr. Beale’s attention to his faithful hound.

===Chinese===

「比尔！你这恶棍、无赖、恶魔！你端着枪搞什么名堂？为什么不在家？你干什么去了？干吗那样大呼小叫？瞧你害我干的好事。」

他指着地板。他脚上那双老旧的网球鞋，这会儿已经被泼出来的水浸了个透。

「嗳哟，厄克里奇先生，是您哪？」红头发汉子不动声色地说。「我当是贼呢。」

厨房门那头一声短吠，接着抓挠声再起，把比尔先生的注意力引向他的爱犬。

===Original===

“That’s Bob,” he said.

“I don’t know what you call the brute,” said Ukridge. “Come in and tie him up. And mind what you’re doing with that gun. After you’ve finished with the dog, I should like a brief chat with you, laddie, if you can spare the time and have no other engagements.”

Mr. Beale, having carefully deposited the gun against the wall and dropped a pair of very limp rabbits on the floor, proceeded to climb in through the window. This operation concluded, he stood to one side while the besieged garrison passed out by the same route.

“You will find me in the garden,” said Ukridge coldly. “I’ve one or two little things to say to you.”

Mr. Beale grinned affably. He seemed to be a man of equable temperament.

===Chinese===

「那是博布，」他说。

「随你叫那畜生什么，」厄克里奇说。「进来把它拴上。还有，你那杆枪放规矩点。你收拾完狗，我要同你简短地聊聊，小老弟——要是你抽得出空、又没旁的约会的话。」

比尔先生把猎枪小心靠墙立好，又把一对软塌塌的兔子撂在地上，便从窗口爬了进来。这套动作完毕，他闪身一旁，被围困的守军便循原路撤了出去。

「到园子里来见我，」厄克里奇冷冷地说。「我有一两件小事要同你讲。」

比尔先生咧嘴一笑，和和气气。看样子是个好性子的人。

===Original===

The cool air of the garden was grateful after the warmth of the kitchen. It was a pretty garden, or would have been if it had not been so neglected. I seemed to see myself sitting in a deck-chair on the lawn, smoking and looking through the trees at the harbour below. It was a spot, I felt, in which it would be an easy and a pleasant task to shape the plot of my novel. I was glad I had come. About now, outside my lodgings in town, a particularly foul barrel-organ would be settling down to work.

“Oh, there you are, Beale,” said Ukridge, as the servitor appeared. “Now then, what have you to say?”

The hired man looked thoughtful for a moment, then said that it was a fine evening.

“Fine evening?” shouted Ukridge. “What on earth has that got to do with it? I want to know why you and Mrs. Beale were out when we arrived.”

===Chinese===

从暖烘烘的厨房出来，园子里凉爽的空气宜人。园子挺漂亮——倘若不是荒疏了这么久的话。我仿佛已经看见自己坐在草坪的帆布躺椅上，抽着烟，透过树影望底下的海港。我觉得，在这地方为我小说构思情节，必是一桩又省力又惬意的差事。来的这一步走对了。算算时辰，此刻城里我寓所门外，那架特别可憎的手摇风琴正要开始它的营业呢。

「哦，你来啦，比尔，」那雇工露面时厄克里奇说。「那么，你有什么说的？」

那雇工沉吟片刻，说今晚天气不错。

「不错的天气？」厄克里奇吼道。「这跟天有什么相干！我要知道的是，我们到的时候，你同比尔太太为什么不在家。」

===Original===

“The missus went to Axminster, Mr. Ukridge, sir.”

“She had no right to go to Axminster. It isn’t part of her duties to go gadding about to Axminster. I don’t pay her enormous sums to go to Axminster. You knew I was coming this evening.”

“No, sir.”

“What!”

“No, sir.”

“Beale,” said Ukridge with studied calm, the strong man repressing himself. “One of us two is a fool.”

===Chinese===

「老板娘去阿克斯明斯特了，厄克里奇先生，您哪。」

「她无权去阿克斯明斯特。逛到阿克斯明斯特去不在她的职责之内。我付她大笔大笔的工钱，不是叫她去阿克斯明斯特闲逛的。你知道我今天晚上到。」

「不知道，您哪。」

「什么！」

「不知道，您哪。」

「比尔，」厄克里奇字斟句酌，强自平静，一条硬汉正克制着自己。「咱们两个，总有一个是傻瓜。」

===Original===

“Yes, sir.”

“Let us sift this matter to the bottom. You got my letter?”

“No, sir.”

“My letter saying that I should arrive today. You didn’t get it?”

“No, sir.”

===Chinese===

「是，您哪。」

「咱们把这件事刨到根儿。接到我的信了么？」

「没有，您哪。」

「我那封信，说我今天到的。你没接到？」

「没有，您哪。」

===Original===

“Now, look here, Beale, this is absurd. I am certain that that letter was posted. I remember placing it in my pocket for that purpose. It is not there now. See. These are all the contents of my﻿—well, I’m hanged.”

He stood looking at the envelope which he had produced from his breast-pocket. A soft smile played over Mr. Beale’s wooden face. He coughed.

“Beale,” said Ukridge, “you﻿—er﻿—there seems to have been a mistake.”

“Yes, sir.”

“You are not so much to blame as I thought.”

===Chinese===

「你听着，比尔，这可太荒唐了。我敢肯定那封信寄出去了。我记得清清楚楚，我把它放进衣袋，专为寄信来着。如今它不在了。瞧。我这衣袋里的东西全在这儿——咦，见鬼。」

他手举着从胸前口袋里摸出的那只信封，呆呆立着。比尔先生那张木头脸上掠过一丝温柔的笑意。他咳了一声。

「比尔，」厄克里奇说，「你——呃——看来是出了点儿岔子。」

「是，您哪。」

「你的罪过没有我原先想的那么重。」

===Original===

“No, sir.”

There was a silence.

“Anyhow,” said Ukridge in inspired tones, “I’ll go and slay that infernal dog. I’ll teach him to tear my door to pieces. Where’s your gun, Beale?”

But better counsels prevailed, and the proceedings closed with a cold but pleasant little dinner, at which the spared mongrel came out unexpectedly strong with ingenious and diverting tricks.

===Chinese===

「是，您哪。」

一阵沉默。

「不管怎么说，」厄克里奇以受了神启的声调说，「我得去宰了那条要命的狗。得教教它怎么拆我的门。你的枪呢，比尔？」

但理性终究占了上风。这场风波以一顿冷清却愉快的小晚饭收了场；席间，那条死里逃生的杂种狗大显身手，耍出了好些别出心裁、逗人开怀的把戏。

## XI The Brave Preserver / 十一 勇敢的救星

===Original===

I could have wished, during the next few days, that Mr. Harry Hawk’s attitude towards myself had not been so unctuously confidential and mysterious. It was unnecessary, in my opinion, for him to grin meaningly when he met me in the street. His sly wink when we passed each other on the Cob struck me as in indifferent taste. The thing had been definitely arranged (ten shillings down and ten when it was over), and there was no need for any cloak and dark-lantern effects. I objected strongly to being treated as the villain of a melodrama. I was merely an ordinary well-meaning man, forced by circumstances into doing the work of Providence. Mr. Hawk’s demeanour seemed to say, “We are two reckless scoundrels, but bless you, *I* won’t give away your guilty secret.” The climax came one morning as I was going along the street towards the beach. I was passing a dark doorway, when out shimmered Mr. Hawk as if he had been a spectre instead of the most substantial man within a radius of ten miles.

“ ’St!” he whispered.

“Now look here, Hawk,” I said wrathfully, for the start he had given me had made me bite my tongue, “this has got to stop. I refuse to be haunted in this way. What is it now?”

“Mr. Derrick goes out this morning, zur.”

===Chinese===

接下来那几天，我倒宁愿哈里·霍克先生对我的态度别那么油腻腻的知心、那么神神秘秘。依我看，他在街上碰见我就意味深长地咧嘴一笑，大可不必。我们在科布防波堤上错身而过时他狡黠地眨眼，我认为品味实在不敢恭维。事情早已敲定（先付十先令，完事再付十先令），用不着来什么斗篷加暗灯那一套戏法。我强烈抗议被人当成情节剧里的反派。我不过是个普普通通与人为善的人，被时势所迫，代行上天的工作罢了。霍克先生那副做派仿佛在说：「咱们俩是一对无法无天的恶棍，可是放心，*我*绝不会出卖你那桩罪恶的秘密。」登峰造极的是一天早晨，我正沿着街往海滩去。路过一处幽暗的门洞，霍克先生幽幽地闪了出来，倒好像他是一缕冤魂，而不是方圆十英里内最敦实的一条汉子。

「嘘！」他悄声道。

「我说，霍克，」我怒气冲冲地说，他这一吓害我咬了舌头，「这种事得有个完。我拒绝这样被鬼魂缠身。这回又怎么了？」

「德里克先生今儿早上要出海，您哪。」

===Original===

“Thank goodness for that,” I said. “Get it over this morning, then, without fail. I couldn’t stand another day of it.”

I went on to the Cob, where I sat down. I was excited. Deeds of great import must shortly be done. I felt a little nervous. It would never do to bungle the thing. Suppose by some accident I were to drown the professor! Or suppose that, after all, he contented himself with a mere formal expression of thanks, and refused to let bygones be bygones. These things did not bear thinking of.

I got up and began to pace restlessly to and fro.

Presently from the farther end of the harbour there put off Mr. Hawk’s boat, bearing its precious cargo. My mouth became dry with excitement.

===Chinese===

「谢天谢地，」我说。「那就务必要在今儿上午办利索。再来这么一天我可受不了。」

我继续走到科布防波堤，坐下。我心里兴奋。惊天动地的大事业顷刻就要上演。我觉得有点儿紧张。这事万万砸不得。万一不留神，我把教授淹死了怎么办！又或者，到头来他只干巴巴致一句谢，偏不肯尽释前嫌，又怎么办。这些事，想都不敢想。

我站起来，焦躁地来回踱步。

不多时，海港远处驶出了霍克先生的船，载着它贵重的货物。我兴奋得口干舌燥。

===Original===

Very slowly Mr. Hawk pulled round the end of the Cob, coming to a standstill some dozen yards from where I was performing my beat. It was evidently here that the scene of the gallant rescue had been fixed.

My eyes were glued upon Mr. Hawk’s broad back. Only when going in to bat at cricket have I experienced a similar feeling of suspense. The boat lay almost motionless on the water. I had never seen the sea smoother. Little ripples plashed against the side of the Cob.

It seemed as if this perfect calm might continue forever. Mr. Hawk made no movement. Then suddenly the whole scene changed to one of vast activity. I heard Mr. Hawk utter a hoarse cry, and saw him plunge violently in his seat. The professor turned half round, and I caught sight of his indignant face, pink with emotion. Then the scene changed again with the rapidity of a dissolving view. I saw Mr. Hawk give another plunge, and the next moment the boat was upside down in the water, and I was shooting headforemost to the bottom, oppressed with the indescribably clammy sensation which comes when one’s clothes are thoroughly wet.

I rose to the surface close to the upturned boat. The first sight I saw was the spluttering face of Mr. Hawk. I ignored him, and swam to where the professor’s head bobbed on the waters.

===Chinese===

霍克先生慢吞吞地把船划过防波堤尽头，在我来回巡行的地段十几码外停住了。英勇救人的现场，显然就定在了这里。

我的眼睛牢牢粘在霍克先生宽阔的脊背上。只有在板球场上轮到击球时，我才体会过类似的悬心。船在水上几乎纹丝不动。我从没见过这么平的海面。细碎的涟漪轻轻拍着防波堤的石壁。

这份十全的宁静仿佛可以千秋万代持续下去。霍克先生毫无动静。骤然间，全场改换为一幅热火朝天的图景。我听见霍克先生嗬地闷叫一声，看见他在座位上猛地一蹿。教授半转过身，我瞥见他那张愤慨的脸，激动得通红。接着场景又以幻灯换片般的速度一变。我看见霍克先生又是一蹿，下一刻船已底朝天扣在水里，而我正头朝下扎向水底，浑身浸透的衣裳带来那种说不清道不明的黏腻感，压得我喘不过气。

我在翻掉的船旁浮出水面。头一眼看见的是霍克先生那张噗噗吐水的脸。我不理会他，朝教授的脑袋在水面上一起一伏的地方游去。

===Original===

“Keep cool,” I said. A silly remark in the circumstances.

He was swimming energetically but unskilfully. He appeared to be one of those men who can look after themselves in the water only when they are in bathing costume. In his shore clothes it would have taken him a week to struggle to land, if he had got there at all, which was unlikely.

I know all about saving people from drowning. We used to practise it with a dummy in the swimming-bath at school. I attacked him from the rear, and got a good grip of him by the shoulders. I then swam on my back in the direction of land, and beached him with much éclat at the feet of an admiring crowd. I had thought of putting him under once or twice just to show him he was being rescued, but decided against such a course as needlessly realistic. As it was, I fancy he had swallowed of seawater two or three hearty draughts.

The crowd was enthusiastic.

===Chinese===

「保持冷静，」我说。在这个场合，这是一句蠢话。

他游得卖力而不得法。他似乎是那种只有穿上泳装才会在水里照管自己的人。穿着家常衣裳，他得挣扎一个礼拜才上得了岸——如果真上得了的话，而这可能性不大。

救溺水的人，我全懂。上学时我们在游泳馆里拿假人练过。我从背后接近，一把攥牢他的双肩，然后仰泳朝岸进发，把他风光体面地送上滩头，摆在一圈啧啧称赞的看客脚边。我本想把他往水下按一两回，好教他明白自己正在被人搭救，转念又作罢——未免太逼真了。就这样，我想他也已经灌下去两三大口海水了。

看众们热情高涨。

===Original===

“Brave young feller,” said somebody.

I blushed. This was Fame.

“Jumped in, he did, sure enough, an’ saved the gentleman!”

“Be the old soul drownded?”

“That girt fule, ’Arry ’Awk!”

===Chinese===

「勇敢的小伙子，」有人说。

我脸红了。这就是盛名。

「他真的跳下去了，真格的，救了这位先生！」

「那位老先生淹死没有？」

「那个大蠢货，哈里·霍克！」

===Original===

I was sorry for Mr. Hawk. Popular opinion was against him. What the professor said of him, when he recovered his breath, I cannot repeat﻿—not because I do not remember it, but because there is a line, and one must draw it. Let it be sufficient to say that on the subject of Mr. Hawk he saw eye to eye with the citizen who had described him as a “girt fule.” I could not help thinking that my fellow conspirator did well to keep out of it all. He was now sitting in the boat, which he had restored to its normal position, baling pensively with an old tin can. To satire from the shore he paid no attention.

The professor stood up, and stretched out his hand. I grasped it.

“Mr. Garnet,” he said, for all the world as if he had been the father of the heroine of “Hilda’s Hero,” “we parted recently in anger. Let me thank you for your gallant conduct, and hope that bygones will be bygones.”

I came out strong. I continued to hold his hand. The crowd raised a sympathetic cheer.

===Chinese===

我替霍克先生难过。舆论对他不利。教授缓过气来之后对他说了些什么，我没法复述——倒不是我不记得，而是凡事总有一条线，总得有人来画。这么说就够了：论霍克先生其人，教授的见解同那位骂他「大蠢货」的市民所见略同。我不由得想，我这位同谋者置身事外，实在明智。他此刻坐在船里——船已被他扶正——正拿一只旧铁皮罐若有所思地舀水。岸上的冷嘲热讽，他一概不理。

教授站起来，伸出手。我握住了。

「加尼特先生，」他说，那口吻活脱脱是《希尔达的英雄》里女主角的父亲，「日前我们不欢而散。让我为你的英勇行为谢谢你，但愿前嫌尽释。」

我大出风头。我继续握着他的手。看众们发出一阵同情的欢呼。

===Original===

I said, “Professor, the fault was mine. Show that you have forgiven me by coming up to the farm and putting on something dry.”

“An excellent idea, me boy; I *am* a little wet.”

“A little,” I agreed.

We walked briskly up the hill to the farm.

===Chinese===

我说：「教授，错处在我。请你上农庄去换身干衣裳，以示原谅。」

「好主意，俺的小伙子；俺是有点儿湿了。」

「一点儿，」我附和。

我们精神抖擞地爬上山坡，进了农庄。

===Original===

Ukridge met us at the gate.

He diagnosed the situation rapidly.

“You’re all wet,” he said. I admitted it.

“Professor Derrick has had an unfortunate boating accident,” I explained.

“And Mr. Garnet heroically dived in, in all his clothes, and saved me life,” broke in the professor. “A hero, sir. A﻿—*choo*!”

===Chinese===

厄克里奇在门口迎着。

他火眼金睛，一眼看穿了局面。

「你们全湿透了，」他说。我承认了。

「德里克教授碰上了一起不幸的翻船事故，」我解释道。

「加尼特先生穿着一身衣裳英勇跳水，救了俺的命，」教授插话。「一位英雄，先生。一——*嚏*！」

===Original===

“You’re catching cold, old horse,” said Ukridge, all friendliness and concern, his little differences with the professor having vanished like thawed snow. “This’ll never do. Come upstairs and get into something of Garnet’s. My own toggery wouldn’t fit. What? Come along, come along, I’ll get you some hot water. Mrs. Beale﻿—Mrs. *Beale*! We want a large can of hot water. At once. What? Yes, immediately. What? Very well then, as soon as you can. Now then, Garny, my boy, out with the duds. What do you think of this, now, professor? A sweetly pretty thing in grey flannel. Here’s a shirt. Get out of that wet toggery, and Mrs. Beale shall dry it. Don’t attempt to tell me about it till you’re changed. Socks! Socks forward. Show socks. Here you are. Coat? Try this blazer. That’s right﻿—that’s right.”

He bustled about till the professor was clothed, then marched him downstairs, and gave him a cigar.

“Now, what’s all this? What happened?”

The professor explained. He was severe in his narration upon the unlucky Mr. Hawk.

===Chinese===

「你要着凉了，老马，」厄克里奇说，满腔的友善与关怀，他同教授的小小龃龉已如春雪般消融。「这可不行。上楼来，换身加尼特的衣裳。我自己的行头你穿不下。什么？来吧来吧，我给你弄点热水。比尔太太——比尔*太太*！我们要一大罐热水。马上。什么？对，立刻。什么？很好，那就尽快。好了，加尼我的老弟，把行头都翻出来。你瞧这件怎么样，教授？灰法兰绒的一件漂亮玩意儿。这是衬衫。快脱了那身湿行头，让比尔太太给你烘干。换好之前，一个字也别急着跟我讲。袜子！袜子递上来。看看袜子。给。上衣？试试这件运动夹克。这就对了——这就对了。」

他忙前忙后，直到教授穿戴齐整，然后押他下楼，敬上一支雪茄。

「好了，这是怎么回事？出什么事了？」

教授讲了一遍。他叙述起来，对倒霉的霍克先生严词厉色。

===Original===

“I was fishing, Mr. Ukridge, with me back turned, when I felt the boat rock violently from one side to the other, to such an extent that I nearly lost me equilibrium, and then the boat upset. The man’s a fool, sir. I could not see what had happened, my back being turned, as I say.”

“Garnet must have seen. What happened, old horse?”

“It was very sudden,” I said. “It seemed to me as if the man had got an attack of cramp. That would account for it. He has the reputation of being a most sober and trustworthy fellow.”

“Never trust that sort of man,” said Ukridge. “They are always the worst. It’s plain to me that this man was beastly drunk, and upset the boat while trying to do a dance.”

===Chinese===

「俺正钓着鱼，厄克里奇先生，背朝着船头，忽觉那船剧烈地左右摇晃，晃得俺差点儿失了平衡，跟着船就翻了。这人是个傻瓜，先生。俺背朝着后头，什么也没瞧见，俺说过了。」

「加尼特准看见了。怎么回事，老马？」

「事情非常突然，」我说。「依我看，那人像是腿抽了筋。这就说得通了。他向来有最稳重可靠、滴酒不沾的名声。」

「那种人千万别信，」厄克里奇说。「他们往往最坏。明摆着，这家伙烂醉如泥，跳舞跳翻了船。」

===Original===

“A great curse, drink,” said the professor. “Why, yes, Mr. Ukridge, I think I will. Thank you. Thank you. That will be enough. Not all the soda, if you please. Ah! this tastes pleasanter than salt water, Mr. Garnet. Eh? Eh? Ha﻿—Ha!”

He was in the best of tempers, and I worked strenuously to keep him so. My scheme had been so successful that its iniquity did not worry me. I have noticed that this is usually the case in matters of this kind. It is the bungled crime that brings remorse.

“We must go round the links together one of these days, Mr. Garnet,” said the professor. “I have noticed you there on several occasions, playing a strong game. I have lately taken to using a wooden putter. It is wonderful what a difference it makes.”

Golf is a great bond of union. We wandered about the grounds discussing the game, the *entente cordiale* growing more firmly established every moment.

“We must certainly arrange a meeting,” concluded the professor. “I shall be interested to see how we stand with regard to one another. I have improved my game considerably since I have been down here. Considerably.”

===Chinese===

「酒是一大祸害，」教授说。「哦，好的，厄克里奇先生，我来一点儿。谢谢。谢谢。够了够了。苏打水别全倒上去，劳驾。啊！这可比海水可口多了，加尼特先生。嗯？嗯？哈——哈！」

他心绪极佳，我也卖力地让这份好心境维持下去。我的计谋如此成功，成功得我竟不为它的缺德内疚。我注意到这类事情往往如此：招来悔恨的，从来是砸锅的罪行。

「哪天咱们得一道逛逛球场，加尼特先生，」教授说。「我好几回瞧见你在那儿，打得一手硬球。俺近来改用木制推杆了。真神了，差得不是一星半点。」

高尔夫是联络感情的一大纽带。我们在宅子四周信步，大谈此道，那份*协约*（entente cordiale）一分钟比一分钟牢固。

「咱们务必安排一回碰面，」教授总结道。「我很想看看咱们俩谁高谁低。自打下山来，俺的球技大有长进。大有长进。」

===Original===

“My only feat worthy of mention since I started the game,” I said, “has been to halve a round with Angus M’Lurkin at St. Andrews.”

“*The* M’Lurkin?” asked the professor, impressed.

“Yes. But it was one of his very off days, I fancy. He must have had gout or something. And I have certainly never played so well since.”

“Still﻿—” said the professor. “Yes, we must really arrange to meet.”

===Chinese===

「我打球以来唯一值得一提的成绩，」我说，「是在圣安德鲁斯同安格斯·麦克卢金打平了一轮。」

「*那位*麦克卢金？」教授问，肃然起敬。

「正是。不过我想那天他状态极差。不是痛风就是别的什么。反正我后来再没打得那么好过。」

「话虽如此——」教授说。「对，咱们真得安排一回碰面。」

===Original===

With Ukridge, who was in one of his less tactless moods, he became very friendly.

Ukridge’s ready agreement with his strictures on the erring Hawk had a great deal to do with this. When a man has a grievance, he feels drawn to those who will hear him patiently and sympathise. Ukridge was all sympathy.

“The man is an unprincipled scoundrel,” he said, “and should be torn limb from limb. Take my advice, and don’t go out with him again. Show him that you are not a man to be trifled with. The spilt child dreads the water, what? Human life isn’t safe with such men as Hawk roaming about.”

“You are perfectly right, sir. The man can have no defence. I shall not employ him again.”

I felt more than a little guilty while listening to this duet on the subject of the man whom I had lured from the straight and narrow path. But the professor would listen to no defence. My attempts at excusing him were ill received. Indeed, the professor showed such signs of becoming heated that I abandoned my fellow-conspirator to his fate with extreme promptness. After all, an addition to the stipulated reward﻿—one of these days﻿—would compensate him for any loss which he might sustain from the withdrawal of the professor’s custom. Mr. Harry Hawk was in good enough case. I would see that he did not suffer.

===Chinese===

对厄克里奇——他正巧处在那些不算太不知趣的心绪之一里——教授变得非常友好。

厄克里奇对他历数迷途的霍克之痛斥随声附和得又快又欢，这功不可没。一个人心里有委屈，就亲近那些肯耐心听他、还表同情的人。厄克里奇满腔都是同情。

「那家伙是个无法无天的恶棍，」他说，「该把他撕成碎块。听我一句，别再跟他出海。让他知道你不是好欺负的。一朝被蛇咬，十年怕井绳，对吧？有霍克这号人满世界晃荡，人命就不安全。」

「您说得完全对，先生。那人无从辩白。俺不会再雇他了。」

听着这一曲针对那人的二重唱——那人可是被我引诱出正道的——我心里愧疚不轻。可教授什么辩护都听不进。我替他开脱的尝试处处碰壁。的确，教授眼看就要冒火，我当即当机立断，把我这位同谋者抛弃在他自己的命运里。反正，在讲定的报酬之外再添上一笔——来日方长——足以赔偿他因失去教授这位主顾而蒙受的损失。哈里·霍克先生的处境够好的了。我会关照他不吃亏。

===Original===

Filled with these philanthropic feelings, I turned once more to talk with the professor of niblicks and approach shots and holes done in three without a brassy. We were a merry party at lunch﻿—a lunch fortunately in Mrs. Beale’s best vein, consisting of a roast chicken and sweets. Chicken had figured somewhat frequently of late on our daily bill of fare.

We saw the professor off the premises in his dried clothes, and I turned back to put the fowls to bed in a happier frame of mind than I had known for a long time. I whistled ragtime airs as I worked.

“Rum old buffer,” said Ukridge meditatively, pouring himself out another whisky and soda. “My goodness, I should have liked to have seen him in the water. Why do I miss these good things?”

===Chinese===

怀着这一腔博爱之情，我又回头同教授谈起挖起杆、攻果岭和不用黄铜头木杆三杆进洞。午饭桌上我们是一伙快活人——幸而这顿午饭正逢比尔太太的超水平发挥，计有烤鸡一只，甜食一道。近来，鸡在我们每日菜单上出场的频率颇高。

我们送教授穿着烘干的衣裳出了大门，我折回去安顿群鸡就寝，心里是一段时日以来未有的轻快。我一边干活，一边吹着拉格泰姆的调子。

「怪老头儿，」厄克里奇若有所思地说，又给自己斟了一杯威士忌兑苏打。「我的天，我真想瞧瞧他在水里的模样。这样的好事，怎么回回都叫我错过？」

## XXI The Calm Before the Storm / 二十一 风暴前的平静

===Original===

“Beale,” I said, “are you drunk?”

“Wish I was, sir,” said the Hired Man.

“Then what on earth do you mean? Gone? Where have they gone to?”

“Don’t know, sir. London, I expect.”

===Chinese===

「比尔，」我说，「你醉了？」

「但愿俺醉了，先生，」雇工说。

「那你到底是什么意思？走了？他们上哪儿去了？」

「不知道，先生。俺估摸着，伦敦。」

===Original===

“London? Why?”

“Don’t know, sir.”

“When did they go? Oh, you told me that. Didn’t they say why they were going?”

“No, sir.”

“Didn’t you ask? When you saw them packing up and going to the station, didn’t you do anything?”

===Chinese===

「伦敦？为什么？」

「不知道，先生。」

「什么时候走的？哦，你说过了。他们没说为什么走吗？」

「没说，先生。」

「你没问？你眼看着他们收拾行李上车站，就什么也没做？」

===Original===

“No, sir.”

“Why on earth not?”

“I didn’t see them, sir. I only found out as they’d gone after they’d been and went, sir. Walking down by the Net and Mackerel, met one of them coastguards. ‘Oh,’ says he, ‘so you’re moving?’ ‘Who’s a-moving?’ I says to him. ‘Well,’ he says to me, ‘I seen your Mr. Ukridge and his missus get into the three o’clock train for Axminster. I thought as you was all a-moving.’ ‘Ho,’ I says, ‘Ho,’ wondering, and I goes on. When I gets back, I asks the missus did she see them packing their boxes, and she says, No, she says, they didn’t pack no boxes as she knowed of. And blowed if they had, Mr. Garnet, sir.”

“What! They didn’t pack!”

“No, sir.”

===Chinese===

「没做，先生。」

「到底为什么不做？」

「俺压根儿没瞧见他们，先生。他们走了之后，俺才咂摸出他们走了。俺顺着『网与鲭鱼』酒馆那头遛弯儿，碰上个海岸警卫队的。『哦，』他说，『你们这是搬家？』『谁搬家？』俺跟他说。『喏，』他跟俺说，『俺瞧见你家厄克里奇先生带着他老婆子，上了三点钟去阿克斯明斯特的火车。俺寻思你们全伙儿都搬呢。』『嚯，』俺说，『嚯，』心里直犯嘀咕，接着走俺的。等俺回去，俺问老婆子瞧见他们打箱子里没有，她说，没，她说，她压根儿不知道他们打过什么箱子。可不嘛，他们真没打，加尼特先生，您哪。」

「什么！没打包！」

「没，先生。」

===Original===

We looked at one another.

“Beale,” I said.

“Sir?”

“Do you know what I think?”

“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===

我们面面相觑。

「比尔，」我说。

「先生？」

「你知道我在想什么吗？」

「知道，先生。」

===Original===

“They’ve bolted.”

“So I says to the missus, sir. It struck me right off, in a manner of speaking.”

“This is awful,” I said.

“Yes, sir.”

His face betrayed no emotion, but he was one of those men whose expression never varies. It’s a way they have in the Army.

===Chinese===

「他们跑路了。」

「俺也这么跟老婆子说来着，先生。俺一下子就咂摸出来了，可以说。」

「这可糟透了，」我说。

「是，先生。」

他脸上不动声色，不过他本就是那种表情永不变化的人。这是军队里养成的做派。

===Original===

“This wants thinking out, Beale,” I said.

“Yes, sir.”

“You’d better ask Mrs. Beale to give me some dinner, and then I’ll think it over.”

“Yes, sir.”

I was in an unpleasant position. Ukridge by his defection had left me in charge of the farm. I could dissolve the concern, I supposed, if I wished, and return to London, but I particularly desired to remain in Combe Regis. To complete the victory I had won on the links, it was necessary for me to continue as I had begun. I was in the position of a general who has conquered a hostile country, and is obliged to soothe the feelings of the conquered people before his labours can be considered at an end. I had rushed the professor. It must now be my aim to keep him from regretting that he had been rushed. I must, therefore, stick to my post with the tenacity of an able-bodied leech. There would be trouble. Of that I was certain. As soon as the news got about that Ukridge had gone, the deluge would begin. His creditors would abandon their passive tactics, and take active steps. There was a chance that aggressive measures would be confined to the enemy at our gates, the tradesmen of Combe Regis. But the probability was that the news would spread, and the injured merchants of Dorchester and Axminster rush to the scene of hostilities.

===Chinese===

「这事得好好琢磨琢磨，比尔，」我说。

「是，先生。」

「你最好请比尔太太给我弄顿晚饭，然后我来细想。」

「是，先生。」

我的处境很不妙。厄克里奇这一开小差，把农庄扔给我照管。我想，只要我愿意，大可以把这一摊子散伙，回伦敦去，可我特别想留在库姆里吉斯。要彻底巩固我在高尔夫球场上赢得的胜利，我就必须乘胜追击。我现在的处境，好比一位征服了敌国的将军，还须先抚慰好亡国百姓的情绪，才算大功告成。我已经对教授奇袭得手。如今我的目标，是不让他后悔自己被奇袭过。因此，我必须像一条壮硕的水蛭，死死叮在岗位上。麻烦会有。这一点我笃定。厄克里奇跑路的消息一传开，大洪水就要来了。债主们将放弃消极战术，采取积极步骤。有个指望是：攻势将只限于家门口的敌人，也就是库姆里吉斯的本镇商贾。但多半消息还会走漏，多尔切斯特和阿克斯明斯特的受害商人们将蜂拥奔赴战场。

===Original===

I summoned Beale after dinner and held a council of war. It was no time for airy persiflage. I said, “Beale, we’re in the cart.”

“Sir?”

“Mr. Ukridge going away like this has left me in a most unpleasant position. I would like to talk it over with you. I daresay you know that we﻿—that Mr. Ukridge owes a considerable amount of money round about here to tradesmen?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Well, when they find out that he has﻿—er﻿—”

===Chinese===

饭后我召见比尔，开了个军事会议。这不是耍嘴皮子的时候。我说：「比尔，咱们栽进沟里了。」

「先生？」

「厄克里奇先生这么一走，把我撂在了一个窝心的位子上。我想同你合计合计。咱们——呃——厄克里奇先生在本地欠下各路商家一笔不小的数目，这个你大概知道吧？」

「知道，先生。」

「那么，等他们发觉他已经——呃——」

===Original===

“Shot the moon, sir,” suggested the Hired Retainer helpfully.

“Gone up to town,” I amended. “When they find out that he has gone up to town, they are likely to come bothering us a good deal.”

“Yes, sir.”

“I fancy that we shall have them all round here tomorrow. News of this sort always spreads quickly. The point is, then, what are we to do?”

He propounded no scheme, but stood in an easy attitude of attention, waiting for me to continue.

===Chinese===

「月黑风高连夜溜了，先生，」受雇的忠仆贴心地提示。

「上伦敦去了，」我修正道。「等他们发觉他上伦敦去了，只怕要来找咱们的麻烦，没完没了。」

「是，先生。」

「我估计明天他们就会全数杀上门来。这类消息向来传得飞快。那么问题来了：咱们怎么办？」

他不献一策，只以闲适的立正姿势站着，等我往下讲。

===Original===

I continued.

“Let’s see exactly how we stand,” I said. “My point is that I particularly wish to go on living down here for at least another fortnight. Of course, my position is simple. I am Mr. Ukridge’s guest. I shall go on living as I have been doing up to the present. He asked me down here to help him look after the fowls, so I shall go on looking after them. Complications set in when we come to consider you and Mrs. Beale. I suppose you won’t care to stop on after this?”

The Hired Retainer scratched his chin and glanced out of the window. The moon was up, and the garden looked cool and mysterious in the dim light.

“It’s a pretty place, Mr. Garnet, sir,” he said.

“It is,” I said, “but about other considerations? There’s the matter of wages. Are yours in arrears?”

===Chinese===

我往下讲。

「先把咱们的家底摆清楚，」我说。「我的要点是：我特别想在这儿再住下去，起码再住两个礼拜。当然，我的处境简单。我是厄克里奇先生的客人。我照旧住下去就是了，同以往一样。他请我来帮他照看群鸡，那我就照看下去。麻烦出在你同比尔太太身上。我猜，出了这事，你们不愿留下来了？」

受雇的忠仆挠了挠下巴，朝窗外望去。月亮升起来了，昏光里的园子显得清凉而神秘。

「这地方怪好的，加尼特先生，您哪。」他说。

「确实，」我说，「可别的事呢？工钱怎么办？欠着你的么？」

===Original===

“Yes, sir. A month.”

“And Mrs. Beale’s the same, I suppose?”

“Yes, sir. A month.”

“H’m. Well, it seems to me, Beale, you can’t lose anything by stopping on.”

===Chinese===

「欠着，先生。一个月。」

「比尔太太想必也一样？」

「是，先生。一个月。」

「唔。这么说吧，比尔，留下来你横竖不吃亏。」

===Original===

“I can’t be paid any less than I have bin, sir,” he agreed.

“Exactly. And, as you say, it’s a pretty place. You might just as well stop on, and help me in the fowl-run. What do you think?”

“Very well, sir.”

“And Mrs. Beale will do the same?”

“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===

「反正工钱不能再比这回少了，先生。」他表示同意。

「正是。再说，如你所说，这地方怪好的。你索性留下一块儿帮我看鸡圈，岂不两全。你看呢？」

「成，先生。」

「比尔太太也照旧？」

「是，先生。」

===Original===

“That’s excellent. You’re a hero, Beale. I shan’t forget you. There’s a cheque coming to me from a magazine in another week for a short story. When it arrives, I’ll look into that matter of back wages. Tell Mrs. Beale I’m much obliged to her, will you?”

“Yes, sir.”

Having concluded that delicate business, I lit my pipe, and strolled out into the garden with Bob. I cursed Ukridge as I walked. It was abominable of him to desert me in this way. Even if I had not been his friend, it would have been bad. The fact that we had known each other for years made it doubly discreditable. He might at least have warned me, and given me the option of leaving the sinking ship with him.

===Chinese===

「那太好了。你是一条好汉，比尔。我不会忘了你。再过一礼拜，有家杂志该寄我一笔短篇的稿费支票。一到手，我就研究研究欠薪的事。请转告比尔太太，我非常感激她，行吗？」

「是，先生。」

这桩难办的交易办妥，我点上烟斗，带着博布溜达到园子里。我一路走一路咒骂厄克里奇。他这样弃我而去，实在可恶。就算我不是他的朋友，这也不像话；我们相识多年，这就加倍地丢人。他至少也该给我个信儿，让我可以拣着同他一块儿弃船逃生。

===Original===

But, I reflected, I ought not to be surprised. His whole career, as long as I had known him, had been dotted with little eccentricities of a type which an unfeeling world generally stigmatises as shady. They were small things, it was true; but they ought to have warned me. We are most of us wise after the event. When the wind has blown, we can generally discover a multitude of straws which should have shown us which way it was blowing.

Once, I remembered, in our schoolmaster days, when guineas, though regular, were few, he had had occasion to increase his wardrobe. If I recollect rightly, he thought he had a chance of a good position in the tutoring line, and only needed good clothes to make it his. He took four pounds of his salary in advance﻿—he was in the habit of doing this: he never had any salary left by the end of term, it having vanished in advance loans beforehand. With this he was to buy two suits, a hat, new boots, and collars. When it came to making the purchases, he found, what he had overlooked previously in his optimistic way, that four pounds did not go very far. At the time, I remember, I thought his method of grappling with the situation humorous. He bought a hat for three-and-sixpence, and got the suits and the boots on the instalment system, paying a small sum in advance, as earnest of more to come. He then pawned one suit to pay for the first few instalments, and finally departed, to be known no more. His address he had given﻿—with a false name﻿—at an empty house, and when the tailor arrived with the minions of the law, all he found was an annoyed caretaker, and a pile of letters written by himself, containing his bill in its various stages of evolution.

Or again. There was a bicycle and photograph shop near the school. He went into this one day, and his roving eye fell on a tandem bicycle. He did not want a tandem bicycle, but that influenced him not at all. He ordered it provisionally. He also ordered an enlarging camera, a kodak, and a magic lantern. The order was booked, and the goods were to be delivered when he had made up his mind concerning them. After a week the shopman sent round to ask if there were any further particulars which Mr. Ukridge would like to learn before definitely ordering them. Mr. Ukridge sent back word that he was considering the matter, and that in the meantime would he be so good as to let him have that little clockwork man in his window, which walked when wound up? Having got this, and not paid for it, Ukridge thought that he had done handsomely by the bicycle and photograph man, and that things were square between them. The latter met him a few days afterwards, and expostulated plaintively. Ukridge explained. “My good man,” he said, “you know, I really think we need say no more about the matter. Really, you’re come out of it very well. Now, look here, which would you rather be owed for? A clockwork man﻿—which is broken, and you can have it back﻿—or a tandem bicycle, an enlarging camera, a kodak, and a magic-lantern? What?” His reasoning was too subtle for the uneducated mind. The man retired, puzzled, and unpaid, and Ukridge kept the clockwork toy.

===Chinese===

不过我转念一想，本不该大惊小怪。自我认识他以来，他的整个生涯就散布着种种小小的怪癖，冷血的世界一般管这叫行迹可疑。都是些小事，不假；可它们本该给我敲响警钟。我们大抵都是事后诸葛。风刮过去了，我们总能找出一大把麦芒，本来嘛，正是它们该告诉我们风往哪边刮。

我记得，还是当年教书的时候，几尼虽然月月照发，却少得可怜，有一回他不得不扩充他的衣柜。如果我没记错，他自以为在家庭教师这一行有望谋个肥缺，只消有身好衣裳便可到手。他预支了四镑薪水——这是他的老习惯：每到学期末他从不剩一文薪水，因为早在之前就借支个精光了。他要用这笔钱置两身衣服、一顶帽子、新靴子，还有领子。及至动手采买，他才发现——他一贯乐观，先前竟没看到——四镑花不出多大动静。我记得，当时我还觉得他对付局面的手法挺幽默。他花三先令六便士买了顶帽子，衣裳和靴子走了分期付款，先付一小笔，作为后续款项的定金。随后他当掉其中一身衣服来付头几期分期款，最后扬长而去，从此下落不明。他留的地址——用的还是假名——是所空房子；裁缝带着法律的爪牙登门时，只寻见一位气哄哄的看门人，和一堆他自己写的信，里面是他那笔账单各个演化阶段的稿本。

再举一例。学校附近有一家兼卖自行车和照相器材的铺子。一天他进了这家铺子，游移的目光落在一辆双人自行车上。他并不需要双人自行车，可这全然不影响他。他先把它订购了下来。他还订购了一台放大照相机、一台柯达和一盏幻灯。订单登记在册，货物等他拿定主意便发货。过了一个礼拜，店主打发人来问，厄克里奇先生在正式下单之前，可还有什么详情想了解。厄克里奇先生回话说，他正在考虑此事；在此期间，可否劳驾把橱窗里那个上发条会走路的小发条人给他送来？到手之后，钱照旧不付，厄克里奇倒觉得他待这位自行车兼照相器材商不薄，两下里已经两讫。几天后店主撞见他，可怜巴巴地抗议。厄克里奇做了解释。「我的好人，」他说，「你知道，这件事我看咱们真不必再提了。说真的，你这头已经够划算了。来，我问你，你情愿人家欠你哪一样？一个发条人——已经摔坏了，你随时可以收回——还是一辆双人自行车、一台放大照相机、一台柯达、外加一盏幻灯？怎么样？」他的推理，对没受过教育的头脑来说未免太精微。那人悻悻而退，账没讨着；发条玩具则归了厄克里奇。

## VII The *Entente Cordiale* Is Sealed / 七 协约达成

===Original===

There are moments and moments. The present one belonged to the more painful variety.

Even to my exhausted mind it was plain that there was a need here for explanations. An Irishman’s croquet-lawn is his castle, and strangers cannot plunge in through hedges without inviting comment.

Unfortunately, speech was beyond me. I could have emptied a water-butt, laid down and gone to sleep, or melted ice with a touch of the finger, but I could not speak. The conversation was opened by the other man, in whose restraining hand Aunt Elizabeth now lay, outwardly resigned but inwardly as I, who knew her haughty spirit, could guess, boiling with baffled resentment. I could see her looking out of the corner of her eye, trying to estimate the chances of getting in one good hard peck with her aquiline beak.

“Come right in,” said the man pleasantly. “Don’t knock.”

===Chinese===

时刻与时刻，各有分别。眼前这一刻，便属于难堪的那一类。

连我这颗累透了的脑袋也看得明白：此地需要解释。爱尔兰人的槌球草坪就是他的城堡，外人不能从树篱里一头扎进来而不招人说三道四。

不幸的是，开不得口。我本可以把一整桶雨水倒空、躺倒睡去，或是一指头化开坚冰，偏偏说不出话。话头由那位先生开了——伊丽莎白姨妈此刻正躺在他那只钳制的手里，外表顺从，内心，凭我对她那股傲气的了解，分明在无可奈何的怨愤中翻滚。我看得见她从眼角里瞟出来，估量着用那鹰钩喙狠狠钳上一口的机会有多大。

「进来吧，」那人和和气气地说。「不必敲门。」

===Original===

I stood there, gasping. I was only too well aware that I presented a quaint appearance. I had removed my hat before entering the hedge, and my hair was full of twigs and other foreign substances. My face was moist and grimy. My mouth hung open. My legs felt as if they had ceased to belong to me.

“I must apol﻿—﻿ ﻿…” I began, and ended the sentence with gulps.

The elderly gentleman looked at me with what seemed to me indignant surprise. His daughter appeared to my guilty conscience to be looking through me. Aunt Elizabeth sneered. The only friendly face was the man’s. He regarded me with a kindly smile, as if I were some old friend who had dropped in unexpectedly.

“Take a long breath,” he advised.

I took several, and felt better.

===Chinese===

我站在那儿直喘。我心里太清楚自己这副尊容有多古怪了。进树篱之前我摘了帽子，头发里满是细枝和别的外来杂质。脸又湿又脏。嘴张着。两条腿仿佛已经不归我管。

「我必须道——……」我起了个头，后半句全化作了吞咽。

老先生望着我，那神情依我看是又惊又怒。他的女儿，在我这颗做贼心虚的良心看来，简直是看穿了我。伊丽莎白姨妈嗤之以鼻。唯一友善的一张脸是那位先生的。他含笑打量我，仿佛我是哪位不期而至的老友。

「来个深呼吸，」他建议道。

我来了好几个深呼吸，感觉好些了。

===Original===

“I must apologise for this intrusion,” I said successfully. “Unwarrantable” would have rounded off the sentence neatly, but I would not risk it. It would have been mere bravado to attempt unnecessary words of five syllables. I took in more breath. “The fact is, I did﻿—didn’t know there was a private garden beyond the hedge. If you will give me my hen﻿ ﻿…”

I stopped. Aunt Elizabeth was looking away, as if endeavouring to create an impression of having nothing to do with me. I am told by one who knows that hens cannot raise their eyebrows, not having any; but I am prepared to swear that at this moment Aunt Elizabeth raised hers. I will go further. She sniffed.

“Here you are,” said the man. “Though it’s hard to say goodbye.”

He held out the hen to me, and at this point a hitch occurred. He did his part, the letting go, all right. It was in my department, the taking hold, that the thing was bungled. Aunt Elizabeth slipped from my grasp like an eel, stood for a moment eyeing me satirically with her head on one side, then fled and entrenched herself in some bushes at the end of the lawn.

There are times when the most resolute man feels that he can battle no longer with fate; when everything seems against him and the only course is a dignified retreat. But there is one thing essential to a dignified retreat. You must know the way out. It was the lack of that knowledge that kept me standing there, looking more foolish than anyone has ever looked since the world began. I could not retire by way of the hedge. If I could have leaped the hedge with a single debonair bound, that would have been satisfactory. But the hedge was high, and I did not feel capable at the moment of achieving a debonair bound over a footstool.

===Chinese===

「我必须为这番擅闯赔罪，」我总算把话说囫囵了。「『狂悖无礼』这个词本可以把句子收得漂漂亮亮，可我不敢冒险。去尝试不必要的五音节词汇，纯属虚张声势。我又吸了口气。「事情是这样的：我原先不——不知道树篱后头还有一座私家花园。要是您肯把我的母鸡还我……」

我住口了。伊丽莎白姨妈正望着别处，仿佛竭力要给人造成一种「与俺无干」的印象。有位行家告诉过我，母鸡扬不起眉毛，因为根本没有眉毛；可我随时愿意起誓，这一刻伊丽莎白姨妈偏偏扬了扬她那对。我还能更进一步：她嗤了一声。

「给你，」那人说。「只是真舍不得道别。」

他把母鸡朝我递过来，就在这时出了岔子。他那一半——撒手——完成得无可挑剔。砸锅的是我这一半——接住。伊丽莎白姨妈泥鳅似的从我手里滑脱，歪着头讥诮地打量了我片刻，随即逃之夭夭，在草坪尽头一丛灌木里筑起了工事。

人有时到了这般田地：最有决断的汉子也觉得再斗不过命运；万事与自己作对，唯一的出路是有体面的撤退。可有体面的撤退少不了一样东西——你得认得路。正是少了这一样，害我站在原地，那副蠢相自开天辟地以来无人能出其右。我没法从树篱原路撤退。倘若我能一个潇洒的箭步跃过树篱，倒也心满意足。可树篱高，而眼下的我，恐怕连一步跨过脚凳的潇洒箭步都拿不出来。

===Original===

The man saved the situation. He seemed to possess that magnetic power over his fellows which marks the born leader. Under his command we became an organised army. The common object, the pursuit of the elusive Aunt Elizabeth, made us friends. In the first minute of the proceedings the Irishman was addressing me as “me dear boy,” and the man, who had introduced himself as Mr. Chase﻿—a lieutenant, I learned later, in His Majesty’s Navy﻿—was shouting directions to me by name. I have never assisted at any ceremony at which formality was so completely dispensed with. The ice was not merely broken; it was shivered into a million fragments.

“Go in and drive her out, Garnet,” shouted Mr. Chase. “In my direction if you can. Look out on the left, Phyllis.”

Even in that disturbing moment I could not help noticing his use of the Christian name. It seemed to me more than sinister. I did not like the idea of dashing young lieutenants in the senior service calling a girl Phyllis whose eyes had haunted me since I had first seen them.

Nevertheless, I crawled into the bushes and administered to Aunt Elizabeth a prod in the lower ribs﻿—if hens have lower ribs. The more I study hens, the more things they seem able to get along without﻿—which abruptly disturbed her calm detachment. She shot out at the spot where Mr. Chase was waiting with his coat off, and was promptly enveloped in that garment and captured.

“The essence of strategy,” observed Mr. Chase approvingly, “is surprise. A neat piece of work!”

===Chinese===

那位先生挽救了局面。他身上似乎有一种统摄同侪的磁力，天生领袖的标记。在他的号令下，我们成了一支有组织的军队。共同的目标——追捕狡黠的伊丽莎白姨妈——使我们成了朋友。行动开始不到一分钟，那位爱尔兰人已经「我亲爱的孩子」长「我亲爱的孩子」短地唤我；那位先生——他自我介绍姓蔡斯，我后来得知他是皇家海军的一位上尉——已经指名道躯地朝我发号施令。我从没参加过哪场仪式，礼数省得这么彻底。坚冰何止是破了；它粉成了千千万万块碎片。

「进去把她轰出来，加尼特，」蔡斯先生喊道。「尽量朝我这边。菲莉斯，盯住左边。」

即便在那个心慌意乱的时刻，我仍不免注意到他对教名的使用。依我看，此事凶多吉少。一位堂堂皇家海军的年轻上尉，管一个姑娘叫菲莉斯——而自从我第一眼见到她的眼睛，那双眼睛便在我心头萦绕不去——这个念头我不喜欢。

尽管如此，我还是爬进灌木丛，照伊丽莎白姨妈的下肋戳了一指头——倘若母鸡有下肋的话。我对母鸡研究得越深，越觉得它们没有这个那个也照样过活——这一指骤然搅了她的超然宁静。她从蔡斯先生守候的地方一射而出——他早已脱下外衣严阵以待——旋即被那件衣服裹住，就擒。

「战略的精髓，」蔡斯先生赞许道，「在于出其不意。干得漂亮！」

===Original===

I thanked him. He deprecated my thanks. He had, he said, only done his duty, as expected to by England. He then introduced me to the elderly Irishman, who was, it seemed, a professor at Dublin University, by name, Derrick. Whatever it was that he professed, it was something that did not keep him for a great deal of his time at the University. He informed me that he always spent his summers at Combe Regis.

“I was surprised to see you at Combe Regis,” I said. “When you got out at Yeovil, I thought I had seen the last of you.”

I think I am gifted beyond other men as regards the unfortunate turning of sentences.

“I meant,” I added, “I was afraid I had.”

“Ah, of course,” he said, “you were in our carriage coming down. I was confident I had seen you before. I never forget a face.”

===Chinese===

我谢了他。他对我的道谢连连逊让。他说，他不过是尽了本分，正如英格兰所期许的那样。随后他把我引见给那位年长的爱尔兰人。此人原来是都柏林大学的教授，姓德里克。不管他执教的是哪一门，反正那门学问不怎么需要他把大把时间耗在大学里。他告诉我，他历年夏天都是在库姆里吉斯度过的。

「在库姆里吉斯见到您，我很意外，」我说。「您在约维尔下车的时候，我还以为这一别就再见不着您了。」

我想，在把句子说倒霉这件事上，我的天资确实超出常人。

「我的意思是，」我补了一句，「我怕的是再见不着。」

「啊，当然，」他说，「下山那趟车您跟我们同一节车厢。我就说我从前见过您。我从不忘掉一张脸。」

===Original===

“It would be a kindness,” said Mr. Chase, “if you would forget Garnet’s as now exhibited. You seem to have collected a good deal of the scenery coming through that hedge.”

“I was wondering﻿—” I said. “A wash﻿—if I might﻿—”

“Of course, me boy, of course,” said the professor. “Tom, take Mr. Garnet off to your room, and then we’ll have lunch. You’ll stay to lunch, Mr. Garnet?”

I thanked him, commented on possible inconvenience to his arrangements, was overruled, and went off with my friend the lieutenant to the house. We imprisoned Aunt Elizabeth in the stables, to her profound indignation, gave directions for lunch to be served to her, and made our way to Mr. Chase’s room.

“So you’ve met the professor before?” he said, hospitably laying out a change of raiment for me﻿—we were fortunately much of a height and build.

===Chinese===

「您要是肯把加尼特眼下这张脸忘掉，」蔡斯先生说，「就算积德了。您钻树篱的时候，好像顺路采集了不少风景。」

「我正想——」我说。「洗一把——要是方便的话——」

「当然，俺的孩子，当然，」教授说。「汤姆，带加尼特先生去你房间，回头咱们吃午饭。您留下吃午饭吧，加尼特先生？」

我道了谢，委婉提到怕搅了他们的安排，被一口驳回，便随我这位上尉朋友进了宅子。我们把伊丽莎白姨妈囚进马厩——惹得她老人家义愤填膺——吩咐给她开午饭，然后往蔡斯先生的房间去。

「这么说，你从前见过教授？」他说，殷勤地为我摆出一套换洗衣裳——所幸我们俩个头身量相去无几。

===Original===

“I have never spoken to him,” I said. “We travelled down from London in the same carriage.”

“He’s a dear old boy, if you rub him the right way. But﻿—I’m telling you this for your good and guidance; a man wants a chart in a strange sea﻿—he can cut up rough. And, when he does, he goes off like a four-point-seven and the population for miles round climbs trees. I think, if I were you, I shouldn’t mention Sir Edward Carson at lunch.”

I promised that I would try to avoid the temptation.

“In fact, you’d better keep off Ireland altogether. It’s the safest plan. Any other subject you like. Chatty remarks on Bimetallism would meet with his earnest attention. A lecture on What to do With the Cold Mutton would be welcomed. But not Ireland. Shall we do down?”

We got to know each other at lunch.

===Chinese===

「没说过话，」我说。「我们从伦敦同乘一节车厢下来的。」

「他是个可亲的老伙计，只要你顺着毛摸。不过——我是为你的福祉与指导着想才说的；人到生海域，总得有张海图——他也会翻脸。一旦翻起脸来，他就跟一尊四点七英寸炮似的炸开，方圆几英里的居民纷纷上树。我要是你，午饭桌上千万别提爱德华·卡森爵士。」

我保证尽力抵御这一诱惑。

「其实，爱尔兰的事你干脆整个儿绕开。那是最稳妥的方案。旁的题目随你挑。聊聊复本位制的闲天，他一定洗耳恭听。讲一堂《冷羊肉的处置》，他也竭诚欢迎。就是别提爱尔兰。咱们下去吧？」

午饭桌上我们彼此熟络起来。

===Original===

“Do you hunt hens,” asked Tom Chase, who was mixing the salad﻿—he was one of those men who seemed to do everything a shade better than anyone else﻿—“for amusement or by your doctor’s orders? Many doctors, I believe, insist on it.”

“Neither,” I said, “and especially not for amusement. The fact is, I’ve been lured down here by a friend of mine who has started a chicken farm﻿—”

I was interrupted. All three of them burst out laughing. Tom Chase allowed the vinegar to trickle on to the cloth, missing the salad-bowl by a clear two inches.

“You don’t mean to tell us,” he said, “that you really come from the one and only chicken farm? Why, you’re the man we’ve all been praying to meet for days past. You’re the talk of the town. If you can call Combe Regis a town. Everybody is discussing you. Your methods are new and original, aren’t they?”

“Probably. Ukridge knows nothing about fowls. I know less. He considers it an advantage. He says our minds ought to be unbiased.”

===Chinese===

「您猎母鸡，」汤姆·蔡斯问——他正在拌色拉，他属于那种干什么都比旁人略胜一筹的人——「是为了消遣，还是遵医嘱？据我所知，不少医生力荐此项运动。」

「都不是，」我说，「尤其不是为了消遣。事情是这样的：我是叫一位朋友诱下山来的，他开办了一座养鸡场——」

我被打断了。他们三位哄堂大笑。汤姆·蔡斯手一歪，醋全洒在了桌布上，离色拉盆足有两英寸。

「你该不是要说，」他道，「你当真来自那独一无二、绝无分号的养鸡场？哎呀，你就是我们大伙儿这几天祷告上苍想遇的人！你是本镇的头号话题——如果库姆里吉斯也配称镇的话。人人都在议论你。你们的法子又新又原创，对吧？」

「多半是。厄克里奇对鸡一窍不通。我懂得更少。他认为这是优势。他说我们的头脑应当不带成见。」

===Original===

“Ukridge!” said the professor. “That was the name old Dawlish, the grocer, said. I never forget a name. He is the gentleman who lectures on the management of poultry? You do not?”

I hastened to disclaim any such feat. I had never really approved of these infernal talks on the art of chicken-farming which Ukridge had dropped into the habit of delivering when anybody visited our farm. I admit that it was a pleasing spectacle to see my managing director in a pink shirt without a collar and very dirty flannel trousers lecturing the intelligent native; but I had a feeling that the thing tended to expose our ignorance to men who had probably had to do with fowls from their cradle up.

“His lectures are very popular,” said Phyllis Derrick with a little splutter of mirth.

“He enjoys them,” I said.

“Look here, Garnet,” said Tom Chase, “I hope you won’t consider all these questions impertinent, but you’ve no notion of the thrilling interest we all take﻿—at a distance﻿—in your farm. We have been talking of nothing else for a week. I have dreamed of it three nights running. Is Mr. Ukridge doing this as a commercial speculation, or is he an eccentric millionaire?”

===Chinese===

「厄克里奇！」教授说。「杂货铺的老道利什提的就是这个名字。我从不忘掉一个名字。就是在养鸡经上发表高论的那位先生？不是您？」

我赶紧声明无此功绩。厄克里奇有个习惯，每逢有人来我们农庄，他就要就养鸡之道发一通议论；对这类要命的讲演，我其实从来不曾赞成。我承认，眼看我们的总经理身穿无领的粉红衬衫和脏得可以的法兰绒长裤，向聪明的本地人布道，场面煞是可观；可我总有一种感觉：当着一帮多半从摇篮里就同鸡打交道的汉子，这种事容易露怯。

「他的讲座大受欢迎，」菲莉斯·德里克说着，噗嗤笑出声来。

「他自己乐在其中，」我说。

「我说，加尼特，」汤姆·蔡斯道，「希望你别嫌这些问题唐突，可你想象不到我们对贵场——远远地——怀着多么惊心动魄的兴趣。我们一个礼拜来别的什么都没谈。我连着三夜梦见它。厄克里奇先生这是商业投机呢，还是一位古怪的百万富翁？」

===Original===

“He’s not a millionaire yet, but I believe he intends to be one shortly, with the assistance of the fowls. But you mustn’t look on me as in any way responsible for the arrangements at the farm. I am merely a labourer. The brainwork of the business lies in Ukridge’s department. As a matter of fact, I came down here principally in search of golf.”

“Golf?” said Professor Derrick, with the benevolent approval of the enthusiast towards a brother. “I’m glad you play golf. We must have a round together.”

“As soon as ever my professional duties will permit,” I said gratefully.

---

===Chinese===

「眼下还不是百万富翁，不过我相信他打算借助群鸡在短期内成为一位。可你千万别把农庄里的任何安排算到我头上。我不过是个苦力。这门生意的脑力活归厄克里奇掌管。说实话，我下山主要为了打高尔夫。」

「高尔夫？」德里克教授说，带着一位爱好者对同道的那种嘉许。「你打高尔夫，我很高兴。咱们务必同打一轮。」

「一等我职责许可，」我感激地说。

---

===Original===

There was croquet after lunch﻿—a game of which I am a poor performer. Phyllis Derrick and I played the professor and Tom Chase. Chase was a little better than myself; the professor, by dint of extreme earnestness and care, managed to play a fair game; and Phyllis was an expert.

“I was reading a book,” she said, as we stood together watching the professor shaping at his ball at the other end of the lawn, “by an author of the same surname as you, Mr. Garnet. Is he a relation of yours?”

“My name is Jeremy, Miss Derrick.”

“Oh, you wrote it?” She turned a little pink. “Then you must have﻿—oh, nothing.”

“I couldn’t help it, I’m afraid.”

===Chinese===

午饭过后是槌球——这门玩意儿我玩得很糟。菲莉斯·德里克和我对打教授同汤姆·蔡斯。蔡斯比我略强；教授凭十二分的认真和仔细，勉强打出个平顺；菲莉斯则是行家。

「我先前读一本书，」我们并肩站着，望草坪那一头教授正对着球摆架势，她说，「作者同您同姓，加尼特先生。是您的亲眷么？」

「我的名字叫杰里米，德里克小姐。」

「哦，是您写的？」她的脸微微红了红。「那您一定——哦，没什么。」

「只怕是身不由己。」

===Original===

“Did you know what I was going to say?”

“I guessed. You were going to say that I must have heard your criticisms in the train. You were very lenient, I thought.”

“I didn’t like your heroine.”

“No. What is a ‘creature,’ Miss Derrick?”

“Pamela in your book is a ‘creature,’ ” she replied unsatisfactorily.

===Chinese===

「您知道我本来要说什么？」

「猜到了。您本来要说，我一定在火车上听见了您的批评。依我看，您已经很宽厚了。」

「我不喜欢您的女主人公。」

「是么。什么是『货色』，德里克小姐？」

「您书里的帕梅拉就是个『货色』，」她答得不能令人满意。

===Original===

Shortly after this the game came somehow to an end. I do not understand the intricacies of croquet. But Phyllis did something brilliant and remarkable with the balls, and we adjourned for tea. The sun was setting as I left to return to the farm, with Aunt Elizabeth stored neatly in a basket in my hand. The air was deliciously cool, and full of that strange quiet which follows soothingly on the skirts of a broiling midsummer afternoon. Far away, seeming to come from another world, a sheep-bell tinkled, deepening the silence. Alone in a sky of the palest blue there gleamed a small, bright star.

I addressed this star.

“She was certainly very nice to me. Very nice indeed.” The star said nothing.

“On the other hand, I take it that, having had a decent upbringing, she would have been equally polite to any other man whom she had happened to meet at her father’s house. Moreover, I don’t feel altogether easy in my mind about that naval chap. I fear the worst.”

===Chinese===

此后不久，那局球不知怎么就收了场。槌球的错综我搞不懂。反正菲莉斯拿那些球干出了一件辉煌出众的事，我们便移师喝茶。我动身回农庄时，夕阳正在西沉，伊丽莎白姨妈整整齐齐地收在我手里的篮子里。空气凉爽宜人，弥漫着一种奇异的静谧——它温存地缀在酷暑仲夏午后的裙裾边上。远处，仿佛来自另一个世界，一枚羊铃叮咚，愈发衬深了这份寂静。极淡的蓝天里，孤零零地闪烁着一颗小小的亮星。

我对这颗星发话了。

「她方才对我实在很好。实在很好。」星星一言不发。

「另一方面，我揣想，她既受过体面的教养，那么换成在任何旁人于她父亲家中偶遇，她也会同样客气。再者，我心里对那个海军小子总不很踏实。我怕凶多吉少。」

===Original===

The star winked.

“He calls her Phyllis,” I said.

“*Charawk!*” chuckled Aunt Elizabeth from her basket, in that beastly cynical, satirical way which has made her so disliked by all right-thinking people.

===Chinese===

星星眨了眨眼。

「他管她叫菲莉斯，」我说。

「*咯嘎！*」伊丽莎白姨妈在篮子里嗤笑，用的正是那副可恶的愤世嫉俗、冷嘲热讽的腔调——正派的各界人士所以如此讨厌她，缘由全在于此。

## XXII The Storm Breaks / 二十二 风暴骤起

===Original===

Rather to my surprise, the next morning passed off uneventfully. Our knocker advertised no dun. Our lawn remained untrodden by hobnailed boots. By lunchtime I had come to the conclusion that the expected Trouble would not occur that day, and I felt that I might well leave my post for the afternoon, while I went to the professor’s to pay my respects. The professor was out when I arrived. Phyllis was in, and it was not till the evening that I started for the farm again.

As I approached, the sound of voices smote my ears.

I stopped. I could hear Beale speaking. Then came the rich notes of Vickers, the butcher. Then Beale again. Then Dawlish the grocer. Then a chorus.

The storm had burst, and in my absence.

===Chinese===

颇出我意料，第二天上午竟平安无事地过去了。门环上没有登门索债的。草坪上也未见钉靴踏访。到午饭时分，我已得出结论：预期中的大麻烦今天不会来了。我觉得下午不妨擅离一下岗位，去教授府上拜望。我到时教授出去了。菲莉斯在家，直到傍晚我才动身回农庄。

走近农庄，人声鼎沸，直灌进我耳朵。

我停下脚步。我听得见比尔在讲话。接着是维克斯——那位屠户——圆润的嗓音。又是比尔。又是杂货商道利什。然后是大合唱。

风暴爆发了，还偏偏趁我不在。

===Original===

I blushed for myself. I was in command, and I had deserted the fort in time of need. What must the faithful Hired Man be thinking of me? Probably he placed me, as he had placed Ukridge, in the ragged ranks of those who have Shot the Moon.

Fortunately, having just come from the professor’s, I was in the costume which of all my wardrobe was most calculated to impress. To a casual observer I should probably suggest wealth and respectability. I stopped for a moment to cool myself, for, as is my habit when pleased with life, I had been walking fast; then opened the gate and strode in, trying to look as opulent as possible.

It was an animated scene that met my eyes. In the middle of the lawn stood the devoted Beale, a little more flushed than I had seen him hitherto, parleying with a burly and excited young man without a coat.
