Grouped round the pair were some dozen men, young, middle-aged, and old, all talking their hardest. I could distinguish nothing of what they were saying. I noticed that Beale’s left cheekbone was a little discoloured, and there was a hard, dogged expression on his face. He, too, was in his shirtsleeves.

My entry created no sensation. Nobody, apparently, had heard the latch click, and nobody had caught sight of me. Their eyes were fixed on the young man and Beale. I stood at the gate, and watched them.

===Chinese===

我替自己脸红。我执掌帅印，却在危急关头弃了要塞。那位忠心耿耿的雇工此刻会怎么想我？多半他把我也归了类——同厄克里奇一道，编进趁夜跑路者的褴褛行列。

所幸我刚从教授府上过来，一身装束在我的全部行头里最足以慑人。在漫不经心的旁观者眼里，我多半意味着财富与体面。我停了片刻让自己冷静——一如我对人生满意时的老习惯，我走得飞快——然后推开院门，大步而入，竭力摆出财大气粗的架势。

映入眼帘的是一幕生气勃勃的场景。草坪中央立着忠勇的比尔，比我历来所见的略多几分红光，正与一个没穿外衣的壮硕青年交涉。两人周围聚着十来个人，少的、中的、老的，个个拼命说着话。他们说些什么，我一个字也分辨不出。我注意到比尔左颧骨上有一块淡淡的乌青，脸上是一种硬邦邦的倔强神情。他也只穿着衬衫。

我的进场没有引起轰动。显然没人听见门闩响，也没人瞧见我。众人的眼睛都钉在那青年同比尔身上。我立在门口，观望着。

===Original===

There seemed to have been trouble already. Looking more closely, I perceived sitting on the grass apart a second young man. His face was obscured by a dirty pocket handkerchief, with which he dabbed tenderly at his features. Every now and then the shirt-sleeved young man flung his hand towards him with an indignant gesture, talking hard the while. It did not need a preternaturally keen observer to deduce what had happened. Beale must have fallen out with the young man who was sitting on the grass and smitten him; and now his friend had taken up the quarrel.

“Now this,” I said to myself, “is rather interesting. Here, in this one farm, we have the only three known methods of dealing with duns. Beale is evidently an exponent of the violent method. Ukridge is an apostle of Evasion. I shall try Conciliation. I wonder which of us will be the most successful.”

Meanwhile, not to spoil Beale’s efforts by allowing him too little scope for experiment, I refrained from making my presence known, and continued to stand by the gate, an interested spectator.

Things were evidently moving now. The young man’s gestures became more vigorous. The dogged look on Beale’s face deepened. The comments of the Ring increased in point and pungency.

===Chinese===

看来已经出过乱子了。细看之下，我发觉草地上还坐着另一个青年，离人群稍远。他的脸埋在一块脏手绢后面，正拿它轻柔地往五官上按。那个穿衬衫的青年不时朝他愤然一挥手，嘴上滔滔不绝。这不需要眼力过人也能推断出方才发生了什么。比尔准是同坐在草地上的那位闹翻了、动手打了他；如今他的朋友又接过了这场官司。

「有意思，」我自忖，「就在这一座农场上，我们凑齐了世人已知的对付债主的全套三种法门。比尔显然是暴力派的健将。厄克里奇是逃避门的宗师。我来试试怀柔。倒要看看咱们仨谁的成绩最佳。」

与此同时，为了不致因给他的实验空间太小而糟蹋了比尔的努力，我暂不亮相，继续倚门旁观，做一名兴致盎然的看客。

局面显然在动了。青年的手势愈发激昂。比尔脸上的倔强愈发深沉。外圈看众的点评愈发又扎又辣。

===Original===

“What did you hit him for, then?”

The question was put, always in the same words and with the same air of quiet triumph, at intervals of thirty seconds by a little man in a snuff-coloured suit with a purple tie. Nobody ever answered him, or appeared to listen to him, but he seemed each time to think that he had clinched the matter and cornered his opponent.

Other voices chimed in.

“You hit him, Charlie. Go on. You hit him.”

“We’ll have the law.”

===Chinese===

「那你干嘛打他？」

这句话由一个穿鼻烟色西服、系紫领带的小个子提出，措辞永远相同，神气永远是安静的得意，每三十秒来一轮。从来没有人答他，也没有人像在听，可他每一回都仿佛觉得自己一锤定音、把对手逼进了死角。

别的嗓音也来凑热闹。

「你打了他，查理。说呀。你打了他。」

「咱们要告到官府。」

===Original===

“Go on, Charlie.”

Flushed with the favour of the many-headed, Charlie now proceeded from threats to action. His right fist swung round suddenly. But Beale was on the alert. He ducked sharply, and the next moment Charlie was sitting on the ground beside his fallen friend. A hush fell on the Ring, and the little man in the purple tie was left repeating his formula without support.

I advanced. It seemed to me that the time had come to be conciliatory. Charlie was struggling to his feet, obviously anxious for a second round, and Beale was getting into position once more. In another five minutes conciliation would be out of the question.

“What’s all this?” I said.

I may mention here that I do not propose to inflict dialect upon the reader. If he has borne with my narrative thus far, I look on him as a friend, and feel that he deserves consideration. I may not have brought out the fact with sufficient emphasis in the foregoing pages, but nevertheless I protest that I have a conscience. Not so much as a “thiccy” shall he find.

===Chinese===

「上啊，查理。」

查理得了芸芸众生的喝彩，热血上头，由口头威胁转入实际行动。他的右拳猛地抡将过来。但比尔早有防备。他猛一低头，下一刻查理已经坐在了草地上，挨着他那位倒下的朋友。一圈看众鸦雀无声，只剩那个系紫领带的小个子还在无人捧场地复诵他的公式。

我走上前去。我看，行怀柔之道的时机到了。查理正挣扎着爬起来，分明巴望着再战一合，比尔也在重新摆架势。再过五分钟，怀柔就要无从谈起了。

「这是唱的哪一出？」我说。

趁此交代一句：我不打算拿土腔土调折磨读者。他既已容忍我的叙述到了这个份上，我便把他看作朋友，觉得他值得体恤。这一点我在前文也许强调得不够响亮，然而我郑重声明：我是有良心的。他连半个「这嗒」也休想找到。

===Original===

My advent caused a stir. Excited men left Beale, and rallied round me. Charlie, rising to his feet, found himself dethroned from his position of Man of the Moment, and stood blinking at the setting sun and opening and shutting his mouth. There was a buzz of conversation.

“Don’t all speak at once, please,” I said. “I can’t possibly follow what you say. Perhaps you will tell me what you want?”

I singled out a short, stout man in grey. He wore the largest whiskers ever seen on human face.

“It’s like this, sir. We all of us want to know where we are.”

“I can tell you that,” I said, “you’re on our lawn, and I should be much obliged if you would stop digging your heels into it.”

===Chinese===

我的驾到激起一阵骚动。激动的人们撇下比尔，朝我聚拢。查理站起身来，发现自己已从「当下红人」的宝座上被撵了下来，站在那儿对着西沉的太阳直眨眼，嘴一开一合。人声嗡嗡。

「请大家别一齐开口，」我说。「你们说什么我根本跟不上。哪位愿意告诉我，你们想要什么？」

我挑中一个矮胖的灰衣人。他脸上留着人类脸上所能见到的最大一副络腮胡。

「是这么回事，先生。我们大伙儿都想知道自己的处境。」

「这个我答得上，」我说，「你们正站在我们家草坪上，劳驾别再用脚跟往里踩。」

===Original===

This was not, I suppose, Conciliation in the strictest and best sense of the word; but the thing had to be said. It is the duty of every good citizen to do his best to score off men with whiskers.

“You don’t understand me, sir,” he said excitedly. “When I said we didn’t know where we were, it was a manner of speaking. We want to know how we stand.”

“On your heels,” I replied gently, “as I pointed out before.”

“I am Brass, sir, of Axminster. My account with Mr. Ukridge is ten pounds eight shillings and fourpence. I want to know﻿—”

The whole strength of the company now joined in.

===Chinese===

我承认，严格意义、上乘意义上的怀柔，这大概算不得；可这话非说不可。每个好公民都有义务，尽其所能地刺一刺留络腮胡的人。

「您误会了，先生，」他激动地说。「我说我们不知道自己的处境，那是一种修辞。我们想知道咱们之间怎么个算法。」

「站在你们的脚跟上，」我温和地答道，「如我方才指出的。」

「我是布拉斯，先生，阿克斯明斯特来的。厄克里奇先生欠我十镑八先令又四便士。我想知道——」

全体人马此刻一齐加入了。

===Original===

“You know me, Mr. Garnet. Appleby, in the High﻿—” (Voice lost in the general roar).

“… and eightpence.”

“My account with Mr. Uk﻿ ﻿…”

“… settle﻿ ﻿…”

“I represent Bodger﻿ ﻿…”

A diversion occurred at this point. Charlie, who had long been eyeing Beale sourly, dashed at him with swinging fists, and was knocked down again. The whole trend of the meeting altered once more, Conciliation became a drug. Violence was what the public wanted. Beale had three fights in rapid succession. I was helpless. Instinct prompted me to join the fray; but prudence told me that such a course would be fatal.

===Chinese===

「您认识我，加尼特先生。高街上的阿普尔比——」（嗓音淹没在众人的吼声里。）

「……又八便士。」

「厄克里奇先生欠我……」

「……结账……」

「鄙人代表博杰……」

正在这时出了岔子。查理早就气恨恨地拿眼瞟着比尔，此刻抡开双拳直扑过去，又被撂倒了。大会的走向再度全盘扭转，怀柔成了冷背货。暴力才是众望所归。比尔接连快打了三架。我毫无办法。本能怂恿我加入战团；理智却告诫我，那样干等于自取灭亡。

===Original===

At last, in a lull, I managed to catch the Hired Retainer by the arm, as he drew back from the prostrate form of his latest victim. “Drop it, Beale,” I whispered hotly, “drop it. We shall never manage these people if you knock them about. Go indoors, and stay there while I talk to them.”

“Mr. Garnet, sir,” said he, the light of battle dying out of his eyes, “it’s ’ard. It’s cruel ’ard. I ain’t ’ad a turn-up, not to *call* a turn-up, since I’ve been a time-expired man. I ain’t hitting of ’em, Mr. Garnet, sir, not hard I ain’t. That there first one of ’em he played me dirty, hittin’ at me when I wasn’t looking. They can’t say as I started it.”

“That’s all right, Beale,” I said soothingly. “I know it wasn’t your fault, and I know it’s hard on you to have to stop, but I wish you would go indoors. I must talk to these men, and we shan’t have a moment’s peace while you’re here. Cut along.”

“Very well, sir. But it’s ’ard. Mayn’t I ’ave just one go at that Charlie, Mr. Garnet?” he asked wistfully.

“No, no. Go in.”

===Chinese===

好容易在一间歇里，趁他从最新一位牺牲品的横陈之躯旁退开，我一把攥住受雇忠仆的胳膊。「收手，比尔，」我气急败坏地低声道，「收手。你把人都打跑了，这群人咱们就永远没法对付。进屋去，我同他们讲话的时候，你在里头待着。」

「加尼特先生，您哪，」他说，眼里的战火渐渐熄灭，「这事憋屈。憋屈得狠。俺自从兵役期满以来，就没打过一场——够格叫打架的架。俺没真打他们，加尼特先生，您哪，没下死手，真没有。头里那一个，他使阴招，趁俺没瞧见就动的手。他们可不能说是俺挑的头。」

「这就对了，比尔，」我抚慰道，「我知道不怪你，我也知道叫你就此收手难为你了，可你还是进屋去吧。我必须同这些人讲话，你在这儿，咱们一刻不得安生。快去。」

「成，先生。可这事憋屈。就不能叫俺再跟那个查理来一合么，加尼特先生？」他一脸巴望地问。

「不行，不行。进去。」

===Original===

“And if they goes for you, sir, and tries to wipe the face off you?”

“They won’t, they won’t. If they do, I’ll shout for you.”

He went reluctantly into the house, and I turned again to my audience.

“If you will kindly be quiet for a moment﻿—” I said.

“I am Appleby, Mr. Garnet, in the High Street. Mr. Ukridge﻿—”

===Chinese===

「要是他们冲您来呢，先生，要抹您的脸呢？」

「不会的，不会的。真要是那样，我喊你。」

他老大不情愿地进了屋，我转身重对我的听众。

「劳驾，诸位静一静——」我说。

「我是阿普尔比，加尼特先生，高街的。厄克里奇先生——」

===Original===

“Eighteen pounds fourteen shillings﻿—”

“Kindly glance﻿—”

I waved my hands wildly above my head.

“Stop! stop! stop!” I shouted.

The babble continued, but diminished gradually in volume. Through the trees, as I waited, I caught a glimpse of the sea. I wished I was out on the Cob, where beyond these voices there was peace. My head was beginning to ache, and I felt faint for want of food.

===Chinese===

「十八镑十四先令——」

「请过目——」

我把双手在头顶疯狂挥舞。

「停！停！停！」我喊。

嗡嗡声还在继续，音量却渐渐低下去。等候之际，我透过树影瞥见了海。我真盼自己远在科布防波堤上，那边，在这些嗓音之外，自有安宁。我的头开始疼了，还饿得发慌。

===Original===

“Gentlemen,” I cried, as the noise died away.

The latch of the gate clicked. I looked up, and saw a tall thin young man in a frock coat and silk hat enter the garden. It was the first time I had seen the costume in the country.

He approached me.

“Mr. Ukridge, sir?” he said.

“My name is Garnet. Mr. Ukridge is away at the moment.”

===Chinese===

「诸位，」喧嚣渐息时我喊道。

门闩咔哒一响。我抬头，看见一个穿长礼服、戴礼帽的瘦高青年走进园子。这种行头，我在乡间还是头一回见。

他朝我走来。

「厄克里奇先生？」他说。

「敝姓加尼特。厄克里奇先生眼下不在。」

===Original===

“I come from Whiteley’s, Mr. Garnet. Our Mr. Blenkinsop having written on several occasions to Mr. Ukridge, calling his attention to the fact that his account has been allowed to mount to a considerable figure, and having received no satisfactory reply, desired me to visit him. I am sorry that he is not at home.”

“So am I,” I said with feeling.

“Do you expect him to return shortly?”

“No,” I said, “I do not.”

He was looking curiously at the expectant band of duns. I forestalled his question.

===Chinese===

「我从怀特利来，加尼特先生。敝行的布伦金索普先生几次致函厄克里奇先生，提请注意他的账目已积至相当数目，均未获满意答复，故而派我登门造访。很遗憾他不在家。」

「我也遗憾，」我由衷地说。

「您预计他很快就回来么？」

「不，」我说，「我不作此想。」

他好奇地打量着那群引颈以待的债主。我抢在了他的问题前头。

===Original===

“Those are some of Mr. Ukridge’s creditors,” I said. “I am just about to address them. Perhaps you will take a seat. The grass is quite dry. My remarks will embrace you as well as them.”

Comprehension came into his eyes, and the natural man in him peeped through the polish.

“Great Scott, has he done a bunk?” he cried.

“To the best of my knowledge, yes,” I said.

He whistled.

===Chinese===

「这几位是厄克里奇先生的部分债主，」我说。「我正要对他们训话。您不妨也找个座。草地相当干。我的话头会把您同他们一并囊括在内。」

他眼里透出会意，那层光鲜的漆面底下，自然人探出头来窥了一眼。

「我的老天，他卷包跑了？」他叫道。

「据我所知，跑了。」我说。

他打了个呼哨。

===Original===

I turned again to the local talent.

“Gentlemen,” I shouted.

“Hear, hear,” said some idiot.

“Gentlemen, I intend to be quite frank with you. We must decide just how matters stand between us. (A voice: Where’s Ukridge?) Mr. Ukridge left for London suddenly (bitter laughter) yesterday afternoon. Personally I think he will come back very shortly.”

Hoots of derision greeted this prophecy. I resumed.

===Chinese===

我又转向本地的英才们。

「诸位，」我喊道。

「好哇，好哇，」哪个蠢货说。

「诸位，我打算对你们开诚布公。咱们必须把彼此的账目摆个明白。（一个声音：厄克里奇呢？）厄克里奇先生昨天下午突然动身去了伦敦（一阵刻毒的哄笑）。敝人以为，他不久就会回来。」

这番预言迎来一片轻蔑的嘘声。我继续讲。

===Original===

“I fail to see your object in coming here. I have nothing for you. I couldn’t pay your bills if I wanted to.”

It began to be borne upon me that I was becoming unpopular.

“I am here simply as Mr. Ukridge’s guest,” I proceeded. After all, why should I spare the man? “I have nothing whatever to do with his business affairs. I refuse absolutely to be regarded as in any way indebted to you. I am sorry for you. You have my sympathy. That is all I can give you, sympathy﻿—and good advice.”

Dissatisfaction. I was getting myself disliked. And I had meant to be so conciliatory, to speak to these unfortunates words of cheer which should be as olive oil poured into a wound. For I really did sympathise with them. I considered that Ukridge had used them disgracefully. But I was irritated. My head ached abominably.

“Then am I to tell our Mr. Blenkinsop,” asked the frock-coated one, “that the money is not and will not be forthcoming?”

===Chinese===

「我不明白诸位此来所为何事。我一无所有。就算想付你们的账，也付不出。」

我渐渐觉察到，我正在变得不受欢迎。

「我留在此地，纯以厄克里奇先生客人的身份，」我接着说。反正，我何必替那人遮掩？「他的生意往来，我一概无关。我绝对拒绝被视作欠了诸位的任何东西。我很同情诸位。诸位有我的同情。我能给的只有这些——同情，外加忠告。」

群情不满。我正在把自己弄到人憎鬼厌。而我原本存心要那么怀柔，要给这些不幸的人说几句宽心话，如浇进伤口的橄榄油一般。我是真心同情他们。我认为厄克里奇待他们太不光彩。可我火上来了。我的头疼得要命。

「那么，我是否可以回复敝行的布伦金索普先生，」穿礼服的那位问，「款项没有、也永远不会有了？」

===Original===

“When next you smoke a quiet cigar with your Mr. Blenkinsop,” I replied courteously, “and find conversation flagging, I rather think I *should* say something of the sort.”

“We shall, of course, instruct our solicitors at once to institute legal proceedings against your Mr. Ukridge.”

“Don’t call him my Mr. Ukridge. You can do whatever you please.”

“That is your last word on the subject?”

“I hope so. But I fear not.”

===Chinese===

「下回您同贵行的布伦金索普先生静静抽雪茄、话头不济的时候，」我彬彬有礼地答道，「我想这类的意思，*不妨*说说。」

「我们当然即刻委托律师，对贵方的厄克里奇先生提起诉讼。」

「别叫他贵方的厄克里奇先生。你们爱怎么办怎么办。」

「这就是您在这件事上的最后答复？」

「但愿是。只怕不是。」

===Original===

“Where’s our money?” demanded a discontented voice from the crowd.

An idea struck me.

“Beale!” I shouted.

Out came the Hired Retainer at the double. I fancy he thought that his help was needed to save me from my friends.

He slowed down, seeing me as yet unassaulted.

===Chinese===

「咱们的钱呢？」人群里一个愤愤的声音喝问。

我灵机一动。

「比尔！」我喊。

受雇的忠仆跑步出列。我揣测，他以为需要他出手，把我从我的朋友们手里救出来。

见我还未挨打，他放慢了脚步。

===Original===

“Sir?” he said.

“Isn’t there a case of that whisky left somewhere, Beale?”

I had struck the right note. There was a hush of pleased anticipation among the audience.

“Yes, sir. One.”

“Then bring it out here and open it.”

===Chinese===

「先生？」他说。

「比尔，那威士忌是不是还剩一箱，搁在哪儿？」

我这一记敲在了正音上。听众中响起一片喜滋滋的期待的静默。

「是，先生。一箱。」

「那就搬出来，当场开了。」

===Original===

Beale looked pained.

“For *them*, sir!” he ejaculated.

“Yes. Hurry up.”

He hesitated, then without a word went into the house. A hearty cheer went up as he reappeared with the case. I proceeded indoors in search of glasses and water.

Coming out, I realised my folly in having left Beale alone with our visitors even for a minute. A brisk battle was raging between him and a man whom I did not remember to have seen before. The frock-coated young man was looking on with pale fear stamped upon his face; but the rest of the crowd were shouting advice and encouragement: and to my surprise all the encouragement was being given to Beale. How, I wondered, had he pacified the mob?

===Chinese===

比尔一脸痛心。

「给*他们*喝，先生！」他失声道。

「正是。快点。」

他犹豫了一下，一言未发进了屋。他抱着箱子再露面时，响起一阵衷心的欢呼。我进屋去找杯子和水。

出来时我才明白，把比尔单独同我们的来客撂下哪怕一分钟都是失策。他正同一个我不记得见过的人斗得火热。穿礼服的青年面色惨白地旁观，脸上印着恐惧；其余的群众却在喊话支招、呐喊助威：而且叫我吃惊的是，所有的助威全是给比尔的。我纳闷，他是怎么把暴众安抚下来的？

===Original===

I soon discovered. As I ran up as quickly as I could, hampered as I was by the jugs and glasses, Beale knocked his man out with the clean precision of the experienced boxer; and the crowd explained in chorus that it was the potboy, from the Net and Mackerel. Like everything else, the whisky had not been paid for, and the potboy, arriving just as the case was being opened, had made a gallant effort to save it from being distributed free to his fellow-citizens. By the time he came to, the glasses were circulating merrily; and, on observing this, he accepted the situation philosophically enough, and took his turn and turn about with the others.

Everybody was now in excellent fettle. The only malcontents were Beale, whose heart plainly bled at the waste of good Scotch whisky, and the frock-coated young man, who was still pallid.

I was just congratulating myself, as I eyed the revellers, on having achieved a masterstroke of strategy, when that demon Charlie, his defeat, I suppose, still rankling, made a suggestion. From his point of view a timely and ingenious suggestion.

Filled with whisky and viciousness, he proposed that the company should sack the place.

===Chinese===

我很快就发现了。我提着水壶杯子尽快跑过去时，比尔正以老拳师的干净利落把他的对手击倒；众人齐声解释说，那是「网与鲭鱼」的小厮。同别的东西一样，那威士忌原是没付钱的；小厮恰好在开箱当口赶到，奋不顾身地想抢救它，免得白白散给他的同胞们。等他苏醒过来，杯子已经欢畅地流转开了；他见状倒也达观，认了这局面，同大伙儿轮着喝了起来。

这会儿人人兴致极佳。唯独两个不满分子：比尔，他的心明摆着在为糟蹋上好苏格兰威士忌滴血；还有那位穿礼服的青年，脸依旧煞白。

我正望着这伙狂欢者，暗自庆贺自己下了一着战略妙手，那个恶魔查理——想必他的败绩仍在心里作痛——提了一条建议。从他的观点看，一条及时而巧妙建议。

威士忌和恶气灌满了肚皮，他提议把这地方洗劫一空。

===Original===

“We can’t see the colour of our money,” he said pithily, “but we can have our own back.”

That settled it. The battle was over. The most skilful general must sometimes recognise defeat. I recognised it then, and threw up my hand. I could do nothing further with them. I had done my best for the farm. I could do no more.

I lit my pipe, and strolled into the paddock.

Chaos followed. Indoors and out-of-doors they raged without check. Even Beale gave the thing up. He knocked Charlie into a flowerbed, and then disappeared in the direction of the kitchen.

===Chinese===

「咱们连自己钱的成色都见不着，」他言简意赅地说，「可咱们能自己讨个补偿。」

这一下大局已定。战斗结束了。最高明的统帅有时也得认输。我彼时便认了，摊开了手。我再拿他们没有办法。我为农庄已尽了力。再无可为了。

我点上烟斗，溜达进了围场。

大乱接踵而来。屋里屋外，他们横行无阻。连比尔也撒手不管了。他把查理一拳打进花坛，便朝厨房方向消失了。

===Original===

It was growing dusk. From inside the house came faint sounds of bibulous mirth, as the sacking party emptied the rooms of their contents. In the fowl-run a hen was crooning sleepily in its coop. It was a very soft, liquid, soothing sound.

Presently out came the invaders with their loot, one with a picture, another with a vase, another bearing the gramophone upside down. They were singing in many keys and times.

Then I heard somebody﻿—Charlie again, it seemed to me﻿—propose a raid on the fowl-run.

The fowls had had their moments of unrest since they had been our property, but what they had gone through with us was peace compared with what befell them then. Not even on the second evening of our visit, when we had run unmeasured miles in pursuit of them, had there been such confusion. Roused abruptly from their beauty-sleep they fled in all directions. Their pursuers, roaring with laughter, staggered after them. They tumbled over one another. The summer evening was made hideous with the noise of them.

===Chinese===

暮色渐浓。屋里传出微弱的、酒酣耳热的欢笑，劫掠队正把各房间的存货搬空。鸡圈里，一只母鸡在笼中睡意朦胧地咕哝。那是一种极轻柔、水润、抚慰人的声音。

不多时，入侵者们抱着战利品出来了，一个抱画，一个捧花瓶，还有一个倒提着留声机。他们用五花八门的调门和节拍唱着歌。

接着我听见有人——听来又是查理——提议去袭击鸡圈。

自打群鸡归了我们所有，它们也有过几个不得安生的时刻，可它们在我们手里受的罪，同彼时彼刻降临到头上的相比，简直就是太平岁月。即便在我们到访的第二天晚上——那一晚我们追着它们跑了不知多少英里——也不曾有这样的大乱。它们从美容觉里被猛然惊醒，四散奔逃。追兵们哄笑着，跌跌撞撞地尾随。他们你绊我、我绊你。夏夜被这一片喧嚣搅得乌烟瘴气。

===Original===

“Disgraceful, sir. Is it not disgraceful!” said a voice in my ear.

The young man from Whiteley’s stood beside me. He did not look happy. His forehead was damp. Somebody seemed to have stepped on his hat, and his coat was smeared with mould.

I was turning to answer him, when from the dusk in the direction of the house came a sudden roar. A passionate appeal to the world in general to tell the speaker what all this meant.

There was only one man of my acquaintance with a voice like that.

I walked without hurry towards him.

“Good evening, Ukridge,” I said.

===Chinese===

「不成体统啊，先生。岂不骇人听闻！」我耳边一个声音说。

怀特利来的青年立在我旁边。他看上去不甚快活。额头汗津津的。礼帽似乎叫谁踩了一脚，礼服上抹着霉斑。

我正要转身作答，暮色里从宅子方向传来一声怒吼。一声面向全人类的恳切呼吁，请世人评评理，告诉他这一切究竟是怎么回事。

我的相识里，只有一个人有这样一副嗓门。

我不慌不忙地朝他走去。

「晚上好，厄克里奇，」我说。

## XVIII Ukridge Gives Me Advice / 十八 厄克里奇给我进言

===Original===

Hours after﻿—or so it seemed to me﻿—we reached the spot at which our ways divided. We stopped, and I felt as if I had been suddenly cast back into the workaday world from some distant and pleasanter planet. I think Phyllis must have felt much the same sensation, for we both became on the instant intensely practical and businesslike.

“But about your father,” I said.

“That’s the difficulty.”

“He won’t give us his consent?”

===Chinese===

好几个钟头之后——至少我自觉如此——我们到了分道之处。我们停下脚步，我觉得自己仿佛从某颗遥远而更宜人的星球，猛然被扔回了柴米油盐的人间。我想菲莉斯必定也有同感，因为我们俩当即都变得无比务实、公事公办起来。

「可是你父亲，」我说。

「难就难在这儿。」

「他不肯应允？」

===Original===

“I’m afraid he wouldn’t dream of it.”

“You can’t persuade him?”

“I can in most things, but not in this. You see, even if nothing had happened, he wouldn’t like to lose me just yet, because of Norah.”

“Norah?”

“My sister. She’s going to be married in October. I wonder if we shall ever be as happy as they will.”

===Chinese===

「我怕他想都不会想。」

「你劝不动他？」

「多数事情劝得动，这件不行。你瞧，就算什么事都没出过，他也舍不得这会儿就失去我，因为诺拉。」

「诺拉？」

「我妹妹。她十月出嫁。也不知咱们将来能不能有他们那么幸福。」

===Original===

“Happy! They will be miserable compared with us. Not that I know who the man is.”

“Why, Tom, of course. Do you mean to say you really didn’t know?”

“Tom! Tom Chase?”

“Of course.”

I gasped.

===Chinese===

「幸福！跟咱们一比，他们只有伤心的份儿。倒不是我知道那男人是谁。」

「嗐，当然是汤姆。你该不是说，你当真一直不知道吧？」

「汤姆！汤姆·蔡斯？」

「当然。」

我倒抽一口气。

===Original===

“Well, I’m﻿—hanged,” I said. “When I think of the torments I’ve been through because of that wretched man, and all for nothing, I don’t know what to say.”

“Don’t you like Tom?”

“Very much. I always did. But I was awfully jealous of him.”

“You weren’t! How silly of you.”

“Of course I was. He was always about with you, and called you Phyllis, and generally behaved as if you and he were the heroine and hero of a musical comedy, so what else could I think? I heard you singing duets after dinner once. I drew the worst conclusions.”

===Chinese===

「好嘛，我——服了，」我说。「一想起我为那个冤家受过的苦，到头来全属白受，我都不知道说什么好。」

「你不喜欢汤姆？」

「非常喜欢。一向如此。可我妒忌他妒忌得要命。」

「才没有呢！你可真傻。」

「当然有。他成天围着你转，管你叫菲莉斯，一言一动都俨然你同他是音乐喜剧里的女主角和男主角，我还能怎么想？有一回我听见你们晚饭后唱二重唱。我推想出了最坏的结论。」

===Original===

“When was that? What were you doing there?”

“It was shortly after Ukridge had got on your father’s nerves, and nipped our acquaintance in the bud. I used to come every night to the hedge opposite your drawing-room window, and brood there by the hour.”

“Poor old boy!”

“Hoping to hear you sing. And when you did sing, and he joined in all flat, I used to swear. You’ll probably find most of the bark scorched off the tree I leaned against.”

“Poor old man! Still, it’s all over now, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===

「那是什么时候？你在那儿干什么？」

「就是厄克里奇惹毛你父亲、掐死咱们交情之后不久。我每天夜里都到你家客厅窗子对面的树篱那儿，一守就是几个钟头。」

「可怜的老伙计！」

「盼着听你唱歌。等你真唱起来，他跟着一块儿唱还净唱跑调，我就咒骂。我靠过的那棵树，树皮多半叫我烤焦了大半。」

「可怜的人！不过，现在全过去了，对不对？」

===Original===

“And when I was doing my very best to show off before you at tennis, you went away just as I got into form.”

“I’m very sorry, but I couldn’t know, could I? I thought you always played like that.”

“I know. I knew you would. It nearly turned my hair white. I didn’t see how a girl could ever care for a man who was so bad at tennis.”

“One doesn’t love a man because he’s good at tennis.”

“What *does* a girl see to love in a man?” I inquired abruptly; and paused on the verge of a great discovery.

===Chinese===

「还有，我在网球场上正拿出看家本事在你面前显摆呢，你偏偏在我刚进入状态的时候走了。」

「真对不起，可我哪能知道呢？我还以为你一向就那水平。」

「我知道。我就知道你会这么说。差点儿把我的头发愁白。我不明白，一个网球打得这么糟的男人，姑娘家怎么会爱上他。」

「爱一个男人，又不是因为他网球打得好。」

「那姑娘家到底看上男人哪一点呢？」我冷不丁发问；随即在一项重大发现的边缘站住了脚。

===Original===

“Oh, I don’t know,” she replied, most unsatisfactorily.

And I could draw no views from her.

“But about father,” said she. “What *are* we to do?”

“He objects to me.”

“He’s perfectly furious with you.”

===Chinese===

「哦，我也说不上来，」她答道，答得实在不能令人满意。

我再也没能从她嘴里套出什么高见来。

「可是父亲的事，」她说，「我们到底怎么办？」

「他看我不顺眼。」

「他正跟你大发雷霆呢。」

===Original===

“Blow, blow,” I said, “thou winter wind. Thou art not so unkind﻿—”

“He’ll never forgive you.”

“﻿—As man’s ingratitude. I saved his life. At the risk of my own. Why, I believe I’ve got a legal claim on him. Who ever heard of a man having his life saved, and not being delighted when his preserver wanted to marry his daughter? Your father is striking at the very root of the short-story writer’s little earnings. He mustn’t be allowed to do it.”

“Jerry!”

I started.

===Chinese===

「吹吧，吹吧，你这冬天的风。你的无情还比不上——」

「他永远不会原谅你。」

「——男人的忘恩负义更甚。我救过他的命。还是冒着自家性命危险的。嘿，我相信我对他享有法定债权。谁听说过一个人的命被救了，救命的恩人想娶他女儿，他反倒不乐意的？你父亲这是在掘短篇小说作者那点微薄收入的命根子。可不能由着他这么干。」

「杰里！」

我一激灵。

===Original===

“Again!” I said.

“What?”

“Say it again. Do, please. Now.”

“Very well. Jerry!”

“It was the first time you had called me by my Christian name. I don’t suppose you’ve the remotest notion how splendid it sounds when you say it. There is something poetical, almost holy, about it.”

===Chinese===

「再来一遍！」我说。

「什么？」

「再叫一遍。求你了。现在就叫。」

「好吧。杰里！」

「这是你头一回叫我的教名。你多半全然想象不到，你叫出来的时候它有多好听。那里头有点诗意，差不多算得上神圣。」

===Original===

“Jerry, please!”

“Say on.”

“Do be sensible. Don’t you see how serious this is? We must think how we can make father consent.”

“All right,” I said. “We’ll tackle the point. I’m sorry to be frivolous, but I’m so happy I can’t keep it all in. I’ve got you, and I can’t think of anything else.”

“Try.”

===Chinese===

「杰里，拜托！」

「请讲。」

「讲点道理好不好。你难道看不出这事有多紧要？咱们得想法子让父亲点头。」

「好，」我说。「咱们来攻克这个要点。抱歉我有点轻浮，可我幸福得兜不住了。有了你，别的一概想不动。」

「试着想想。」

===Original===

“I’ll pull myself together.﻿ ﻿… Now, say on once more.”

“We can’t marry without his consent.”

“Why not?” I said, not having a marked respect for the professor’s whims. “Gretna Green is out of date, but there are registrars.”

“I hate the very idea of a registrar,” she said with decision. “Besides﻿—”

“Well?”

===Chinese===

「我这就收心。……好，接着说。」

「没有他点头，咱们结不成婚。」

「怎么不能？」我说，我对教授的任性素来不怎么敬重。「格雷特纳格林是过时了，可还有登记处嘛。」

「我一想到登记处就讨厌，」她斩钉截铁地说。「再说——」

「再说什么？」

===Original===

“Poor father would never get over it. We’ve always been such friends. If I married against his wishes, he would﻿—oh, you know. Not let me near him again, and not write to me. And he would hate it all the time he was doing it. He would be bored to death without me.”

“Who wouldn’t?” I said.

“Because, you see, Norah has never been quite the same. She has spent such a lot of her time on visits to people, that she and father don’t understand each other so well as he and I do. She would try and be nice to him, but she wouldn’t know him as I do. And, besides, she will be with him such a little, now she’s going to be married.”

“But, look here,” I said, “this is absurd. You say your father would never see you again, and so on, if you married me. Why? It’s nonsense. It isn’t as if I were a sort of social outcast. We were the best of friends till that man Hawk gave me away like that.”

“I know. But he’s very obstinate about some things. You see, he thinks the whole thing has made him look ridiculous, and it will take him a long time to forgive you for that.”

===Chinese===

「可怜的父亲会缓不过来的。我们一向是这么好的朋友。我若违背他的心意嫁了人，他就会——唉，你知道的。不许我再靠近他，也不再给我写信。而他做这些的时候，心里会一直恨着这事。没有我，他会闷死的。」

「谁不会呢？」我说。

「因为，你瞧，诺拉从来不一样。她那么多时间都在外头做客，她同比不上我懂他。她也会努力对他好，可她不像我那样了解他。何况她就要出嫁了，往后陪他的日子更少。」

「可是，听我说，」我道，「这就荒唐了。你说你父亲从此再见不得你，等等等等，要是你嫁了我。为什么？没道理。我又不是什么社会弃儿。在那家伙霍克把我这么一出卖之前，咱们本来是至交。」

「我知道。可他在有些事情上固执得很。你瞧，他认为这整件事叫他显得可笑，单为这一条，他也要很久才肯饶你。」

===Original===

I realised the truth of this. One can pardon any injury to oneself, unless it hurts one’s vanity. Moreover, even in a genuine case of rescue, the rescued man must always feel a little aggrieved with his rescuer, when he thinks the matter over in cold blood. He must regard him unconsciously as the super regards the actor-manager, indebted to him for the means of supporting existence, but grudging him the limelight and the centre of the stage and the applause. Besides, everyone instinctively dislikes being under an obligation which they can never wholly repay. And when a man discovers that he has experienced all these mixed sensations for nothing, as the professor had done, his wrath is likely to be no slight thing.

Taking everything into consideration, I could not but feel that it would require more than a little persuasion to make the professor bestow his blessing with that genial warmth which we like to see in our fathers-in-law elect.

“You don’t think,” I said, “that time, the Great Healer, and so on﻿—? He won’t feel kindlier disposed towards me﻿—say in a month’s time?”

“Of course he *might*,” said Phyllis; but she spoke doubtfully.

“He strikes me from what I have seen of him as a man of moods. I might do something one of these days which would completely alter his views. We will hope for the best.”

===Chinese===

我明白这话在理。对自身的伤害，人都可以宽恕，唯独伤了虚荣的不行。再者，即便是货真价实的搭救，被救的人冷静下来细想时，对救命恩人也总要存一点芥蒂。他必不自觉地把他看作龙套之看待主演兼经理——多亏了他才有了糊口的营生，却容不得他独占脚灯光圈、舞台中央和满堂彩。何况，人人天生厌恶欠下一笔永远还不清的人情。而当一个人发现自己这一切五味杂陈全属白尝——教授正是如此——他的怒火就小不了。

把方方面面掂量一遍，我不免觉得，要教授以我们乐意在未来岳丈身上看到的那份和煦热忱来赐福，光凭三言两语的劝说是远远不够的。

「你不想想，」我说，「时间这位伟大的良医，等等等等——？过些日子——比方一个月后——他对我的气会不会消一点？」

「当然*有可能*，」菲莉斯说；可她语气存疑。

「照我平素的观察，他给我的印象是个喜怒无常的人。说不定哪天我做出件什么事，能让他的看法彻底改观。咱们往好里盼吧。」

===Original===

“About telling father﻿—?”

“Need we, do you think?” I said.

“Yes, we must. I couldn’t bear to think that I was keeping it from him. I don’t think I’ve ever kept anything from him in my life. Nothing bad, I mean.”

“You count this among your darker crimes, then?”

“I was looking at it from father’s point of view. He will be awfully angry. I don’t know how I shall begin telling him.”

===Chinese===

「要不要告诉父亲——？」

「依你看，非告诉不可么？」我说。

「非告诉不可。一想到瞒着他，我就受不了。我这辈子还从没瞒过他什么。我是说，没瞒过什么坏事。」

「那么你把这件事归入你的重罪档案了？」

「我是从父亲的角度看。他会大发雷霆的。我都不知道该怎么对他开这个口。」

===Original===

“Good heavens!” I cried, “you surely don’t think I’m going to let you do that! Keep safely out of the way while you tell him! Not much. I’m coming back with you now, and we’ll break the bad news together.”

“No, not tonight. He may be tired and rather cross. We had better wait till tomorrow. You might speak to him in the morning.”

“Where shall I find him?”

“He is certain to go to the beach before breakfast for a swim.”

“Good. I’ll be there.”

---

===Chinese===

「天哪！」我叫道，「你总不至于以为我会让你一个人去开口吧！让你去讲、我躲得远远的！想得美。我这就跟你回去，咱们一块儿把这条坏消息捅给他。」

「不行，今儿晚上不行。他兴许累了，脾气正坏。不如等明天。你明早去同他讲。」

「上哪儿找他？」

「早饭前他准去海里游泳。」

「好。我到时恭候。」

---

===Original===

“Ukridge,” I said, when I got back, “I want your advice.”

It stirred him like a trumpet blast. I suppose, when a man is in the habit of giving unsolicited counsel to everyone he meets, it is as invigorating as an electric shock to him to be asked for it spontaneously.

“Bang it out, laddie!” he replied cordially. “I’m with you. Here, come along into the garden, and state your case.”

This suited me. It is always easier to talk intimately in the dark, and I did not wish to be interrupted by the sudden entrance of the Hired Man or Mrs. Beale, of which there was always a danger indoors. We walked down to the paddock. Ukridge lit a cigar.

“Ukridge,” I said, “I’m engaged!”

===Chinese===

「厄克里奇，」我回到农庄说，「我要向你讨个主意。」

这话像一声号角，震得他精神抖擞。我想，一个惯于逢人便主动进言的人，冷不丁被人主动讨教，那份提神赛过电击。

「竹筒倒豆子吧，小老弟！」他热诚地答道。「我挺你。来，跟我到园子里去，陈述案情。」

这正合我意。摸黑说体己话总要容易些，我也不想被受雇的忠仆或比尔太太的突然进门打断——屋里始终有这个风险。我们踱到围场。厄克里奇点上一支雪茄。

「厄克里奇，」我说，「我订婚了！」

===Original===

“What!” A huge hand whistled through the darkness and smote me heavily between the shoulder-blades. “By Jove, old boy, I wish you luck. ’Pon my Sam I do! Best thing in the world for you. Bachelors are mere excrescences. Never knew what happiness was till I married. When’s the wedding to be?”

“That’s where I want your advice. What you might call a difficulty has arisen about the wedding. It’s like this. I’m engaged to Phyllis Derrick.”

“Derrick? Derrick?”

“You can’t have forgotten her! Good Lord, what eyes some men have! Why, if I’d only seen her once, I should have remembered her all my life.”

“I know, now. Rather a pretty girl, with blue eyes.”

===Chinese===

「什么！」一只大手带着风声穿过黑暗，重重拍在我两肩胛骨之间。「老天，老弟，祝你走运。我以我的萨姆起誓，真心实意！这是天底下对你最好的事。光棍汉不过是人身上的赘疣。没结婚之前，我从不知道幸福为何物。婚礼定在几时？」

「我讨主意的就是这个。婚事上出了点你或可称之为难题的情况。是这样的。我订婚的对象是菲莉斯·德里克。」

「德里克？德里克？」

「你总不至于把她忘了吧！老天，有些人的眼睛！嗐，哪怕我只见过她一面，我也会记她一辈子。」

「哦，想起来了。怪俊的一个姑娘，蓝眼睛。」

===Original===

I stared at him blankly. It was not much good, as he could not see my face, but it relieved me. “Rather a pretty girl!” What a description!

“Of course, yes,” continued Ukridge. “She came to dinner here one night with her father, that fat little buffer.”

“As you were careful to call him to his face at the time, confound you! It was that that started all the trouble.”

“Trouble? What trouble?”

“Why, her father﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

我木然瞪着他。这也是白瞪，反正他瞧不见我的脸，可多少让我缓了口气。「怪俊的一个姑娘」！好一段人物描写！

「对了，对了，」厄克里奇接着说。「有一晚她跟她父亲来这儿吃过饭，就是那个矮胖子。」

「亏你当时还当着他的面就这么称呼他，你这混账！祸根就是那时候种下的。」

「祸根？什么祸根？」

「嗐，她父亲……」

===Original===

“By Jove, I remember now! So worried lately, old boy, that my memory’s gone groggy. Of course! Her father fell into the sea, and you fished him out. Why, damme, it’s like the stories you read.”

“It’s also very like the stories I used to write. But they had one point about them which this story hasn’t. They invariably ended happily, with the father joining the hero’s and heroine’s hands and giving his blessing. Unfortunately, in the present case, that doesn’t seem likely to happen.”

“The old man won’t give his consent?”

“I’m afraid not. I haven’t asked him yet, but the chances are against it.”

“But why? What’s the matter with you? You’re an excellent chap, sound in wind and limb, and didn’t you once tell me that, if you married, you came into a pretty sizeable bit of money?”

===Chinese===

「老天，我现在想起来了！近来操心的事儿多，老伙计，我的记性晃悠了。当然！她父亲掉进海里，你把他捞了上来。嗐，我说，这跟你读的小说一个样。」

「也很像我从前写的小说。可我那些小说有一样好处，这故事偏偏没有。它们千篇一律以大团圆收场：父亲牵着男女主人公的手放到一处，赐下祝福。不幸，眼下这桩，看来不大会这么收场。」

「老头子不肯点头？」

「怕是不肯。我还没开口问，可形势对他不利。」

「可为什么？你有什么毛病？你是条顶好的汉子，筋骨齐全；再说你不是告诉过我，你一结婚就能继承挺可观的一笔钱？」

===Original===

“Yes, I do. That part of it is all right.”

Ukridge’s voice betrayed perplexity.

“I don’t understand this thing, old horse,” he said. “I should have thought the old boy would have been all over you. Why, damme, I never heard of anything like it. You saved his life! You fished him out of the water.”

“After chucking him in. That’s the trouble.”

“You chucked him in?”

===Chinese===

「对，现在还作数。这一层不成问题。」

厄克里奇的嗓音透出了困惑。

「这事我就不懂了，老马，」他说。「照理那老头子该缠住你不放才对。嗐，我说，这种事我闻所未闻。你救了他的命！你把他从水里捞了出来。」

「捞之前先把他掀了进去。麻烦就麻烦在这儿。」

「你把他掀了进去？」

===Original===

“By proxy.”

I explained. Ukridge, I regret to say, laughed in a way that must have been heard miles away in distant villages in Devonshire.

“You devil!” he bellowed. “ ’Pon my Sam, old horse, to look at you one would never have thought you’d have had it in you.”

“I can’t help looking respectable.”

“What are you going to do about it?”

===Chinese===

「借刀杀人的掀法。」

我解释了一遍。遗憾的是，厄克里奇那阵大笑，德文郡远处的村子想必要听得见。

「你这恶魔！」他吼道。「以我的萨姆起誓，老马，瞧你这副尊容，谁想得到你肚里还有这一手。」

「我这张脸长得太正派，没法子。」

「你打算怎么办？」

===Original===

“That’s where I wanted your advice. You’re a man of resource. What would you do in my place?”

Ukridge tapped me impressively on the shoulder.

“Laddie,” he said, “there’s one thing that’ll carry you through any mess.”

“And that is﻿—?”

“Cheek, my boy, cheek. Gall. Nerve. Why, take my case. I never told you how I came to marry, did I? I thought not. Well, it was this way. It’ll do you a bit of good, perhaps, to hear the story, for, mark you, blessings weren’t going cheap in my case either. You know Millie’s Aunt Elizabeth, the female who wrote that letter? Well, when I tell you that she was Millie’s nearest relative and that it was her consent I had to snaffle, you’ll see that I was faced with a bit of a problem.”

===Chinese===

「这不就是我来讨主意的么。你足智多谋。换了我，你怎么办？」

厄克里奇郑重其事地拍拍我的肩。

「小老弟，」他说，「有一样东西，保你闯过任何烂摊子。」

「那是什么——？」

「脸皮，我的老弟，脸皮。胆气。横劲。瞧我自己的例子。我当初怎么结婚的，从没跟你讲过吧？我就知道没有。是这么回事。听听这个故事对你或许不无好处，因为，你听着，我当年也不是祝福满天飞的行情。你认得米莉的伊丽莎白姨妈吧，写那封信的那个女人？喏，我告诉你：她是米莉至亲的亲眷，我得弄到手的偏偏是她的点头，你瞧瞧，我面对的是何等一道难题。」

===Original===

“Let’s have it,” I said.

“Well, the first time I ever saw Millie was in a first-class carriage on the underground. I’d got a third-class ticket, by the way. The carriage was full, and I got up and gave her my seat, and, as I hung suspended over her by a strap, damme, I fell in love with her then and there. You’ve no conception, laddie, how indescribably ripping she looked, in a sort of blue dress with a bit of red in it and a hat with thingummies. Well, we both got out at South Kensington. By that time I was gasping for air and saw that the thing wanted looking into. I’d never had much time to bother about women, but I realised that this must not be missed. I was in love, old horse. It comes over you quite suddenly, like a tidal wave﻿ ﻿…”

“I know! I know! Good Heavens, you can’t tell me anything about that.”

“Well, I followed her. She went to a house in Thurloe Square. I waited outside and thought it over. I had got to get into that shanty and make her acquaintance, if they threw me out on my ear. So I rang the bell. ‘Is Lady Lichenhall at home?’ I asked. You spot the devilish cunning of the ruse, what? My asking for a female with a title was to make ’em think I was one of the Upper Ten.”

“How were you dressed?” I could not help asking.

===Chinese===

「细细道来。」我说。

「是这样，我头一回见到米莉，是在地铁的头等车厢里。顺带一提，我手里捏的是张三等票。车厢满了，我起身给她让座，随后吊着皮带来回晃在她上头，嗐，我当场就爱上了她。小老弟，你无法想象她那模样有多说不出的俏丽：一身蓝裙子，里头缀着点红，帽子上有那叫什么来着的小玩意儿。后来我们俩都在南肯辛顿下的车。到那时我已连气都喘不匀，看得出这事必须认真研究。我从前从不多余在女人身上费工夫，可我明白这一趟错过不得。我恋爱了，老马。那玩意儿来势突兀，像一场海啸……」

「我知道！我知道！老天，这种事你不必对我上课。」

「于是我就跟上了她。她进了瑟洛广场上的一所宅子。我在外头守着，盘算开来。我非进那座宝庐不可，非结识她不可——哪怕人家揪着耳朵把我扔出来。于是我去按门铃。『请问利肯霍尔夫人在府上么？』我问。你品出这一计的毒辣刁钻了么，嗯？我张口求见一位有头衔的夫人，好叫他们当我是上流社会里的人物。」

「那你那身打扮呢？」我忍不住问。

===Original===

“Oh, it was one of my frock-coat days. I’d been to see a man about tutoring his son, and by a merciful dispensation of Providence there was a fellow living in the same boardinghouse with me who was about my build and had a frock-coat, and he had lent it to me. At least, he hadn’t exactly lent it to me, but I knew where he kept it and he was out at the time. There was nothing the matter with my appearance. Quite the young duke, I assure you, laddie, down to the last button. ‘Is Lady Lichenhall at home?’ I asked. ‘No,’ said the maid, ‘nobody of that name here. This is Lady Lakenheath’s house.’ So, you see, I had a bit of luck at the start, because the names were a bit alike. Well, I got the maid to show me in somehow, and, once in you can bet I talked for all I was worth. Kept up a flow of conversation about being misdirected and coming to the wrong house. Went away, and called a few days later. Gradually wormed my way in. Called regularly. Spied on their movements, met ’em at every theatre they went to, and bowed, and finally got away with Millie before her aunt knew what was happening or who I was or what I was doing or anything.”

“And what’s the moral?”

“Why, go in like a mighty, rushing wind! Bustle ’em! Don’t give ’em a moment’s rest or time to think or anything. Why, if I’d given Millie’s Aunt Elizabeth time to think, where should we have been? Not at Combe Regis together, I’ll bet. You heard that letter, and know what she thinks of me now, on reflection. If I’d gone slow and played a timid waiting-game, she’d have thought that before I married Millie, instead of afterwards. I give you my honest word, laddie, that there was a time, towards the middle of our acquaintance﻿—after she had stopped mixing me up with the man who came to wind the clocks﻿—when that woman ate out of my hand! Twice﻿—on two separate occasions﻿—she actually asked my advice about feeding her toy Pomeranian! Well, that shows you! Bustle ’em, laddie! Bustle ’em!”

“Ukridge,” I said, “you inspire me. You would inspire a caterpillar. I will go to the professor﻿—I was going anyhow, but now I shall go aggressively. I will prise a father’s blessing out of him, if I have to do it with a crowbar.”

“That’s the way to talk, old horse. Don’t beat about the bush. Tell him exactly what you want and stand no nonsense. If you don’t see what you want in the window, ask for it. Where did you think of tackling him?”

===Chinese===

「哦，那天正逢我的长礼服日。我原是去见一位先生、商量给他儿子当家庭教师的事，而托上天仁慈的安排，同我寄宿在一处的恰有位同我身量相仿、有件长礼服的老兄，他就把它借给我了。至少，算不上正儿八经借，可我知道他搁在哪儿，而他又恰好外出。我那副仪表无一处可挑剔。十足一位年轻公爵，小老弟，我向你担保，连最后一颗纽扣都在。『请问利肯霍尔夫人在府上么？』我问。『不在，』女仆说，『本宅没有这位太太。这里是利肯霍尔夫人的家。』你瞧，我头一步就走了运，两个名字有几分相像。反正我不记得怎么着就叫女仆引了进去，而一旦进了门，你放心，我可着劲儿地神侃。滔滔不绝地解释如何被指错了路、如何走错了门。告辞了，过几天再来拜访。渐渐钻营进去。按期登门。侦察她们的行踪，她们上哪家戏院我上哪家，鞠躬如仪，终于赶在米莉她姨妈弄明白出了什么事、我是什么人、我想干什么，以及其他一切之前，把米莉弄到了手。」

「那么，教训何在？」

「嗐，就像一股大能的狂风刮进去！猛攻他们！不给他们一刻喘息、一刻思索的工夫，什么都不给。嗐，想当年我要是给米莉的伊丽莎白姨妈留出思索的工夫，咱们如今会在哪儿？我敢打赌，绝凑不齐在库姆里吉斯。那封信你听见了，如今她回头一想对我是何评价，你也知道了。当年我若稳扎稳打、来一手怯生生的观望，这些话她早在娶米莉之前就想好了，何须等到婚后。我给你句实在话，小老弟：有那么一阵子——在我们相识的中段，也就是她不再把我同上门给钟上弦的那人弄混之后——那位太太对我百依百顺！有两回——正经两回——她居然向我讨教怎么喂她那只玩具博美犬！喏，这就见分晓了！猛攻他们，小老弟！猛攻！」

「厄克里奇，」我说，「你叫我士气大振。你连一条毛毛虫都能鼓动起来。我去见教授——本来也是要去的，可现在我要杀气腾腾地去。我要从他身上撬出一位父亲的祝福，必要的时候动撬棍。」

「这就叫会说话，老马。别拐弯抹角。你要什么，原原本本告诉他，一点废话别容他讲。橱窗里看不着你想要的，就开口讨。你打算在哪儿截他？」

===Original===

“Phyllis tells me that he always goes for a swim before breakfast. I thought of going down tomorrow and waylaying him.”

“You couldn’t do better. By Jove!” said Ukridge suddenly. “I’ll tell you what I’ll do, laddie. I wouldn’t do it for everybody, but I look on you as a favourite son. I’ll come with you, and help break the ice.”

“What!”

“Don’t you be under any delusion, old horse,” said Ukridge paternally. “You haven’t got an easy job in front of you, and what you’ll need more than anything else, when you really get down to brass-tacks, is a wise, kindly man of the world at your elbow, to whoop you on when your nerve fails you and generally stand in your corner and see that you get a fair show.”

“But it’s rather an intimate business﻿ ﻿…”

“Never mind! Take my tip and have me at your side. I can say things about you that you would be too modest to say for yourself. I can plead your cause, laddie. I can point out in detail all that the old boy will be missing if he gives you the miss-in-baulk. Well, that’s settled, then. About eight tomorrow morning, what? I’ll be there, my boy. A swim will do me good.”

===Chinese===

「菲莉斯说，他早饭前总要下海游一趟。我想明天一早下山，埋伏截他。」

「再好没有了。老天！」厄克里奇蓦地说。「我告诉你怎么办，小老弟。换个人我才不费这个神，可我拿你当亲儿子看。我陪你去，帮你破冰。」

「什么！」

「你可别犯糊涂，老马，」厄克里奇以慈父的口吻说。「你前头的差事不轻松，等真刀真枪见了分晓，你最缺的就是肘边有一位世事练达的仁厚长者，在你胆气涣散时为你擂鼓，总之替你守着场子角落，保你得到公平的机会。」

「可这事毕竟颇为私密……」

「不用管！听我一句，把我带在你身边。你不好意思自己夸的，我能替你夸。我能替你陈情，小老弟。那老头子若要给你来一记『故意脱杆』，我能逐条给他点明，他将因此错过的一切好处。好，就这么定了。明早八点前后，如何？我准到，我的老弟。游一回泳对我也有益处。」

## III Waterloo Station, Some Fellow-Travellers, and a Girl with Brown Hair / 三 滑铁卢车站、几位同路人，还有一位棕发姑娘

===Original===
The austerity of Waterloo Station was lightened on the following morning at ten minutes to eleven, when I arrived to catch the train to Combe Regis, by several gleams of sunshine and a great deal of bustle and activity on the various platforms. A porter took my suitcase and golf-clubs, and arranged an assignation on Number 6 platform. I bought my ticket, and made my way to the bookstall, where, in the interests of trade, I inquired in a loud and penetrating voice if they had got Jeremy Garnet’s *Manœuvres of Arthur*. Being informed that they had not, I clicked my tongue reproachfully, advised them to order in a supply, as the demand was likely to be large, and spent a couple of shillings on a magazine and some weekly papers. Then, with ten minutes to spare, I went off in search of Ukridge.

I found him on platform six. The eleven-twenty was already alongside, and presently I observed my porter cleaving a path towards me with the suitcase and golf-bag.

“Here you are!” shouted Ukridge vigorously. “Good for you. Thought you were going to miss it.”

I shook hands with the smiling Mrs. Ukridge.

“I’ve got a carriage and collared two corner seats. Millie goes down in another. She doesn’t like the smell of smoke when she’s travelling. Hope we get the carriage to ourselves. Devil of a lot of people here this morning. Still, the more people there are in the world, the more eggs we shall sell. I can see with half an eye that all these blighters are confirmed egg-eaters. Get in, sonnie. I’ll just see the missis into her carriage, and come back to you.”

I entered the compartment, and stood at the door, looking out in the faint hope of thwarting an invasion of fellow-travellers. Then I withdrew my head suddenly and sat down. An elderly gentleman, accompanied by a pretty girl, was coming towards me. It was not this type of fellow traveller whom I had hoped to keep out. I had noticed the girl at the booking office. She had waited by the side of the queue while the elderly gentleman struggled gamely for the tickets, and I had had plenty of opportunity of observing her appearance. I had debated with myself whether her hair should rightly be described as brown or golden. I had finally decided on brown. Once only had I met her eyes, and then only for an instant. They might be blue. They might be grey. I could not be certain. Life is full of these problems.

===Chinese===
次日上午十点五十分，我赶往库姆里吉斯的列车，来到滑铁卢车站；车站一贯的肃穆，已被几缕阳光和各站台上大量的忙碌与活跃冲淡了几分。一位搬运工接过我的手提箱和高尔夫球杆，同我约好在 6 号站台碰头。我买了车票，踱到书报摊前，出于对行业的关怀，用洪亮穿透的嗓音询问，他们可有杰里米·加尼特的《阿瑟的谋略》出售。得到否定的答复后，我啧啧责备了两声，劝他们赶紧进货，因为需求想必不小，随即花两先令买了本杂志和几份周报。然后，还余十分钟，我便去寻厄克里奇。

我在 6 号站台找到他。十一点二十分的列车已经进站停靠，不多时，我瞧见我那位搬运工拎着手提箱和高尔夫球袋，在人丛中劈开一条路朝我而来。

“你可来了！”厄克里奇中气十足地吼道。“好样儿的。还当你赶不上了呢。”

我同笑吟吟的厄克里奇太太握了手。

“我占下一节车厢，抢了两个角座。米莉坐另一节。她出门不爱闻烟味。但愿这车厢能由咱们独享。今儿早上这人可真不少。不过话又说回来，世上人越多，咱们的鸡蛋越好卖。我一眼就看出来，这帮家伙全是铁杆的吃蛋群众。上车吧，小子。我把太太安顿进她的车厢，就回来陪你。”

我走进车厢，立在门口探头外望，怀着一丝渺茫的希望，想挫败同路人的入侵。随后我猛地缩回头，坐下了。一位老绅士，由一位漂亮姑娘陪伴着，正朝我走来。我本想拒之门外的，可并非这一类型的同路人。在售票处我就注意到这位姑娘了。老绅士奋勇力争车票时，她在一旁的队伍边等着，我得以把她端详了个够。她那头发，究竟该算棕色还是金色，我在心里反复斟酌，最后裁定为棕色。我们的目光只交会过一回，且只一瞬。那双眼睛也许是蓝的，也许是灰的。我没法确定。人生总是充满这类难题。

===Original===
“This seems to be tolerably empty, my dear Phyllis,” said the elderly gentleman, coming to the door of the compartment and looking in. “You’re sure you don’t object to a smoking-carriage?”

“Oh no, father. Not a bit.”

“Then I think﻿ ﻿…” said the elderly gentleman, getting in.

The inflection of his voice suggested the Irishman. It was not a brogue. There were no strange words. But the general effect was Irish.

“That’s good,” he said, settling himself and pulling out a cigar case.

The bustle of the platform had increased momentarily, until now, when, from the snorting of the engine, it seemed likely that the train might start at any minute, the crowd’s excitement was extreme. Shrill cries echoed down the platform. Lost sheep, singly and in companies, rushed to and fro, peering eagerly into carriages in search of seats. Piercing voices ordered unknown “Tommies” and “Ernies” to “keep by aunty, now.” Just as Ukridge returned, that *sauve qui peut* of the railway crowd, the dreaded “Get in anywhere” began to be heard, and the next moment an avalanche of warm humanity poured into the carriage.

===Chinese===
“这节车厢看来相当空，亲爱的菲莉斯，”老绅士走到车厢门口，朝里望了望，说道。“你确实不介意吸烟车厢吧？”

“哦不，爸爸。一点也不。”

“那么我想……”老绅士说着，上了车。

他话音的抑扬透着爱尔兰人。倒不是土腔，也没有什么怪词。可整体的效果就是爱尔兰味儿。

“这就好。”他说着安顿下来，掏出一只雪茄烟盒。

站台上的喧闹一刻比一刻高涨；到这时，从机车喷鼻息的动静听来，列车随时可能开动，人群的亢奋已达极点。尖利的喊声沿站台回荡。迷途的羔羊们，三三两两，来回狂奔，急切地探头张望各节车厢，寻找座位。刺耳的嗓音此起彼伏，喝令素不相识的“汤米”们和“厄尼”们“这会儿可要紧跟阿姨”。厄克里奇刚回来，铁路人群那句人人闻风丧胆的“各自逃命”——*sauve qui peut*（各顾各逃）——便已响起；转瞬间，一大片热气腾腾的人肉雪崩灌进了车厢。

===Original===
The newcomers consisted of a middle-aged lady, addressed as Aunty, very stout and clad in a grey alpaca dress, skintight; a youth called Albert, not, it was to appear, a sunny child; a niece of some twenty years, stolid and seemingly without interest in life, and one or two other camp-followers and retainers.

Ukridge slipped into his corner, adroitly foiling Albert, who had made a dive in that direction. Albert regarded him fixedly and reproachfully for a space, then sank into the seat beside me and began to chew something that smelt of aniseed.

Aunty, meanwhile, was distributing her substantial weight evenly between the feet of the Irish gentleman and those of his daughter, as she leaned out of the window to converse with a lady friend in a straw hat and hair curlers, accompanied by three dirty and frivolous boys. It was, she stated, lucky that she had caught the train. I could not agree with her. The girl with the brown hair and the eyes that were either blue or grey was bearing the infliction, I noticed, with angelic calm. She even smiled. This was when the train suddenly moved off with a jerk, and Aunty, staggering back, sat down on the bag of food which Albert had placed on the seat beside him.

“Clumsy!” observed Albert tersely.

“*Albert*, you mustn’t speak to Aunty so!”

“Wodyer want to sit on my bag for then?” said Albert disagreeably.

===Chinese===
新来者包括：一位中年太太，人称“阿姨”，身量极丰，裹一条紧绷绷的灰色羊驼呢裙；一个叫阿尔伯特的少年——日后可见，绝非阳光儿童；一位二十来岁的外甥女，木木呆呆，仿佛对人生毫无兴趣；另外还有一两名随军贩子和仆从之流。

厄克里奇溜进他的角落，灵巧地挫败了朝那个方向鱼跃而来的阿尔伯特。阿尔伯特直勾勾地、谴责地盯了他一阵，然后一屁股陷进我旁边的座位，嚼起一样闻起来是茴芹味的东西。

与此同时，阿姨正把她那可观的体重均匀地分摊到爱尔兰绅士和他女儿的双脚上，一面探身车窗外，同一位头戴草帽、发上卷着卷发筒的女友交谈——女友身边还跟着三个脏兮兮、嬉皮笑脸的男孩。她宣称，自己赶上了这班车，实在运气。我不敢苟同。那位棕发、眼睛或蓝或灰的姑娘，我注意到，正以天使般的平静承受着这场磨难。她甚至笑了。那是列车猛一颠簸突然开动、阿姨踉跄倒退、一屁股坐在阿尔伯特放在旁边座位上的食物袋上的时候。

“笨！”阿尔伯特言简意赅地评道。

“*阿尔伯特*，不许这样跟阿姨说话！”

“那你干吗坐俺的袋子？”阿尔伯特不耐烦地说。

===Original===
They argued the point. Argument in no wise interfered with Albert’s power of mastication. The odour of aniseed became more and more painful. Ukridge had lighted a cigar, and I understood why Mrs. Ukridge preferred to travel in another compartment, for

> “In his hand he bore the brand

 Which none but he might smoke.”

I looked across the carriage stealthily to see how the girl was enduring this combination of evils, and noticed that she had begun to read. And as she put the book down to look out of the window, I saw with a thrill that trickled like warm water down my spine that her book was *The Manœuvres of Arthur*. I gasped. That a girl should look as pretty as that and at the same time have the rare intelligence to read Me﻿ ﻿… well, it seemed an almost superhuman combination of the excellencies. And more devoutly than ever I cursed in my heart these intrusive outsiders who had charged in at the last moment and destroyed forever my chance of making this wonderful girl’s acquaintance. But for them, we might have become intimate in the first half hour. As it was, what were we? Ships that pass in the night! She would get out at some beastly wayside station, and vanish from my life without my ever having even spoken to her.

Aunty, meanwhile, having retired badly worsted from her encounter with Albert, who showed a skill in logomachy that marked him out as a future Labour member, was consoling herself with meat sandwiches. The niece was demolishing sausage rolls. The atmosphere of the carriage was charged with a blend of odours, topping all Ukridge’s cigar, now in full blast.

The train raced on towards the sea. It was a warm day, and a torpid peace began to settle down upon the carriage. Ukridge had thrown away the stump of his cigar, and was now leaning back with his mouth open and his eyes shut. Aunty, still clutching a much-bitten section of a beef sandwich, was breathing heavily and swaying from side to side. Albert and the niece were dozing, Albert’s jaws working automatically, even in sleep.

===Chinese===
他们就这一点争论起来。争论丝毫不妨碍阿尔伯特的咀嚼功能。茴芹的气味越来越折磨人。厄克里奇点上了一支雪茄，我顿时明白了厄克里奇太太为何宁可坐另一节车厢，因为——

> “他手中擎着的那支烟，

 唯有他本人才配抽。”

我偷偷横望车厢那头，看那姑娘如何消受这重重灾难的合击，却见她已开始读书。而当她放下书、望向窗外时，我一阵激灵，像一股暖水流下脊背——她那本书，竟是《阿瑟的谋略》。我倒抽一口气。一个姑娘，既能生得那般漂亮，同时又具备读我……读我作品的这份罕见慧根，这似乎几乎是种种美德的超人合体。我在心里比先前更虔诚地诅咒起这帮闯入的外人：他们在最后关头一拥而入，永远毁了我结识这位奇妙姑娘的机会。若不是他们，头半个钟头里我们本可以熟络起来。而如今，我们算什么？夜色里交臂而过的船！她会在某个可恶的小站下车，从我的生命里消失得无影无踪，而我连一句话都不曾同她讲过。

另一方面，阿姨在同阿尔伯特的交锋中一败涂地，退下阵来——后者显露的字战功夫，注定他将来是块工党议员的料——此刻正拿肉三明治聊以自慰。外甥女在消灭香肠酥卷。车厢的空气里充满种种气味的混合体，而压倒一切的，是厄克里奇那支已燃至全速的雪茄。

列车朝着大海疾驰。天气和暖，一种昏昏欲睡的安宁开始笼罩车厢。厄克里奇扔掉雪茄烟头，仰靠着，张着嘴，闭着眼。阿姨仍攥着咬得七零八落的一截牛肉三明治，呼吸沉重，左右摇晃。阿尔伯特和外甥女都在打盹，阿尔伯特连睡着了，两颚还在自动运转。

===Original===
“What’s your book, me dear?” asked the Irishman.

“*The Manœuvres of Arthur*, father. By Jeremy Garnet.”

I would not have believed without the evidence of my ears that my name could possibly have sounded so musical.

“Molly McEachern gave it to me when I left the Abbey. She keeps a shelf of books for her guests when they are going away. Books that she considers rubbish, and doesn’t want, you know.”

I hated Miss McEachern without further evidence.

“And what do you think of it?”

===Chinese===
“你看的什么书，俺乖囡？”爱尔兰人问道。

“《阿瑟的谋略》，爸爸。杰里米·加尼特著。”

若非亲耳听见，我决不相信，我的名字竟能发出这般乐音。

“我离开阿比庄园时，莫莉·麦凯克恩送我的。她专备了一架书，送给临行的客人。都是她认为是垃圾、自己不想留的书，你懂的。”

无需更多证据，我就恨上了麦凯克恩小姐。

“那你觉得这书怎么样？”

===Original===
“I like it,” said the girl decidedly. The carriage swam before my eyes. “I think it is very clever.”

What did it matter after that that the ass in charge of the Waterloo bookstall had never heard of *The Manœuvres of Arthur*, and that my publishers, whenever I slunk in to ask how it was selling, looked at me with a sort of grave, paternal pity and said that it had not really “begun to move?” Anybody can write one of those rotten popular novels which appeal to the unthinking public, but it takes a man of intellect and refinement and taste and all that sort of thing to turn out something that will be approved of by a girl like this.

“I wonder who Jeremy Garnet is,” she said. “I’ve never heard of him before. I imagine him rather an old young man, probably with an eyeglass, and conceited. And I should think he didn’t know many girls. At least if he thinks Pamela an ordinary sort of girl. She’s a cr-r-eature,” said Phyllis emphatically.

This was a blow to me. I had always looked on Pamela as a well-drawn character, and a very attractive, kittenish little thing at that. That scene between her and the curate in the conservatory﻿ ﻿… And when she talks to Arthur at the meet of the Blankshires﻿ ﻿… I was sorry she did not like Pamela. Somehow it lowered Pamela in my estimation.

“But I like Arthur,” said the girl.

This was better. A good chap, Arthur﻿—a very complete and thoughtful study of myself. If she liked Arthur, why, then it followed﻿ ﻿… but what was the use? I should never get a chance of speaking to her. We were divided by a great gulf of Aunties and Alberts and meat sandwiches.

===Chinese===
“我喜欢。”姑娘斩钉截铁地说。车厢在我眼前游动起来。“我觉得它非常聪明。”

这以后，滑铁卢书报摊那个蠢驴没听说过《阿瑟的谋略》，又有何妨？每当我溜进出版社问销路、他们带着一种庄重的、父亲般的怜悯望着我、说它还没“开始动起来”时，又有何妨？谁都能写一部那种讨好不动脑子的大众的破烂畅销小说；可要弄出一部能博得这样一位姑娘嘉许的作品，却须得是有智识、有教养、有品味云云的人物才行。

“不知这杰里米·加尼特是谁，”她说，“从前从没听说过。我想他是个未老先衰的年轻人，多半夹着单片眼镜，自命不凡。我猜他也不大认识姑娘家。至少，他若是把帕梅拉当成寻常姑娘，那就是了。她是个怪——物。”菲莉斯重重地说。

这对我是一记打击。我一向把帕梅拉看作塑造得很成功的人物，而且是那种非常迷人、小猫般娇俏的可人儿。她同副牧师在温室里的那一幕……还有她在布兰克夏猎队晨猎集合上同阿瑟的谈话……她不喜欢帕梅拉，我很难过。不知怎么，帕梅拉在我心目中的身价随之跌落了。

“不过我喜欢阿瑟。”姑娘说。

这就好多了。阿瑟是条好汉——一份以本人为底本的相当完备而体贴的研究。她既然喜欢阿瑟，那么，顺理成章……可说这些又有什么用？我永远得不到同她说话的机会了。横亘在我们之间的，是一道由阿姨们、阿尔伯特们和肉三明治组成的鸿沟。

===Original===
The train was beginning to slow down. Signs of returning animation began to be noticeable among the sleepers. Aunty’s eyes opened, stared vacantly round, closed, and reopened. The niece woke, and started instantly to attack a sausage roll. Albert and Ukridge slumbered on.

A whistle from the engine, and the train drew up at a station. Looking out, I saw that it was Yeovil. There was a general exodus. Aunty became instantly a thing of dash and electricity, collected parcels, shook Albert, replied to his thrusts with repartee, and finally headed a stampede out of the door.

The Irishman and his daughter also rose, and got out. I watched them leave stoically. It would have been too much to expect that they should be going any further.

“Where are we?” said Ukridge sleepily. “Yeovil? Not far now. I tell you what it is, old horse, I could do with a drink.”

With that remark he closed his eyes again, and returned to his slumbers. And, as he did so, my eye, roving discontentedly over the carriage, was caught by something lying in the far corner. It was *The Manœuvres of Arthur*. The girl had left it behind.

I suppose what follows shows the vanity that obsesses young authors. It did not even present itself to me as a tenable theory that the book might have been left behind on purpose, as being of no further use to the owner. It only occurred to me that, if I did not act swiftly, the poor girl would suffer a loss beside which the loss of a purse or vanity-case were trivial.

===Chinese===
列车开始减速。酣睡的人们中间，渐渐显出还阳的迹象。阿姨的眼睛睁开，茫然四顾，合上，又睁开。外甥女醒了，立刻向一根香肠酥卷发起攻击。阿尔伯特和厄克里奇鼾睡如故。

机车一声汽笛，列车停靠在一个车站。往外一看，是约维尔。车厢里发生了总撤退。阿姨霎时变成一团风驰电掣的物体，收拾包裹，摇醒阿尔伯特，以妙语回敬他的抢白，最后率领一场夺门而出的溃逃。

爱尔兰绅士和女儿也起身下了车。我坚忍地目送他们离去。指望他们再往前坐几站，未免太奢求了。

“咱们到哪儿了？”厄克里奇睡眼惺忪地说。“约维尔？不远了。我跟你说，老马，我真想来杯喝的。”

丢下这句话，他又阖上眼，回到梦里去了。也就在这时，我的目光不满地扫过车厢，被远远角落里躺着的一件东西勾住了。那是《阿瑟的谋略》。姑娘把它落下了。

我猜，接下来的举动暴露了盘踞在青年作家心头的那点虚荣。这本书也许是主人故意丢下的，于她再无用处——这个说法即便当作说得通的假设，都不曾进入我的脑海。我想到的只是：若不火速行动，可怜的姑娘将蒙受一场损失，相比之下，丢了钱夹或化妆盒都算小事。

===Original===
Five seconds later I was on the platform.

“Excuse me,” I said, “I think﻿ ﻿… ?”

“Oh, thank you so much,” said the girl.

I made my way back to the carriage, and lit my pipe in a glow of emotion.

“They are blue,” I said to my immortal soul. “A wonderful, deep, soft, heavenly blue, like the sea at noonday.”

===Chinese===
五秒钟后，我已站在站台上。

“打扰一下，”我说，“我想……？”

“哦，太感谢您了。”姑娘说。

我循原路回到车厢，在一阵情感的暖流中点起了烟斗。

“是蓝的。”我对我不朽的灵魂说。“一种美妙、深沉、温柔、天国似的蓝，像正午的大海。”
